# SGAI for Linear and Non-Linear Ads in MPEG-DASH

This document specifies Server-Guided Ad Insertion (SGAI) for MPEG-DASH,
covering linear advertising and non-linear advertising — overlay, squeezeback
and pause ads — under one contract. It defines how a Publisher declares an ad
opportunity in the manifest, how a Player resolves that opportunity against an
Ad Presentation Server, what the resolution document contains, and how the
Player selects, renders, times, tracks and bounds the ads it receives.

It is written for implementers of DASH players, of ad presentation and ad
decision services, and for publishers authoring manifests that carry ad
opportunities.

The key words MUST, MUST NOT, REQUIRED, SHALL, SHALL NOT, SHOULD, SHOULD NOT,
RECOMMENDED, MAY and OPTIONAL in this document are to be interpreted as
described in IETF RFC 2119.

**Notation.** `@name` denotes an XML attribute. Element names are written
capitalised and in angle brackets, as `<Event>`. The prefix `svta:` denotes the
extension namespace this specification introduces; the prefix is conventional
and an implementation binds the namespace URI under any prefix. `DASH §X.Y`
refers to a clause of the base specification identified in chapter 2. XML
examples elide attributes that carry no meaning for the point being made.

## 1. Scope

### 1.1 What this specification covers

This specification defines SGAI for **both linear and non-linear ads** in
MPEG-DASH, as one complete extension of the base specification identified in
chapter 2.

**Linear SGAI is absorbed as the baseline.** The base specification already
carries the constructs a linear ad break needs: the alternative-MPD insertion
and replacement events, the List MPD that resolves them, and the imported
single-period MPD that carries each creative. Of the alternative Media
Presentation the base specification says:

> "An alternative Media Presentation is a presentation that replaces the main
> Media Presentation at a certain point on the media timeline for a duration of
> time. After the playback of the alternative Media Presentation is complete,
> the playback is continued with the main Media Presentation. A key use case for
> this is advertisement, where alternative Media Presentation represents the
> inserted advertisement" (DASH §5.16.1)

This specification reuses those constructs verbatim, states the Player
behaviour a conformant linear break relies on, and extends them only where the
baseline leaves an ad deployment without an answer: the meaning of a slot
declaration that carries no cap, the binding of a candidate to the declarations
of the window that served it, and the order in which the candidates of one slot
are presented.

**Non-linear SGAI is the principal new content.** This specification defines
the opportunity window, the resolution document, the creative carriers, the
selection procedure, the composition rules, the tracking carrier and the
click carrier for ads that coexist with the primary content — overlays composited
over playing content, squeezebacks that shrink the primary content to share the
frame, and pause ads presented while the viewer has paused.

**Player behaviour is specified normatively here.** The base specification is
explicit that it does not specify it:

> "as DASH Client operation is not specified normatively in this document, it is
> also unspecified how a DASH Client conforms to a particular profile. Hence,
> profiles merely specify restrictions on MPD and Segments rather than DASH
> Client behaviour." (DASH §8.1 NOTE 1)

An ad contract that stops at manifest syntax leaves the viewer-visible outcome
undetermined, so chapters 4 and 7 state obligations for each actor, and the
guarantees this specification makes are scoped to actors that conform to it.

### 1.2 The four-actor model

Four actors participate. The **Publisher** owns the primary content and the
screen, and declares in the main manifest where an ad opportunity sits on the
timeline, which family it belongs to, which layouts it admits and what its cap
is; the Publisher acts at authoring time only. The **Ad Decision Server (ADS)**
decides which ads fill an opportunity, how many, in what order, and owns the
tracking schedule, emitting a decision document. The **Ad Presentation Server
(APS)** is the Player-facing adapter: it exposes the endpoint the opportunity's
`@uri` resolves to, obtains the decision from the ADS, and converts it into the
resolution document the Player reads. The **Player** reads the manifest,
resolves the APS, validates each candidate against the declarations of the
window that served it, selects, renders, composes, fires beacons and enforces
the cap.

Stated once: the Publisher declares *when* and *what kind*; the ADS decides
*which ads* and *how many*; the APS converts; the Player validates, selects and
renders. The Player is the enforcer of the Publisher's policy, and it is the
only actor this specification relies on for enforcement — the ADS and the APS
are external services whose output this specification treats as untrusted input.

### 1.3 Out of scope

Each exclusion is stated where a reader would look for the capability, with its
reason.

- **A parallel layout engine.** Spatial arrangement is delegated to HTML5 and
  CSS and to the IAB-defined layouts named in §3.2; a second layout standard
  would compete with them rather than complete them.
- **Position semantics inside a layout** (left, right, top, bottom). They follow
  from the IAB ad type the layout token names, and are rendered with HTML5 and
  CSS primitives.
- **The ADS's decisioning logic and the APS-to-ADS contract.** Targeting,
  frequency capping, brand-safety filtering, competitive separation and the
  exchange that conveys them are implementation-specific; this specification
  defines the contract from the manifest through the resolution document to the
  screen.
- **Creative carrier formats outside video, image and HTML.** Raw scripts,
  vector payloads, documents and proprietary binary creatives are outside the
  admissible set of §3.3; a sender needing a scripted creative wraps the script
  in an HTML document and uses the HTML form.
- **Interactive ad frameworks such as SIMID.** An ad built on such a framework
  is delivered by that framework; this specification defines no form that
  carries such a payload.
- **Single-decoder slice or tile replacement.** The technique exists, and this
  edition's decoder budgets in §3.4 assume one decoder per concurrent form; a
  future edition may cover it.
- **Seek-blocking on the non-linear families.** The base specification's
  `@noJump` control stays preserved and unchanged on the inherited linear
  breaks, where the ad occupies the timeline and forbidding the jump forbids
  skipping the ad. On the non-linear families the region a window covers is
  programme playing underneath the ad, so blocking the jump there would oblige
  the viewer to watch programme rather than an ad.

The composition of two visual surfaces is likewise left to the implementation.
The base specification takes the same position for its own supplementary-video
service:

> "Potential manipulation of the stream and the composition of the main video
> and the supplementary video are out of the scope of the DASH client."
> (DASH §5.8.5.16)

This specification states which surfaces are simultaneously active, in what
order and for how long, and leaves how they are drawn to the device.

The following documents are referred to in the text in such a way that some or
all of their content constitutes requirements of this document. For dated
references, only the edition cited applies.

| Reference | Identity | Used for |
|---|---|---|
| The base specification | ISO/IEC 23009-1:2026 (Sixth edition, 2026-07) | The manifest schema, the event and Event Stream carriers, alternative-MPD insertion and replacement, the List MPD and Single-Period Static profiles, linked periods, the callback tracking scheme, the URL-parameter annex, and the `PlayList` metric. Every clause citation of the form `DASH §X.Y` in this document is a clause of this edition. |
| IETF RFC 2119 | *Key words for use in RFCs to Indicate Requirement Levels*, March 1997 | The normative vocabulary of this document. |
| IETF RFC 4337 | *MIME Type Registration for MPEG-4*, March 2006 | The media types every Representation of a sub-MPD carries. The base specification requires it: *"The @mimeType attribute of each Representation shall be provided according to IETF RFC 4337."* (DASH §7.3.1) |
| IETF RFC 6381 | *The 'Codecs' and 'Profiles' Parameters for "Bucket" Media Types*, August 2011 | The additional media-type parameters a Representation carries where this document cites them. The base specification admits them in the same clause: *"Additional parameters may be added according to IETF RFC 6381"* (DASH §7.3.1). |
| IETF RFC 8141 | *URN Syntax*, April 2017 | The syntax of the profile identifiers declared in `MPD@profiles`, including the profile URI this specification introduces. The base specification binds them: *"profiles with which an MPD complies are indicated in the MPD@profiles attribute as a comma-separated list of profile identifiers. Profile identifiers defined in this document are URNs and shall conform to IETF RFC 8141."* (DASH §8.1) |
| IAB Tech Lab, *Ad Format Guidelines for Digital Video and CTV* | Live document: https://docs.google.com/document/d/17JXFhHWWX1SVD3s2vMTMO-bvvj9XXK5e | The ad types, the visual placements and their spatial bounds that §3.2 adopts. The reference is to the live document rather than to a snapshot, because the definitions are maintained there; the subset this edition admits is fixed by §3.2 and does not widen when the source does. |

**Informative reference.** IAB VAST 4.x is referenced informatively only. This
specification depends on no VAST version: the ADS emits a decision document
that is typically VAST but is not required to be, and every mention of VAST in
this document is illustrative of a common deployment rather than a requirement
of it. Conformance is assessed against the resolution document the APS
produces, which this specification defines in full.

### 2.1 URIs this edition introduces

A URI is declared by the edition that introduces it, so the five URIs below are
stated here and used throughout the rest of the document. The `2026` suffix is
the edition year; a later edition introduces its own URIs rather than
redefining these.

| URI | Role |
|---|---|
| `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026` | The extension namespace of every element and attribute this specification introduces. Conventional prefix `svta`. |
| `urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026` | The `@schemeIdUri` of the Event Stream carrying overlay opportunity windows. |
| `urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-pause-trigger:2026` | The `@schemeIdUri` of the Event Stream carrying pause-trigger opportunity windows. |
| `urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026` | The profile identifier of the non-linear resolution document, declared in its `MPD@profiles`. |
| `urn:svta:dash:request:sgai-resolution:2026` | The request-type URN naming the non-linear resolution request, for scoping Publisher-declared URL parameters to it. |

URIs this specification reuses without redefining them — the DASH namespace,
the callback tracking scheme, the alternative-MPD insertion and replacement
schemes, the List MPD and Single-Period Static profiles, and the URL-parameter
scheme — belong to the base specification and are cited where they are used.

## 3. Terms, definitions, abbreviations

For the purposes of this document, the following terms and definitions apply.

**ad candidate** — one ad offered for a slot by the resolution document,
carrying a declared duration, one or more presentation options, and optionally
its tracking schedule, its click carrier and application-level metadata.

**ad decision server (ADS)** — the actor that decides which ads fill an
opportunity, how many and in what order, and that owns the tracking schedule.
It emits a decision document, typically VAST. It performs no conversion into
the resolution document, and the enforcement of the Publisher's declarations
sits elsewhere.

**Ad Presentation Server (APS)** — the Player-facing actor that exposes the
endpoint an opportunity window's `@uri` resolves to, obtains the decision from
the ADS, and converts it into the resolution document. It is a translation and
presentation layer.

**beacon** — a tracking URL the Player fetches at a scheduled point of an ad's
presentation. Beacons ride the base specification's callback event scheme,
whose response the client ignores: *"the client is expected to entirely ignore
the response"* (DASH §5.10.4.5.1).

**cap** — the maximum duration a slot declares, carried by `@maxDuration` on the
opportunity window.

**capability parameter** — a query parameter the Player attaches to a resolution
request describing what its device renders. The names are reserved by this
specification; sending any of them is optional, and an absent parameter means
its value is undetermined rather than unsupported.

**creative-carrier form** — the kind of asset a presentation option renders:
`video`, `image` or `html`. Enumerated in §3.3.

**device class** — a device's rendering capability expressed on the two axes
this specification depends on: how many video decoders it runs concurrently,
and which surfaces it composites over video. Enumerated in §3.4.

**Earliest Resolution Time (ERT)** — the earliest presentation time at which the
Player issues the resolution request for an opportunity window, obtained by
subtracting the window's resolution-time offset from its `@presentationTime`.

**empty resolution** — a well-formed resolution document of the expected family
that carries no candidate. It is served as a successful response with a body,
and it states that no ad is available for that slot.

**failed execution** — an attempt to resolve an opportunity window that produces
no candidate to present. The base specification defines the outcome: *"A failed
execution results in smooth continued playback of the main media presentation."*
(DASH §5.16.2.2.6)

**foreign-namespace open content** — the base specification's extension
mechanism, under which elements and attributes from namespaces other than the
DASH namespace appear under DASH containers. A conformant manifest stays valid
once they are removed: *"the MPD shall be authored such that, after XML
attributes or elements in the other namespaces than the DASH namespace are
removed, the result is a valid XML document formatted according to that schema
and that conforms to this document."* (DASH §5.2.1) Every construct this
specification introduces is carried this way.

**`<ImportedMPD>`** — the base specification's element by which a period imports
its content from an external manifest: *"Linked Periods are Periods which import
their content from an external MPD, referred to as imported MPD. Linked Periods
are indicated by the ImportedMPD element in a Period element."* (DASH §5.3.2.6.1)

**layout token** — the value of a presentation option's `@layout`, naming one
spatial arrangement of an ad on the screen. The complete value space is §3.2.

**legacy Player** — a Player that implements the base specification and has
never heard of this specification. It removes this specification's
foreign-namespace content and plays the primary content uninterrupted.

**linear ad** — an ad whose form takes over the primary content surface for the
duration of a slot. The Player switches its rendering source from the primary
timeline to the ad and back. The timing positions this edition covers are
pre-roll, mid-roll, and multi-ad breaks.

**List MPD** — the base specification's manifest type used as the **linear**
resolution document: `MPD@type="list"`, one period per ad, each importing its
creative.

**main manifest** — the manifest of the primary content, authored by the
Publisher, in which opportunity windows are declared.

**non-linear ad** — an ad that coexists with the primary content rather than
replacing it on the timeline: composited over playing content, sharing the
frame with shrunk primary content, or presented while playback is paused.

**opportunity window** — a Publisher-authored declaration, carried by an event
in the main manifest, of a place on the timeline where an ad may be presented,
together with the layouts it admits and its cap. The window bounds where the
opportunity exists, not what fills it.

**Player** — the client-side actor that reads the main manifest, issues
resolution requests, validates candidates against the declarations of the window
that served them, selects, renders and composites the ad, fires the beacons and
enforces the cap.

**presentation option** — a form-and-layout pairing a candidate offers. A
candidate carries one or more; when it carries several, document order is the
preference order the Player follows. The base specification uses the same
convention for alternatives it offers: *"If multiple URLs are provided, the
content author expresses the preferences of using one of those by the order with
the first one having the highest preference."* (DASH §5.11.3)

**presentation timeline** — the media timeline of the presentation being
rendered, against which durations, cap arithmetic and beacon schedules are
computed. It does not advance while playback is suspended.

**primary content** — the programme the Publisher owns, into or over which ads
are presented.

**Publisher** — the actor that owns the primary content and the viewer's screen,
and that authors the opportunity windows in the main manifest. It acts at
authoring time only.

**resolution document** — the document the APS returns to the Player in answer to
a resolution request, describing the ads offered for a slot. For the linear
family it is a List MPD, or a single-period alternative manifest for a
single-ad slot; for the non-linear families it is the Overlay Resolution
Document.

**resolution request** — the HTTP GET the Player issues against the URL an
opportunity window declares, at or after the Earliest Resolution Time.

**Overlay Resolution Document** — the resolution document of the **non-linear**
families, identified by this specification's profile URI. "Overlay" in this name
denotes the non-linear family as a whole, not the `overlay` layout token: a
pause-ad resolution document is an Overlay Resolution Document.

**SGAI (Server-Guided Ad Insertion)** — the pattern in which the ad decision is
taken server-side and the manifest carries a reference to it, so that the Player
resolves a URL at the moment of the opportunity and receives a document
describing the ads to present, rather than receiving ads stitched into the
primary content.

**slot** — the interval in which the ads obtained from one opportunity window are
presented.

**sub-MPD** — a manifest reached through `<ImportedMPD>`, carrying one ad
creative and conforming to the Single-Period Static profile.

**Single-Period Static (SPS) profile** — the base specification's restrictive
profile for imported manifests: *"Single-Period static MPDs are used as Imported
MPDs in context of Linked Periods and are referenced from the
Period.ImportedMPD element."* (DASH §8.15)

### 3.1 Ad families

This specification recognises **four ad families**. A family is a kind of ad
opportunity — it determines how the opportunity is declared, which resolution
document answers it, and how the presentation relates to the primary content.

| Family | Relation to the primary content |
|---|---|
| `linear` | The ad takes over the primary content surface for the slot; the primary content is inserted-past or replaced on the timeline. |
| `overlay` | The ad is composited over the playing primary content, which continues underneath. |
| `squeezeback` | The primary content is shrunk to share the frame with the ad; nothing of it is covered. |
| `pause-ad` | The ad is presented while the viewer has paused; the slot lasts as long as the pause. |

A **layout token** is a different thing: it names **one spatial arrangement** of
one ad on the screen, and it is the value a presentation option carries in
`@layout`. One family admits several layout tokens — the `squeezeback` family
admits three — and one token belongs to exactly one family.

**The word `overlay` is a member of both vocabularies, and the two meanings are
not the same.** As a family, `overlay` covers every arrangement composited over
playing content, including the corner and lower-third placements. As a layout
token, `overlay` names exactly one of them: a plain image or HTML surface with
no named placement. The names of the non-linear resolution document and of its
profile URI use the family sense. Where this document needs the distinction it
writes "the `overlay` family" or "the `overlay` layout token".

### 3.2 Accepted ad types and layout tokens

This subclause is normative and defines the **complete, closed** value space of
`@layout`.

Every value below maps 1:1 onto an ad type and a visual placement defined by the
IAB Tech Lab *Ad Format Guidelines for Digital Video and CTV* (live document:
https://docs.google.com/document/d/17JXFhHWWX1SVD3s2vMTMO-bvvj9XXK5e). This
specification adopts those identifiers and defines **no** ad type and **no**
layout value of its own.

| Layout token | Family | IAB ad type | IAB visual placement |
|---|---|---|---|
| `linear` | linear | Linear Ad | Full video viewing pane, 16:9 preferred; the timing positions this edition covers are pre-roll, mid-roll and multi-ad breaks. |
| `overlay` | overlay | Overlay | Over the playing content, with no named placement; the base type of a plain image or HTML surface. |
| `overlay-corner` | overlay | Overlay | Corner Overlay — up to 25% of a 1920×1080 screen, generally in one of the four corners. |
| `overlay-lower-third` | overlay | Overlay | Lower Third Overlay — 30% of the bottom of a 1920×1080 screen. |
| `squeezeback-l-shape` | squeezeback | Squeezeback | L-Shape — primary content 60% of the frame, squeezed upper-left or upper-right, the ad across the bottom and the vertical bar. |
| `squeezeback-double-box` | squeezeback | Squeezeback | Double Box Video — primary content and ad each 25% of the frame, content centre-left and ad centre-right; the uncovered bands render as black. |
| `squeezeback-double-box-with-background` | squeezeback | Squeezeback | Double Box Video + Background — the Double Box arrangement plus an advertiser-branded background image filling the bands between and around the two boxes. |
| `pause-fullscreen` | pause-ad | Pause Ad | Fullscreen — 1920×1080, 16:9, occupying the whole screen surface of the pause experience. |
| `pause-partial` | pause-ad | Pause Ad | Partial Screen — 600×600, 1:1, composited over the paused primary frame, which stays visible underneath. |

A Publisher declaring the layouts a window admits draws every token from this
table. An APS emitting a presentation option draws its `@layout` from this table.
A Player matches a candidate's `@layout` against the window's admitted tokens by
exact string comparison.

**The set is a deliberate per-edition snapshot.** An ad type or visual placement
the IAB publishes after this edition stays outside its scope until a later
edition of this specification admits it. Widening the set is an edition-level
act, not a runtime one, so that a Publisher's declaration, an APS's output and a
Player's matching agree on one fixed vocabulary.

**Spatial bounds are inherited by normative reference.** Each token above carries
the spatial bound the IAB declares for the placement it names — a Corner Overlay
occupying no more than 25% of the frame, an L-Shape leaving the primary content
at 60% of the frame, and likewise for the others. Those bounds apply as the IAB
states them. This specification introduces no dimensional attribute and
re-declares no dimension in the manifest, so there is one place where a bound is
written and one place where it changes.

**IAB types outside this set.** Four types the IAB catalogue defines stay outside
this edition:

- **Menu Ad** — rendered in the platform user interface (home screen, content
  navigation, guide), off the playing or paused video surface.
- **Screen Saver Ad** — rendered by the operating system or the application after
  a period of inactivity, off the video surface.
- **Companion Ad** — rendered outside the player, alongside or surrounding it.
- **In Scene Ads** — composited into the programme upstream of the Player rather
  than presented as a Player-side ad surface, and outside the closed set above.

The first three are concerns of the application's ad integration rather than of
the Player-facing contract this specification defines. The fourth is outside the
closed set; it is named here so that a reader finds the answer where they look
for it.

### 3.3 Creative-carrier forms

A presentation option carries its creative in exactly one of **three** forms,
the value of `@form`.

| Form | The creative | Concrete media types |
|---|---|---|
| `video` | An ISO-BMFF video presentation reached through the option's `<ImportedMPD>` child, whose sub-MPD conforms to the Single-Period Static profile. | Every Representation of the sub-MPD carries `video/mp4`, `audio/mp4` or `application/mp4`, per the RFC 4337 registry the base specification requires in DASH §7.3.1. |
| `image` | A still image retrieved from the option's asset URL. | `image/jpeg`, `image/png`, `image/webp`. |
| `html` | An HTML document retrieved from the option's asset URL. Inline `<script>` MAY appear in it and runs under the device's HTML capability contract; scripting is not a separate form. | `text/html`. |

These three are the admissible set wherever this document, its annexes and its
examples discuss creative carriers. A sender whose creative is a script, a
vector payload, a document or a proprietary binary wraps it in an HTML document
and uses the `html` form, or delivers it outside this specification.

### 3.4 Device classes

A device class expresses the two rendering capabilities the presentation rules of
this specification depend on: **how many video decoders** the device runs
concurrently, and **which surfaces it composites over video**. Codec support,
DRM, screen geometry and network conditions are orthogonal and are handled
outside this specification. Chapters 4, 5 and 7 and the annexes refer to this
subclause as §3.4.

| Class | Video decoders | Image over video | HTML over video |
|---|---|---|---|
| **D1** | 2 or more | yes | yes |
| **D2** | 2 | no | no |
| **D3** | 1 | yes | yes |
| **D4** | 1 | yes | no |
| **D5** | 1 | no | no |

D2 composites a second **video** on the primary content using its two decoders,
and composites no non-video surface on video. D5 composites no overlay surface
of any kind.

**Decoder and surface budgets.** The table below states, per layout token and
per creative-carrier form, the classes on which the option is renderable. It is
the budget every rule and every annex of this document respects.

| Layout token | `video` creative | `image` creative | `html` creative |
|---|---|---|---|
| `linear` | D1..D5 | D1..D5 | D1..D5 |
| `overlay`, `overlay-corner`, `overlay-lower-third` | D1, D2 | D1, D3, D4 | D1, D3 |
| `squeezeback-l-shape` | D1, D2 | D1, D3, D4 | D1, D3 |
| `squeezeback-double-box` | D1, D2 | D1, D3, D4 | D1, D3 |
| `squeezeback-double-box-with-background` | D1 | D1, D3, D4 | D1, D3 |
| `pause-fullscreen` | D1..D5 | D1, D3, D4 | D1, D3 <!-- refine: v9-spec-validation.md#T1 --> |
| `pause-partial` | D1, D2 | D1, D3, D4 | D1, D3 |

How the budgets follow:

- An **overlay** presented as `video` needs a second decoder alongside the one
  presenting the primary content. Presented as `image` it needs an image surface
  over video, and presented as `html` an HTML surface over video.
- A **squeezeback** keeps the primary content on screen, so the shrunk primary
  content always costs one decoder; a `video` creative costs a second, while an
  `image` or `html` creative costs a surface instead.
  `squeezeback-double-box-with-background` additionally needs an image surface
  for the background, which is why it is renderable on neither D2 nor D5
  whatever the creative's form.
- `linear` is a full-screen takeover of the primary content surface, so it is
  renderable on every class. On a non-linear slot it is the option of last
  resort.
- `pause-fullscreen` replaces the whole surface, so the Player MAY release the
  resources of the primary content and of any overlay: a `video` pause ad is
  therefore renderable on every class. An `image` or `html` pause ad still needs
  its surface, but nothing is being composited **over video** at that moment, so
  what decides is whether the device has the surface at all — which D5 lacks.
- `pause-partial` leaves the paused primary frame visible underneath, so its
  budget is the overlay budget.

The `<svta:BackgroundElement>` of
`squeezeback-double-box-with-background` is always a still image, so it consumes
an image surface and never a video decoder.

## 4. Conformance

### 4.1 What conformance means here

This specification defines conformance for four roles: **Publisher**,
**ADS**, **APS** and **Player**. A product MAY implement more than one
of them — an APS deployed as a module of an ADS is the common case —
and a conformance claim names the roles it is made for. Each is checked
against a different artefact: the Publisher against the main MPD it
authors, the APS against the resolution document it returns, the Player
against its runtime behaviour, and the ADS against the narrow surface
§4.3 describes.

**An obligation this specification places on a Player binds a Player
conformant to this specification.** That scoping is not a choice made
here. The base specification places no normative obligation on a DASH
client at all:

> DASH §8.1, NOTE 1: *"as DASH Client operation is not specified
> normatively in this document, it is also unspecified how a DASH
> Client conforms to a particular profile. Hence, profiles merely
> specify restrictions on MPD and Segments rather than DASH Client
> behaviour."*

The same clause set carries the consequence for extension content.
DASH §5.2.1 puts the removal obligation on the author — *"the MPD
shall be authored such that, after XML attributes or elements in the
other namespaces than the DASH namespace are removed, the result is a
valid XML document formatted according to that schema and that
conforms to this document"* — and its NOTE 2 draws the client-side
consequence: a client that removes every attribute and element outside
the base schema is left with a document it can present as a conforming
Media Presentation `[inferred]`.

**This is not a weakening.** An obligation on a Player that has never
heard of this specification is not available to be made, here or in
any other extension of the base standard, so scoping it to conformant
Players is the strongest form the obligation has. What replaces the
missing half is auditable and is §4.7: every construct introduced here
is removal-safe, so a Player that predates this specification keeps
playing the primary content.

### 4.2 Publisher conformance

The Publisher is an **authoring-time** role: everything it owes is in
the main MPD before a viewer arrives, and every unmet obligation below
degrades into a Player-side skip rather than into interrupted
playback. A conformant Publisher:

- **Declares each ad opportunity as a window of one family** — linear
  with the inherited alternative-MPD event elements, overlay with
  `<svta:OverlayPresentation>`, pause with `<svta:PauseAdPresentation>`,
  each on an `<Event>` of its family's scheme. The window carries the
  address the Player resolves (`@uri`) and the declarations binding what
  may be presented in it.

- **Declares a maximum duration on every slot**, linear or non-linear.
  This narrows the base specification, which reads an absent maximum
  as unbounded: *"If absent, the value is assumed to be infinity, in
  which case the current presentation resumes only when the
  alternative presentation terminates"* (DASH §5.16.5.2). The
  narrowing restricts which documents conform and changes no
  construct's semantics, which is what a profile does (DASH §8.1); it
  is recorded so that a reader who finds the unbounded default knows
  it was excluded deliberately. A declared maximum of zero keeps its
  base meaning — *"If the value of @maxDuration is zero, the event is
  not executed"* (DASH §5.16.5.2) — so a zero cap is a slot that does
  not fire rather than a very short one. What a Player does with a
  slot carrying **no** maximum is §4.5.4, and that position is
  **provisional and open with the working group**.

- **Draws every allowed-layout token from the accepted set** of §3.2,
  each mapping one-to-one onto an IAB-defined ad type or visual
  placement. A pause window names a surface — `pause-fullscreen`,
  `pause-partial`, or both — because a Publisher can admit one and
  exclude the other only where the two are distinguishable in the
  declaration.

- **Authors all windows of one family that share a `<Period>` as
  `<Event>` entries inside a single `<EventStream>`**, which the base
  specification fixes for every event type: *"all Events of one type
  shall be clustered in one Event Stream"* (DASH §5.10.2.1).

- **Requests the play-list metric on content carrying pause windows**,
  through the `<Metrics>` element of the main MPD. Collection is
  triggered by the service provider rather than by the Player — *"The
  trigger mechanism is based on the Metrics element in the MPD"* (DASH
  §5.9.1) — so without the declaration §4.5.16 derives a quantity that
  nothing obliges anyone to collect.

- **MAY bound a pause window to once per session**, with
  `@executeOnce="true"`; a window that omits it yields a pause ad on
  every qualifying pause. It mirrors the base specification's
  single-execution bound — *"specifies whether the event is executed
  only once"* (DASH §5.16.5.2) — restated for a trigger that is the
  viewer's pause rather than the playhead (§4.5.11).

- **Declares its own query parameters on the resolution request, where
  it uses any**, with `<RequestParam>` of `ExtendedUrlInfoType` under
  `urn:mpeg:dash:urlparam:2025`, which *"may be present in elements
  such as but not limited to MPD, Period, AdaptationSet,
  Representation, Preselection, or EventStream"* (DASH §I.3.1).
  `@includeInRequests="altmpd"` scopes a declaration to
  alternative-MPD requests; the non-linear resolution request is named
  by `urn:svta:dash:request:sgai-resolution:2026` in the same
  attribute. Parameters the **Player** attaches are a separate
  mechanism, declared by no one (§4.5.1).


### 4.3 ADS conformance

**This section is short, and the shortness is the design.** The ADS
decides which ads fill an opportunity, how many and in what order, and
owns the tracking schedule — business decisions this specification does
not observe. What it emits never reaches a Player either, because the
APS converts it first, so what is checkable is the APS's document.
Three consequences are stated so that a checker does not invent
obligations the model does not carry:

- **The slot cap does not bind the ADS.** A conformance check on an
  ADS passes regardless of whether the cumulative duration of the
  candidates it returns exceeds the Publisher's cap; enforcement sits
  with the Player (§4.5.4), which is what lets one ADS serve several
  Publishers with different policies. This sentence bounds a
  *checker*, not the ADS's behaviour.
- **The ADS holds no device-capability view.** Producing candidates
  without a device-class matrix is conformant; holding one is equally
  conformant, and the Player's own capability check (§4.5.3) is
  unchanged either way.
- **No-fill is a decision, not a failure.** How it is expressed to the
  Player is the APS's obligation (§4.4); what the Player does with it
  is §4.5.6's.

The ADS's output format, and the exchange between the APS and the ADS,
are outside this specification and agreed bilaterally by those two
parties.

### 4.4 APS conformance

The APS produces the **resolution document**, the one non-Player
artefact this specification checks at runtime. A conformant APS
returns, for each request it answers, a document that satisfies all of
the following.

- **It parses and validates**, tracking subtree included; what that
  means is §4.6.

- **It answers the family the slot asked for** — a `ListMPD` or a
  single-period alternative MPD for a linear slot, an Overlay
  Resolution Document for an overlay or pause slot. A document of
  another family is a failure to resolve at the Player (§4.5.6)
  however good its contents are.

- **An unfilled opportunity is a document carrying no candidates** —
  well-formed, complete, served `200` with a body. The distinction is
  between *a document that says nothing was sold* and *no document at
  all*: expressing the first as an error collapses two conditions onto
  one code and leaves an auditor unable to tell an unfilled
  opportunity from a broken one.

- **Presentation options are an ordered list in preference order.**
  Document order carries the preference, and no ranking attribute is
  defined. How many options a candidate carries is the APS's decision:
  no maximum, no minimum beyond one. A candidate carrying exactly one
  option is conformant and places the suitability call upstream, with
  the APS or with the ADS that returned a single option to it.

- **Creatives are inside the admissible carrier set, on carriers the
  base standard admits.** The admissible set is video, image and HTML
  (§3.3). A video creative is reached through `<ImportedMPD>`, which
  the base specification binds to the single-period profile — *"MPDs
  referenced in the ImportedMPD element shall be restricted to the
  constraints of a single period profile as defined in 8.15"* (DASH
  §5.3.2.6.1) — where every Representation carries a media type from
  the registry it names: *"The @mimeType attribute of each
  Representation shall be provided according to IETF RFC 4337"* (DASH
  §7.3.1). A still image or an HTML document travels as an attribute
  value on `<svta:RenderableAsset>`, which is foreign-namespace open
  content and inherits no media-type constraint to escape.

- **Form metadata is emitted only for accepted ad types**, drawn from
  the set of §3.2; conformance is checked against the document rather
  than against the ADS's internal decision.

- **Beacons are callback events on the ad's presentation timeline** —
  `<Event>` entries inside an `<EventStream>` of scheme
  `urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015`, timed relative to the
  presentation of the ad that carries them. Every such `<Event>`
  carries an `@id`, which this specification narrows from optional to
  required on this carrier because de-duplication is keyed on it
  (§4.5.13).

- **The ClickThrough and its click-tracking travel in
  `<svta:Click>`.** Whether a ClickThrough has click-tracking at all
  is the advertiser's decision; a ClickThrough carried elsewhere is a
  violation the document itself shows.

- **Every pause resolution document declares an exhaustion
  behaviour**, in `@onCandidatesExhausted`. The declaration is the
  APS's rather than the Publisher's: a Publisher-declared limit on how
  many documents may be served for one pause would be answered by
  defensively long candidate lists, since a single response would be
  the APS's only opportunity — the constraint would produce the
  outcome it exists to prevent.

<!-- refine: v9-spec-validation.md#T4 -->
- **The answer depends on no reserved capability parameter.** The APS
  MUST tolerate the absence of every reserved parameter of §5.8, and
  MUST be able to produce candidates without receiving any of them;
  requiring one would make the Player's freedom to omit it
  unattainable. An absent
  parameter means **undetermined**, and how an APS resolves an
  undetermined value is its own decision — two APSs that resolve it
  differently are both conformant.

The conversion from the ADS's decision format, and the fidelity of that
transcription, sit in the APS-to-ADS contract: the resolution document
cannot show what the ADS declared, so it cannot carry that obligation.

### 4.5 Player conformance

The Player is the enforcer. It reads the main MPD, resolves the APS,
validates each candidate against the window that served it, selects,
renders, composes, fires beacons and holds the cap. Every obligation
below binds a Player conformant to this specification, for the reason
§4.1 gives.

#### 4.5.1 Resolving an opportunity

A window's Earliest Resolution Time (ERT) is its `@presentationTime`
minus `@earliestResolutionTimeOffset`, which defaults to 60 seconds in
the units of the parent `<EventStream>@timescale`. The Player MUST
issue the resolution request against the window's `@uri` at or after
the ERT; for a linear or overlay window, resolving anywhere between
the ERT and the window's presentation time is conformant.

For a **pause** window the trigger is the viewer rather than the
playhead. The Player MUST request a resolution document when a viewer
pause begins inside a pause opportunity window; a pause beginning
outside every such window leaves the primary content paused and the
Player idle. The Player MAY resolve speculatively at the ERT and hold
the document until a pause occurs, or lazily at the moment of the
pause.

The Player MAY attach the reserved capability parameters of §5.8. Each
is optional and the decision is the Player's, taken at runtime with no
prior declaration by anyone. Where the Player has no value for a
reserved parameter, or chooses not to disclose it, the Player MUST
omit that parameter entirely. A parameter outside the reserved set
MUST carry a vendor prefix of the form `x-<vendor>-`, so that names
reserved by a later edition stay free.

#### 4.5.2 Validating a candidate against the window that served it

Before anything is rendered, the Player MUST validate each candidate
against the declarations of the window that served it: that window's
`@allowedLayouts`, its `@maxDuration`, and the admissible creative
carriers of §3.3. A candidate that satisfies them is eligible; one
that satisfies none is skipped and the Player moves to the next
candidate in document order.

Validation is the Player's alone: neither the ADS nor the APS is bound
by the Publisher's declarations, which is what lets a Player guarantee
them even when both are external, non-audited services.

#### 4.5.3 Selecting among presentation options in document order

A candidate carries its presentation options as an ordered list, and
**document order is the preference order**. The Player MUST walk the
options in document order and render the **first** that satisfies
both:

1. **the device** — the Player renders only a form and layout whose
   decoder and compositing budget its device class supplies (§3.4);
   and
2. **the window** — the option's `@layout` matches a token in the
   window's `@allowedLayouts` by exact token comparison.

An option failing either check is passed over and the Player evaluates
the next in document order. Where no option on a candidate satisfies
both, the Player MUST skip that candidate and continue with the next.
Once every candidate has been walked, the Player MUST continue with
the primary content.

**A document carrying candidates is a resolution, whatever the Player
then does with them.** Skipping every candidate for want of a
satisfiable option ends at the primary content, not at the next window
of the family; the window-level chain of §4.5.6 is reached only by the
conditions that section lists.

#### 4.5.4 Cap enforcement

Every slot declares `@maxDuration`, and what the cap bounds depends on
the family.

- On a linear **replacement** slot it bounds *until when*: the
  presentation ends at the scheduled end of the slot, so an event
  firing late yields a shorter ad rather than a later end. `@clip`,
  default `true`, carries that; `@clip="false"` moves the end to one
  cap-length after the actual start `[inferred]`. The Player MUST
  honour whichever the event declares — an event declaring
  `@clip="false"` ending later than the scheduled end is the base
  specification moving the bound, not the Player exceeding it.
- On a linear **insertion** slot and on every non-linear family it
  bounds *how long*: the cumulative rendered length of the forms the
  slot presents. Insertion stops the primary timeline and resumes it
  where it stopped, so there is no scheduled end to preserve.
- On a **pause** slot the viewer sets the slot's length, so the cap
  bounds the display of **one** pause ad before automatic dismissal.

**Drop before play.** The Player MAY drop a candidate before playback
when its declared duration would push the cumulative duration past the
cap.

**Trim during play.** Where an accepted candidate's actual rendered
length exceeds the cap, the Player MUST stop rendering at the bound,
even mid-ad, enforcing against actual length rather than declared
length. The remaining beacons of that candidate stop at the trim
boundary (§4.5.13).

**The timescale conversion.** The cap is in units of the parent
`<EventStream>@timescale` and a candidate's duration is an ISO 8601
`xs:duration`. Before comparing them the Player MUST convert the
candidate's duration into the cap's timescale, **rounding up** to the
next whole unit. A candidate whose converted duration equals the cap
exactly is admitted.

**Cap arithmetic runs on the presentation timeline.** An interval in
which that timeline does not advance does not accrue, so a form
suspended during a pause (§4.5.9) resumes with the cap it had.

**A cap of zero, and a slot with no cap.** A declared cap of zero
means the opportunity does not fire, which is the base specification's
own rule (DASH §5.16.5.2). A slot declaration carrying **no** cap is
not a slot this specification defines: the Player MUST present no ads
from it and MUST continue with the primary content. Reading the
absence as the base specification's unbounded default would make cap
enforcement inert for exactly the slots whose declaration is
defective.

**That last position is provisional and open with the working group.**
It diverges from the base specification's unbounded default, and what
is being weighed is which failure is worse: a Publisher who omits the
attribute sells nothing from that slot and may take a while to notice,
against an advertisement that runs for as long as it likes.

#### 4.5.5 Candidate order

<!-- refine: v9-spec-validation.md#T2 -->
The Player MUST present the candidates of a resolution document in the
order the document declares, each starting when the previous ends. It
MAY drop a candidate with no satisfiable presentation option (§4.5.3)
and MAY drop one before playback on declared duration (§4.5.4); after
those drops, the Player MUST preserve the declared order of the
surviving candidates, presenting each exactly once per pass over the
list. Where the slot's exhaustion behaviour is `repeat`, a further pass
presents the sequence again from the start (§4.5.11). Selection and
ordering happen upstream, and the order the document carries is the
one the Player honours.

#### 4.5.6 The fallback chain across overlapping windows of one family

Where two or more opportunity windows of the same family overlap in
time in the main MPD, they are a **declared fallback chain** rather
than a concurrency case: concurrency of windows would be concurrency of
presentation, asking the device for two simultaneous ad presentations
and the Player for a runtime arbitration between two equally valid
windows.

**Order.** The Player MUST order the overlapping windows by
presentation time, oldest first, breaking ties by the order in which
the events appear inside the `<EventStream>`. For the linear family
this is the base specification's own queue, processed *"from oldest
PRT to the most recent"* (DASH §5.16.2.2.5). Its execution model is
built on alternative-MPD events, which overlay and pause-trigger
windows are not, so this specification extends the same ordering to
the non-linear families rather than defining a second one. The
tie-break by position is added here, because the base queue does not
separate equal presentation times and position is what remains.

**The chain.** The Player MUST resolve the first window. An attempt on
a window that produces no ad is a **failed execution**, and on a
failed execution the Player MUST attempt the next window of the
family:

> DASH §5.16.2.2.5: *"If execution fails, steps a-c above are repeated
> for next events in QE, until: — Execution succeeds, or — PRT of the
> topmost event in the queue is in the future (i.e. PRT > PHP), or —
> The queue is empty. If no event can be successfully executed, the
> playback continues uninterrupted."*

**Five conditions are failed executions and the Player MUST treat them
alike:**

1. no response, or a failure at the transport level before any final
   status — the base specification's *"Alternative MPD is unavailable
   or invalid"* (DASH §5.16.2.2.6);
2. a final HTTP status other than `200` — the same condition: nothing
   was obtained;
3. a `200` whose body does not parse as a resolution document — *"…
   or invalid"*;
4. a `200` carrying a well-formed resolution document that carries
   **no candidates** — the base specification's *"Alternative MPD is a
   List MPD, and merge process resulted in no available media"*; and
5. a `200` carrying a well-formed resolution document of the **wrong
   family**, which this specification adds.

**The fifth is distinguished from the fourth deliberately, because the
two produce opposite outcomes from the same status.** A document
carrying no candidates is an *answer*: the opportunity was offered and
nothing filled it. A document of the wrong family answers a question
nobody asked — it cannot fill this slot whatever it contains, because
its candidates are of a kind the slot does not admit. The Player MUST
treat it as a failure to resolve and continue down the chain; reading
it as an empty resolution would let one misrouted response silence
every remaining window, which is the failure mode the chain exists to
prevent.

**The end of the chain.** When every window of the family has been
attempted and none produced an ad, the Player MUST continue with the
primary content uninterrupted — *"If no event can be successfully
executed, the playback continues uninterrupted"* (DASH §5.16.2.2.5).
Where the Publisher declared a single window, that is the behaviour
after the single attempt.

**A failed execution leaves the opportunity available.** The base
specification counts executions rather than attempts — the counter is
the *"Execution counter (number of times alternative MPD playback
successfully started)"* (DASH §5.16.2.2.2) — and states the
consequence: *"counter E.c has not been incremented due to the
failure, consequently if E.c = 0 the event can still be executed in
the future even if the value of @executeOnce is "true""* (DASH
§5.16.2.2.6, NOTE 3). A conformant Player MUST apply the same rule to
the windows defined here: an attempt that produced no ad leaves a
once-only window executable.

#### 4.5.7 Each window binds the candidates it serves

Each window in a fallback chain binds the candidates it serves with
**its own** declarations: its own `@allowedLayouts` and its own
`@maxDuration`. When the Player falls through to a second window, it
MUST validate that window's candidates against that window's
declarations. A declaration belongs to the window that carries it, and
a window standing in for another carries its own policy rather than
inheriting one.

This is a Player obligation and is stated as one: a rule that decides
which presentation option reaches the screen binds only where it can be
obeyed, and an illustration is read rather than obeyed.

#### 4.5.8 The single active form

At any instant the Player MUST keep at most **one** non-linear ad form
active on the screen. Where a slot carries several candidates, the
Player presents them in sequence, each starting when the previous
ends.

The bound exists for the device's decoder budget: two video overlays at
one instant would need three concurrent decoders, and several target
classes supply two. With one active form the device never needs more
than the primary content plus one ad form, which every class of §3.4
supplies.

#### 4.5.9 Cross-family priority during a pause

While the viewer is paused inside a pause window, the pause ad is the
only ad surface visible. The Player MUST:

- **suspend a coexisting overlay** and present the pause ad, whatever
  the pause ad's surface — a `pause-partial` ad composited over the
  paused frame suspends the overlay exactly as a `pause-fullscreen`
  one does, which keeps §4.5.8's bound intact during the pause;
- **suspend a linear ad that occupies the screen**, resuming it from
  the point at which it was suspended when the viewer resumes;
- on resume, **dismiss the pause ad within one rendering frame**, stop
  its remaining beacons, and **restore the suspended form only while
  its window is still open**; where that window expired during the
  pause, the Player MUST keep the surface clear.

This priority is not configurable: this specification carries no
construct by which the Publisher, the ADS or the APS inverts it.

#### 4.5.10 Pause-ad lifecycle and the live presentation-time freeze

A pause ad exists only while playback is paused. The Player MUST
dismiss any rendered pause ad within one rendering frame of the
pause-to-play transition, and MUST stop firing the beacons scheduled
for it from that transition onward: a beacon scheduled at a relative
time past the transition falls outside the pause ad's active window.

**In live content the Player MUST keep its presentation time frozen
inside the pause window for the duration of the pause**, even as the
live edge advances in wall-clock time. The window is anchored to the
Player's frozen presentation time rather than to the advancing live
timeline, which keeps the pause ad admissible until the viewer
resumes.

**That freeze is bounded by the time-shift buffer.** Where the pause
outlives the buffer, the resumption point is clipped to the buffer
edge, which is the base specification's own treatment of a resumption
time fallen out of the window — *"clipping RT to the timeshift buffer
(i.e. the time interval between TSBS and PHPLE)"* (DASH §5.16.2.2.5).
A Player that then resumes at the live edge performs that jump
**after** the resume from pause, outside the pause window.

#### 4.5.11 Exhaustion inside a pause

A pause slot has no authored duration, so the candidates may run out
while the viewer is still paused. The Player MUST apply the behaviour
the resolution document declares in `@onCandidatesExhausted`:

- **`repeat`** — present the sequence again from the start, for as
  long as the pause lasts;
- **`request-again`** — request a new resolution document for the same
  pause; where that request returns a document carrying no candidates,
  the Player MUST apply `stop` for the remainder of that pause;
- **`stop`** — present no further ad; the paused primary frame is
  shown.

Where the document declares nothing, the Player MUST apply `stop`: it
is what the viewer would see if this mechanism did not exist, and
every Player can perform it.

On resume the Player MUST return to the primary content immediately,
including mid-presentation. Falling through to the primary content on
exhaustion is not available here, because the primary content is
paused.

**Once-per-session.** On a pause window declared `@executeOnce="true"`,
the Player MUST present at most one pause ad for that window for the
session, and MUST leave the primary content uninterrupted on a later
qualifying pause inside it. The window is consumed when a pause ad
**begins rendering**, so a pause that resolves to no renderable
candidate leaves it available — the base specification's own counting
rule (§4.5.6) applied to a trigger the playhead does not drive.

#### 4.5.12 Playback speed and the derived wall-clock length

The Player MUST render every ad form, linear or non-linear, at the
playback speed of the primary content at the moment the ad is
presented: an ad presented while the primary content runs at 2×
follows it to 2×.

A form's declared `duration` is a value on the **presentation
timeline** for every form, including `image` and `html`, which have no
intrinsic media. The Player MUST derive the wall-clock on-screen
length as `duration / playback_speed`: a 10-second form at 2× is on
screen for 5 seconds. The presentation-timeline value is the single
declaration and the wall-clock length is computed from it rather than
authored beside it. Cap enforcement (§4.5.4) and beacon scheduling
(§4.5.13) run on the presentation timeline.

#### 4.5.13 Tracking execution and beacon de-duplication

Given an ad accepted for rendering, the Player MUST execute the
tracking schedule the resolution document carries, firing each beacon
at its specified relative time. The schedule is the ADS's; this
specification fixes the carrier and the timebase rather than the
fractions, the granularity or the count.

**The timebase.** Where the beacon carrier is an `<EventStream>`
hosted inside a `<svta:Candidate>`, the Player MUST resolve its
presentation times against **that candidate's own presentation**,
rather than against a `<Period>` the carrier does not sit in.

**De-duplication is scoped to the candidate.** Within one candidate,
beacons sharing an `@id`, or carrying the same URL at the same
presentation time, fire once. Two beacons carrying the same `@id` in
two different candidates of one document are two distinct beacons and
the Player MUST fire both. This is why `@id` is required on this
carrier (§4.4).

<!-- refine: v9-spec-validation.md#T3 -->
**Where the ad stops early, the beacons stop with it.** The Player MUST
stop firing the ad's remaining beacons: a beacon scheduled past a trim
boundary (§4.5.4), or past the dismissal of a pause ad on resume
(§4.5.10), falls outside the ad's active window.

**A beacon failure is non-fatal.** Where a beacon request fails,
times out or returns a non-2xx status, the Player MUST keep the ad and
the primary content unaffected; the base specification takes the same
position on a callback response — *"the client is expected to entirely
ignore the response"* (DASH §5.10.4.5.1).

#### 4.5.14 ClickThrough activation

Where the candidate being presented carries `<svta:Click>`, a Player
conformant to this specification MUST read the ClickThrough URL and,
on viewer activation, open it or hand it to the platform, and fire
every `<svta:ClickTracking>` URL the element carries. Click-tracking
fires on activation rather than at a presentation time. The guarantee
is scoped to conformant Players, for the reason §4.1 states.

#### 4.5.15 Graceful continuation on runtime failure

Where resolving or rendering an accepted ad fails at runtime — a
decode error, a malformed candidate, an ad segment returning an error
status, a network loss mid-ad — the Player MUST abort that ad and
continue playing the primary content uninterrupted. It MAY move to the
next candidate in document order or end the slot.

**What "uninterrupted" means here is observable**: the primary content
continues on its own timeline, with no freeze and no blank slate; no
tracking beacon fires for the opportunity that failed; and the viewer
cannot tell that an ad opportunity existed. An error overlay is a
visible artefact, so the Player renders one only where the application
explicitly opted in. This is the base specification's own floor for
its execution model — *"A failed execution results in smooth continued
playback of the main media presentation"* (DASH §5.16.2.2.6) —
extended to the constructs defined here.

#### 4.5.16 Deriving the pause-delivery measurement

A Player that reports metrics MUST derive the paused interval from the
base specification's play-list metric rather than from a metric of
this specification's own. That metric is *"A list of playback
periods"*, where a playback period is *"the time interval between a
user action and whichever occurs soonest of the next user action, the
end of playback or a failure that stops playback"* (DASH Annex D.4.6),
and each entry carries the *"Timestamp of the user action that starts
the playback period"*.

The paused interval is the interval between the end of a playback
period whose last continuously rendered stretch stopped on
`UserRequest`, and the `start` of the next entry whose `starttype` is
`Resume`. Against that interval, the duration the pause ad occupied is
the **filled fraction** — the quantity worth measuring, because the
number of pause opportunities is set by the viewer and moves for
reasons no party influences, while the filled fraction moves with what
the APS returns.

A playback period whose stretch stopped on `Rebuffering` is a stall
rather than a pause opportunity, and the Player MUST derive no paused
interval from it.

**The transport is out of scope**, as it is for the base
specification's own metrics: *"This document does not define
mechanisms for reporting metrics; however, it does define a set of
metrics and a mechanism that may be used by the service provider to
trigger metric collection and reporting at the clients, if a reporting
mechanism is available"* (DASH §5.9.1).

### 4.6 Validating a resolution document

A resolution document carrying the candidate-level tracking carrier is
an MPD of the base schema whose `<Period>` holds a foreign-namespace
element, inside which sits a baseline `<EventStream>`. The base schema
admits the foreign element with lax processing, which permits a
validator to validate that subtree without obliging it to: an
`<Event>` *"may contain further XML elements meaningful for a
particular event scheme. These may be defined in this document … or in
some external namespace"* (DASH §5.10.2.1).

**A validation procedure that reports such a document valid while
skipping its foreign-namespace subtree has not checked the tracking
carrier at all.** It has checked an MPD with one opaque element in it.
That is the outcome a reader arrives at by default, because it is what
a schema validator run with lax processing and no extension schema
does — and it reports success.

Checking the document means all of the following, and a procedure that
claims to validate a resolution document MUST perform them:

1. **The envelope validates against the base schema**: `MPD@profiles`
   carries the resolution-document profile URI, `@type="static"`, and
   a single `<Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">` whose zero
   duration is what makes a Period with no Adaptation Set conformant —
   *"At least one Adaptation Set shall be present in each Period
   unless the value of the @duration attribute of the Period is set to
   zero"* (DASH §5.3.2.2, Table 4).
2. **The extension subtree validates against the schema of
   `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026`**, which means the validator is given
   that schema rather than left to skip what it cannot resolve: the
   cardinalities of `<svta:OverlayList>`, `<svta:Candidate>` and
   `<svta:RenderableAsset>`, the required attributes on each, and the
   closed value spaces of `@form`, `@layout` and
   `@onCandidatesExhausted`.
3. **The baseline elements nested inside that subtree validate against
   the base schema at the position they occupy**: the tracking
   `<EventStream>` and its `<Event>` children against the base event
   types, with this specification's narrowing — an `@id` on every
   `<Event>` of this carrier — and the beacon URL as element text
   content.
4. **The removal test passes**: removing every attribute and element
   outside the base namespace leaves a document valid against the base
   schema, which is the authoring obligation of DASH §5.2.1 and the
   property §4.7 rests on.

A procedure performing 1 and 4 and omitting 2 and 3 states that the
document is a valid MPD. It states nothing about the tracking carrier,
and a report that does not distinguish the two invites the reader to
assume the stronger claim.

### 4.7 Backward compatibility, per construct

**Carrier classification.** Four carriers of the base standard are
available to an extension, and they differ in what a client that does
not recognise the extension is left with:

- **(a) Foreign-namespace open content** (DASH §5.2.1) — a new element
  or attribute in an extension namespace, on a container whose schema
  admits foreign content. The client removes it with its subtree and
  the surrounding document stands.
- **(b) Event Stream payload** (DASH §5.10) — an `<Event>` whose text
  content carries a payload under a scheme URI. A client that does not
  implement the scheme skips that stream's events.
- **(c1) Supplemental descriptor** (DASH §5.8.4.9) — a scheme URI on a
  `SupplementalProperty`. *"the DASH Client is expected to ignore the
  descriptor"*: the parent element survives and is processed normally.
  The extension **degrades**.
- **(c2) Essential descriptor** (DASH §5.8.4.8) — the same payload on
  an `EssentialProperty`. *"the DASH Client is expected to ignore the
  parent element that contains the descriptor"*: the `AdaptationSet`
  or `Representation` holding it is dropped. The extension
  **removes**.

**(c1) and (c2) are classified separately and never as one "(c)."**
They are the two carriers here whose legacy behaviour differs, and
that difference is the point of classifying: one degrades, the other
removes the parent element. A construct classified as "(c)" states
nothing auditable.

**Every construct this specification introduces is (a)**, and none
uses a descriptor. Why (c1) and (c2) were weighed and not taken is in
§5.3.2; this section states the classification and the audit.

#### 4.7.1 `<svta:OverlayPresentation>`

- **Placement**: child of `<Event>`, inside an `<EventStream>` whose
  `@schemeIdUri` is `urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026`, in a
  `<Period>` of the main MPD. One per `<Event>`.
- **Extension rule**: DASH §5.2.1 foreign-namespace open content, on a
  container the base schema opens to it (DASH §5.10.2.1), under the
  per-scheme rule of DASH §5.10 by which a client processes only the
  event schemes it implements.
- **Walk-through**: the parser first sees the element while reading the
  `<EventStream>` of the main MPD. It does not implement the scheme, so
  the stream's events reach no application handler; the foreign child is
  skipped with its subtree and no error is raised, because the schema
  admits foreign content there with lax processing. The rest of the
  `<Period>` parses as before and the primary content plays on, with no
  overlay opportunity taken.
- **Sibling check**: removal leaves an MPD whose Periods carry their
  media unchanged; no baseline sibling depends on it, and no attribute
  on `<Event>` or `<EventStream>` changes meaning by its presence.
- **Carrier class**: (a). **Namespace**: `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026`.

#### 4.7.2 `<svta:PauseAdPresentation>`

- **Placement**: child of `<Event>`, inside an `<EventStream>` whose
  `@schemeIdUri` is `urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-pause-trigger:2026`, in
  a `<Period>` of the main MPD.
- **Extension rule**: as §4.7.1 — DASH §5.2.1 open content under the
  DASH §5.10 per-scheme rule.
- **Walk-through**: identical up to the skip. What differs is
  afterwards: the trigger here is a viewer pause, and a client that
  skipped the window has no handler bound to it — the viewer pauses, the
  paused frame is shown, and resuming continues the primary content, the
  behaviour that client had before this specification existed.
- **Sibling check**: as §4.7.1; removal leaves the `<Event>`, the
  `<EventStream>` and the `<Period>` valid.
- **Carrier class**: (a). **Namespace**: `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026`.

#### 4.7.3 `<svta:OverlayList>`

- **Placement**: the only child of the single
  `<Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">` of an Overlay Resolution
  Document.
- **Extension rule**: DASH §5.2.1 open content on `<Period>`.
- **Walk-through**: a client reaches this document only by resolving a
  window it did not implement, so in practice it never requests it. One
  handed it anyway parses the envelope, removes the foreign element with
  its subtree, and is left with a zero-duration `<Period>` carrying no
  Adaptation Set — which the base specification admits at zero duration
  (DASH §5.3.2.2, Table 4). No error, and nothing to present.
- **Sibling check**: the zero duration is declared by the envelope
  rather than by this element, so removal leaves the envelope intact.
- **Carrier class**: (a). **Namespace**: `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026`.

#### 4.7.4 `<svta:Candidate>`

- **Placement**: child of `<svta:OverlayList>`, 0..n; zero children is
  the empty resolution.
- **Extension rule**: DASH §5.2.1, inside an extension parent.
- **Walk-through**: removed with its parent's subtree and never seen on
  its own. The baseline tracking `<EventStream>` nested inside it goes
  with it, which is the intent: removal is defined by namespace and
  takes what the removed element contains, so a baseline child authored
  **inside** an extension element is authored for clients that implement
  the namespace — and a beacon for an ad that is not being presented has
  nothing to report.
- **Sibling check**: removal leaves `<svta:OverlayList>` with fewer or
  no candidates, a shape the syntax already defines.
- **Carrier class**: (a). **Namespace**: `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026`.

#### 4.7.5 `<svta:RenderableAsset>`

- **Placement**: child of `<svta:Candidate>`, 1..n, in preference
  order.
- **Extension rule**: DASH §5.2.1, inside an extension parent.
- **Walk-through**: removed with the parent subtree. Its
  `<ImportedMPD>` child is a baseline element inside an extension
  element and goes with it, which is again the intent: a client that
  skipped the candidate has no slot in which to play the creative.
- **Sibling check**: removing one option leaves the rest in their
  declared order; removing all leaves a candidate the syntax rejects,
  which the 1..n cardinality and step 2 of §4.6 catch.
- **Carrier class**: (a). **Namespace**: `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026`.

#### 4.7.6 `<svta:BackgroundElement>`

- **Placement**: child of `<svta:RenderableAsset>`, 0..1, admissible
  where `@layout` is `squeezeback-double-box-with-background`.
- **Extension rule**: DASH §5.2.1, inside an extension parent.
- **Walk-through**: removed with the parent subtree.
- **Sibling check**: optional in its parent, and a composition
  attribute of the layout rather than a presentation option. Removal
  leaves a valid double-box option whose uncovered bands render black,
  the defined behaviour where the advertiser supplies no background.
- **Carrier class**: (a). **Namespace**: `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026`.

#### 4.7.7 `<svta:Click>` with `<svta:ClickTracking>`

- **Placement**: `<svta:Click>` is a child of `<svta:Candidate>`,
  0..1; `<svta:ClickTracking>` is its child, 0..n, carrying the
  tracking URL as element text content.
- **Extension rule**: DASH §5.2.1, inside an extension parent.
- **Walk-through**: removed with the parent subtree. On a linear slot,
  where the carrier sits on the `ListMPD` `<Period>`, the client removes
  it and plays the ad Period: the ad renders, no click is available, and
  no visible artefact is left.
- **Sibling check**: nothing in the presentation depends on it, and
  its absence is already a defined state — whether a ClickThrough
  exists is the advertiser's decision.
- **Carrier class**: (a). **Namespace**: `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026`.

#### 4.7.8 `<svta:AdSystem>`, `<svta:AdTitle>`, `<svta:Advertiser>`, `<svta:UniversalAdId>`

- **Placement**: children of `<svta:Candidate>`, 0..1 each, carrying
  their value as element text content.
- **Extension rule**: DASH §5.2.1, inside an extension parent.
- **Walk-through**: removed with the parent subtree. They are optional
  to emit and optional to read even for a conformant Player, so a
  client that ignores them — legacy or current — is conformant and the
  presentation is identical.
- **Sibling check**: nothing depends on them in either direction.
- **Carrier class**: (a). **Namespace**: `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026`.

#### 4.7.9 The two event scheme URIs

- **Placement**: the value of `@schemeIdUri` on an `<EventStream>` in
  a `<Period>` of the main MPD —
  `urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026` and
  `urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-pause-trigger:2026`.
- **Extension rule**: the per-scheme rule of DASH §5.10. A stream is
  dispatched to the application owning its scheme, and a client owning
  neither has nothing to dispatch to; the base specification takes no
  position on the use of a stream at all — *"This document does not
  provide any specific information on how to use Event Streams"* (DASH
  §5.10.2.1).
- **Walk-through**: the parser reads `<EventStream>`, a baseline element
  whose baseline attribute carries an unrecognised URI. The element
  parses, the scheme is not implemented, the events are not dispatched;
  no error, and the Period's media is untouched.
- **Sibling check**: the stream is a sibling of the Adaptation Sets in
  its `<Period>`, and removing it leaves them and their timing
  unchanged.
- **Carrier class**: (a), by the elements the stream carries; the URI
  itself is a value in a baseline attribute. **Namespace**: the
  `urn:svta:dash:event:*` URI space, year-pinned.

#### 4.7.10 The resolution-document profile URI

<!-- refine: v9-dash-conformance-audit.md#M3 -->
- **Placement**: a token in `MPD@profiles` of an Overlay Resolution
  Document — `urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026`.
- **Extension rule**: the base specification's own profile
  declaration — *"profiles with which an MPD complies are indicated in
  the MPD@profiles attribute as a comma-separated list of profile
  identifiers"* (DASH §8.1).
- **Walk-through**: a client that does not recognise the identifier
  reads the document as an MPD it has no profile claim for, and the
  envelope is a valid base MPD once the extension namespace is removed
  (§4.7.3). An externally defined `@profiles` URI **is** an
  Interoperability Point in the base specification's sense, and its
  owner *"is responsible to provide sufficient semantics on the
  restrictions and permission of this interoperability point"* (DASH
  §8.1); this specification is that owner, and those semantics are the
  rules stated here. The identifier asks nothing of a client: it names
  which family of resolution document this is, so that a conformant
  Player can tell a misrouted answer from an empty one (§4.5.6).
- **Sibling check**: removing the token leaves an MPD whose remaining
  profile declarations stand.
- **Carrier class**: (a), by the constructs it labels. **Namespace**:
  the `urn:svta:dash:profile:*` URI space, year-pinned.

#### 4.7.11 The request-type URN

- **Placement**: a token in `@includeInRequests` on a
  `<RequestParam>` under `urn:mpeg:dash:urlparam:2025` —
  `urn:svta:dash:request:sgai-resolution:2026`.
- **Extension rule**: the base specification's own extension row for
  that attribute, which admits *"a URN or tag URI, where the request
  type semantics is understood by the client and specified by the URN
  / tag URI owner"* and fixes what a client does with one it does not
  know: *"The client shall drop unknown URIs from the
  @includeInRequests and @includeInHeaders strings prior to processing
  them"* (DASH Table I.4).
- **Walk-through**: the parser reads `@includeInRequests`, drops the
  unknown token by that rule, and processes the rest. The
  `<RequestParam>` stays in force for whichever request types it still
  names, and where it names none no parameters are appended; nothing
  fails and the primary content is unaffected.
- **Sibling check**: dropping the token leaves the `<RequestParam>`
  and its `@queryTemplate` valid; the mechanism's own drop rule is
  what makes this legacy-safe, rather than an authoring convention.
- **Carrier class**: (a). **Namespace**: the
  `urn:svta:dash:request:*` URI space, year-pinned.

#### 4.7.12 The reserved capability parameters

- **Placement**: query parameters on the resolution request the Player
  issues — `sgaiVideoDecoders`, `sgaiImageOverlay`,
  `sgaiHtmlOverlay`. They appear in no MPD.
- **Extension rule**: none applies, because they are not MPD
  constructs. They are emitted only by a Player that implements this
  specification, so a Player that does not sends a request without them,
  which an APS answers with every parameter undetermined (§4.4).
- **Walk-through**: not applicable to a document parser; the equivalent
  check sits on the server side and is §4.4's — an APS answering with
  none of them present is the case a conformant APS supports.
- **Sibling check**: each parameter is independent of the others, and
  one the Player cannot populate is omitted rather than sent empty, so
  removing any of them leaves a request the APS answers.
- **Carrier class**: not an MPD construct. **Namespace**: the reserved
  parameter names of this specification; a Player's own parameter
  carries an `x-<vendor>-` prefix.

#### 4.7.13 Aggregated audit

| Construct | Placement | Extension rule | Walk-through | Sibling check | Legacy test | Namespace | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `<svta:OverlayPresentation>` | `<Event>` in the overlay `<EventStream>`, main MPD | DASH §5.2.1 + §5.10 per-scheme | §4.7.1 | Removal leaves a valid MPD | Skipped silently; primary content plays | `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026` | (a) OK |
| `<svta:PauseAdPresentation>` | `<Event>` in the pause-trigger `<EventStream>`, main MPD | DASH §5.2.1 + §5.10 per-scheme | §4.7.2 | Removal leaves a valid MPD | Skipped silently; a pause shows the paused frame | `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026` | (a) OK |
| `<svta:OverlayList>` | `<Period>` of the resolution document | DASH §5.2.1 | §4.7.3 | Zero-duration Period stays valid | Removed with subtree; no error | `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026` | (a) OK |
| `<svta:Candidate>` | `<svta:OverlayList>` | DASH §5.2.1 | §4.7.4 | Fewer or zero candidates is a defined shape | Removed with the parent subtree | `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026` | (a) OK |
| `<svta:RenderableAsset>` | `<svta:Candidate>` | DASH §5.2.1 | §4.7.5 | 1..n checked by validation | Removed with the parent subtree | `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026` | (a) OK |
| `<svta:BackgroundElement>` | `<svta:RenderableAsset>` | DASH §5.2.1 | §4.7.6 | Optional; bands render black without it | Removed with the parent subtree | `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026` | (a) OK |
| `<svta:Click>` + `<svta:ClickTracking>` | `<svta:Candidate>`; linear: the `ListMPD` `<Period>` | DASH §5.2.1 | §4.7.7 | Presentation does not depend on it | Removed; the ad renders, no click fires | `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026` | (a) OK |
| `<svta:AdSystem>`, `<svta:AdTitle>`, `<svta:Advertiser>`, `<svta:UniversalAdId>` | `<svta:Candidate>` | DASH §5.2.1 | §4.7.8 | Nothing depends on them | Removed; presentation identical | `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026` | (a) OK |
| Overlay + pause-trigger scheme URIs | `@schemeIdUri` on `<EventStream>` | DASH §5.10 per-scheme | §4.7.9 | Period media unchanged | Stream parsed, events not dispatched | `urn:svta:dash:event:*:2026` | (a) OK |
| Resolution-document profile URI | `MPD@profiles` of the resolution document | DASH §8.1 profile declaration | §4.7.10 | Other profile tokens stand | Unrecognised identifier; envelope still parses | `urn:svta:dash:profile:*:2026` | (a) OK |
| Request-type URN | `@includeInRequests` on `<RequestParam>` | DASH Table I.4 URN row, with its drop rule | §4.7.11 | `<RequestParam>` stays valid | Unknown URI dropped by the base rule | `urn:svta:dash:request:*:2026` | (a) OK |
