| Reserved capability parameters | Query parameters on the resolution request | Not an MPD construct | §4.7.12 | Each independent; omitted when unpopulated | Never emitted by a Player that predates this specification | Reserved names of this specification | n/a OK |

**What such a Player shows instead is the Publisher's choice, not a
property of the construct.** The invariant this audit asserts is the
silent skip. For live content the opportunity is an expected loss; for
on-demand content the Publisher MAY author a baseline linear break
alongside the non-linear construct, which that Player plays — a
baseline construct, and so outside the skip test above.

### 4.8 Justifications for what is introduced and what is not reused

The default is reuse. This section records, for every construct
introduced here, why an existing construct of the base standard could
not carry it; and for every base construct a reader would expect to
see reused, why it is not.

#### 4.8.1 What is reused unchanged

**The two alternative-MPD event elements** — `<InsertPresentation>`
and `<ReplacePresentation>` — carry the linear families as they stand,
with no extension and no new attribute, because the base standard
already defines the operation those families need: *"An alternative
Media Presentation is a presentation that replaces the main Media
Presentation at a certain point on the media timeline for a duration
of time. After the playback of the alternative Media Presentation is
complete, the playback is continued with the main Media Presentation.
A key use case for this is advertisement"* (DASH §5.16.1). Their
execution model, fallback chain, execution counter and cap semantics
are adopted rather than restated.

**The callback event scheme** carries the tracking beacons: a new
scheme would be a second way to say what
`urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015` already says, and the one property
needed beyond it — a de-duplication key — is obtained by narrowing an
existing optional attribute to required.

**The play-list metric** carries the pause-delivery measurement
(§4.5.16): its fields already separate a viewer pause from a stall and
mark the resume, so a metric of our own would measure what the base
standard measures.

#### 4.8.2 Why the new constructs could not be an existing one

The opportunity windows (`<svta:OverlayPresentation>`,
`<svta:PauseAdPresentation>`) are new because what they declare is not
the alternative-MPD operation. An alternative MPD replaces or
displaces the main presentation; an overlay is composited **over** a
main presentation that keeps playing, and a pause window declares a
region in which a viewer action — not the playhead — triggers a
resolution. Extending the alternative-MPD elements to mean both would
give one element two execution models, and a reader would have to
consult an attribute to learn which.

The resolution-document constructs (`<svta:OverlayList>`,
`<svta:Candidate>`, `<svta:RenderableAsset>`,
`<svta:BackgroundElement>`) are new because the base standard's
resolution document for an alternative MPD is a Media Presentation: a
`ListMPD` whose Periods carry media. A still image and an HTML
document have no Representation in the base standard, and the paths
that reach one are bound to the RFC 4337 registry (§4.4). There is
nothing on that axis to relax; there is something absent from it.

`<svta:Click>` and the four metadata elements are new because the base
standard carries no click destination and no creative metadata at all.
The callback scheme carries a time-scheduled beacon, which a click is
not: a click fires on a viewer action, at a time no schedule predicts.

#### 4.8.3 The supplementary-video / picture-in-picture descriptor — weighed, not taken

The base standard's supplementary video service is the closest
existing thing to compositing a second visual element over a primary
one: *"Supplementary video services (sometimes referred to as
picture-in-picture services) offer the ability to include a video with
a smaller spatial resolution within a video with a bigger spatial
resolution"* (DASH §5.8.5.16).

It carries no ad — the supplementary stream is content the
presentation already declares, with no address to resolve at runtime
and no candidate list. It carries no layout token, so the Publisher's
allowed-layouts declaration has nowhere to live. It carries no
non-video surface: a still image and an HTML document are not
Representations of a Preselection. And, decisively, the base standard
hands the composition back to the application — *"Potential
manipulation of the stream and the composition of the main video and
the supplementary video are out of the scope of the DASH client"*
(DASH §5.8.5.16). A layout token whose entire content is a composition
instruction, carried on a construct whose composition is out of scope,
would declare something nothing is obliged to perform.

#### 4.8.4 The spatial-relationship descriptor — weighed, not taken

The base standard's spatial relationship description expresses where a
piece of content sits relative to a whole, which reads like the
vocabulary a layout needs. It is ruled out twice.

First by placement: *"SRD information shall be contained exclusively
in these two MPD elements (AdaptationSet and SubRepresentation)"*
(DASH Annex H.1). A non-linear opportunity is declared on an `<Event>`
and resolved into a candidate; neither is one of those two elements.

Second, and decisively, by what reusing it would commit this
specification to. Carrying position in the manifest is the first step of
a layout system and the second is expressing the rest — sizing,
stacking, safe areas, responsive behaviour — which this specification
declines to build: spatial arrangement is delegated to HTML5 and CSS,
and where an ad sits inside its layout follows from the IAB ad type the
`@layout` token names.

#### 4.8.5 The nonlinear-playback annex — a precedent, not a carrier

The base standard's nonlinear-playback annex serves *"Interactive
Storyline content with MPEG-DASH"* (DASH Annex L.1), and its selection
window is declared exactly as this specification's pause window is:
*"attributes are used to indicate the start and the duration of the
selection window"* (DASH Annex L.3.4.2), on an `<Event>` on the
primary timeline, with the decision resolved off that timeline by
something other than the playhead.

That is the shape of the pause model, and it is cited here as
**precedent**: a timeline-anchored event declaring a window whose
decision is taken elsewhere is a construction the base standard
already sanctions, so the pause window is not a novel kind of event.

It is not the carrier. Its payload selects among storyline branches of
the presentation the author already composed; it names no external
resolution endpoint, no candidate, no creative and no layout. Reusing
the element and redefining its child would alter the semantics of a
pre-existing construct, which this specification does not do.

#### 4.8.6 The MPD fallback scheme — cited affirmatively, not reused

The base standard already establishes that **document order carries
preference**: *"If multiple URLs are provided, the content author
expresses the preferences of using one of those by the order with the
first one having the highest preference"* (DASH §5.11.3). The ordered
presentation options of this specification follow that convention,
which is why no ranking attribute is introduced — the convention
exists in the base standard and is adopted rather than re-invented.

The mechanism itself is not reused for the window-level fallback
chain, for two reasons of fit. Its granularity is the whole Media
Presentation — it offers alternative MPDs for the same content, not
alternative ad opportunities inside one — and its trigger is an error
on the presentation the client is already using. The chain of §4.5.6
is triggered by five conditions, one of which is a healthy `200`
carrying no candidates, and it selects among windows the Publisher
declared for the same stretch of timeline.

#### 4.8.7 `@selectionPriority` — weighed, not taken

The obvious candidate for expressing preference among presentation
options is the base standard's numeric priority: it *"specifies the
selection priority for the described data structures"*, and *"In the
absence of other information, higher numbers are the preferred
selection over lower numbers"* (DASH §5.3.7.2).

Two things rule it out. Its direction runs opposite to document order
— a higher number wins, while the first option wins — so a document
carrying both would express preference twice and invite the two to
disagree. And its own wording conditions it on there being no other
information; in a resolution document there is other information,
because document order is already the preference. Adding it would give
one fact two carriers, and the value of a second carrier is that it
can drift from the first.

#### 4.8.8 `Preselection` — weighed, not taken

Preselections *"define user experiences that can be selected by the
DASH Client"* (DASH §5.3.11.1), which sounds like a way to offer the
Player several presentation options.

The mismatch is what a Preselection selects **among**. It combines
media components into one experience, so each Preselection is a
composition of parts presented **together**. A candidate's presentation
options are alternatives: exactly one is presented, and the others exist
so that the candidate resolves on devices whose capability budget
differs. Encoding alternatives in a construct built for combinations
would need every alternative expressed as a separate Preselection plus a
rule saying which single one to take — which is the ordered list this
specification already has, carried in a construct that says the
opposite.

#### 4.8.9 The URL-parameter mechanism — used for scoping, not for the capability payload

The base standard's extended URL parameter mechanism is used here in
one direction only: the **Publisher's** own query parameters travel on
`<RequestParam>` under `urn:mpeg:dash:urlparam:2025` (§4.2), and
`@includeInRequests` is what scopes a declaration to the non-linear
resolution request, through the URN row whose drop rule makes an
unknown request type legacy-safe (§4.7.11).

It is **not** the carrier for the Player's capability parameters, for
two reasons that are independent and each sufficient. Its vocabulary
is session state rather than device capability: the annex introduces
its parameter space to express *"knowledge of the current state of the
player"* (DASH §I.4.1), which is what a player is doing rather than
what a device can render. And its template is authored by the content
author — the query string is built from *"one or more
$<ParamIdentifier>$ template identifiers, as listed in Table I.2"*
(DASH §I.2.2.2) — so what travels is fixed by whoever wrote the MPD. A
Player cannot add an axis to a template it did not author, and the
capability parameters exist precisely so that a Player may disclose an
axis no one asked it to disclose, and omit one it will not.

#### 4.8.10 The seek-blocking control — preserved on linear, excluded on non-linear

The base standard provides a control that blocks seeking over an
event's active area: *"If non-zero, the playhead may not move forward
from any point for which PHP < PRT to any point where PHP > EAP
without executing this event. In other words, the active area of this
event cannot be skipped during a seek operation"* (DASH §5.16.5.2).

**On an inherited linear break it is preserved unchanged.** The ad
occupies the timeline there, so the stretch the control protects is
the ad, and blocking the jump does what the control was built to do.

**On the non-linear families it is excluded, and the reason is what
the window spans.** A non-linear ad is composited over primary content
that keeps playing underneath it, so the region an overlay or pause
window covers is programme. Blocking a jump across it would oblige the
viewer to watch a stretch of the programme they chose to skip, rather
than to watch an ad: the mechanism would be doing something other than
what it exists for.

This is an exclusion with a reason, recorded so that it reads as one —
a construct simply absent from a specification says nothing about
whether anyone considered it.

## 5. Syntax

This chapter defines the constructs an implementation authors and
reads. Every attribute block is presented as a table; the prose around
the tables carries the reasoning — why this carrier, why this shape —
and every new construct states inline why an existing construct of the
base specification was not reused.

### 5.1 Ad opportunity declarations in the main MPD

Every ad opportunity is an `<Event>` inside an `<EventStream>` in the
main MPD. The `<EventStream>@schemeIdUri` identifies the slot family;
the `<Event>`'s scheme-specific child element carries the slot's
constraints.

| Slot family | `<EventStream>@schemeIdUri` | Child element of `<Event>` | Resolution document |
|---|---|---|---|
| Linear, insert | `urn:mpeg:dash:event:alternativeMPD:insert:2025` | `<InsertPresentation>` | `ListMPD` (§5.2.1) |
| Linear, replace | `urn:mpeg:dash:event:alternativeMPD:replace:2025` | `<ReplacePresentation>` | `ListMPD` (§5.2.1) |
| Overlay | `urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026` | `<svta:OverlayPresentation>` | Overlay Resolution Document (§5.2.2) |
| Pause | `urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-pause-trigger:2026` | `<svta:PauseAdPresentation>` | Overlay Resolution Document (§5.2.2) |

The `<Event>` carrier uses the baseline attributes of DASH §5.10 —
`@id`, `@presentationTime`, `@duration` — with the time base given by
`@timescale` on the parent `<EventStream>`.

<!-- refine: v9-detail-review.md#flag-2 -->
**What the main MPD declares in `MPD@profiles` is the Publisher's.**
The main MPD declares whatever profile its own media satisfies, and
this specification constrains none of that: carrying an opportunity
window of any family neither requires nor forbids a particular profile
identifier. The annexes therefore differ from one another, and each is
read against the profile it declares.

<!-- refine: v9-dash-conformance-audit.md#M9 -->
**The event machinery is reused, not extended.** `<EventStream>` with
`<Event>` is the base specification's authoring vehicle for
timeline-anchored signalling: *"Events are timed, i.e. each event
starts at a specific media presentation time and may have a
duration"* (DASH §5.10.1). It is already open to a specification such
as this one, because an `<Event>` *"may contain further XML elements
meaningful for a particular event scheme. These may be defined in this
document … or in some external namespace"* (DASH §5.10.2.1). This
specification adds scheme URIs and child elements, and changes nothing
about how an event stream is authored, parsed or dispatched.

**A Player that does not implement a scheme does not act on its
stream** (DASH §5.10.1) `[inferred]`, and independently of that, the
document stays conformant for it, because the base specification places
the obligation on the author: *"the MPD shall be authored such that,
after XML attributes or elements in the other namespaces than the DASH
namespace are removed, the result is a valid XML document formatted
according to that schema and that conforms to this document"* (DASH
§5.2.1).

#### 5.1.1 `<InsertPresentation>` — linear insertion, inherited

Reused verbatim from DASH §5.16.3. The Publisher declares an `<Event>`
whose `@presentationTime` marks a point on the primary timeline at
which the alternative presentation is inserted **without consuming any
of the primary timeline**; when it ends, primary content resumes from
where it paused.

| Attribute | Required | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `@uri` | yes | `xs:anyURI` | — | URL the Player resolves at or after the Earliest Resolution Time. Resolves to the APS endpoint. |
| `@maxDuration` | optional in the base schema; **required under this specification** | `xs:unsignedLong` | Infinity in the base specification | The slot cap, in parent `<EventStream>@timescale` units. An alternative presentation running past it terminates at the cap. |
| `@earliestResolutionTimeOffset` | no | `xs:unsignedLong` | 60 s in `@timescale` units | Offset subtracted from the event's `@presentationTime` to obtain the Earliest Resolution Time. |
| `@executeOnce` | no | `xs:boolean` | `false` | When `true`, the event executes at most once in the session. |
| `@noJump` | no | `xs:integer` | `0` | Seek-interaction control: *"If non-zero, the playhead may not move forward from any point for which PHP < PRT to any point where PHP > EAP without executing this event"* (DASH §5.16.5.2). |
| `@skipAfter` | no | `xs:duration` | `PT0S` | Skip control: the offset from the start of the alternative presentation to *"the moment the rest of that presentation may be skipped by the application in response to a user action"* (DASH §5.16.5.2). |
| `@serviceDescriptionId` | no | `xs:unsignedInt` | — | *"specifies the value of the @id attribute of a ServiceDescription element applicable to this Event and to any presentation initiated as a result of executing this Event"* (DASH §5.16.5.2). |

Children: `<SupplementalProperty>` (zero or more) and foreign-namespace
open content, both inherited.

> **On a cap that is absent, and on a cap of zero.** The base
> specification reads an absent `@maxDuration` as unbounded — *"If
> absent, the value is assumed to be infinity"* — and a cap of zero as
> an instruction not to fire: *"If the value of @maxDuration is zero,
> the event is not executed"* (DASH §5.16.5.2). The zero rule is
> inherited unchanged. The absent case is narrowed: a Publisher
> declares a real cap, and a Player reading a slot declaration that
> carries none presents no ads from it and continues with the primary
> content. This narrowing is **provisional and open with the working
> group**.

**Applicable to on-demand content only.** This event does not appear
when `MPD@type` is `dynamic` (DASH §5.16.3) `[inferred]`, and the
reason is what insertion does: it stops the primary timeline for the
length of the ad, and a timeline that can be stopped for an indefinite
period is an on-demand or pre-recorded one. A live break uses
`<ReplacePresentation>` (§5.1.2).

#### 5.1.2 `<ReplacePresentation>` — linear replacement, inherited

Reused verbatim from DASH §5.16.4. The `@presentationTime` and
`@duration` of the declaring `<Event>` mark a span of the primary
timeline that the alternative presentation **replaces**: *"the main
Media Presentation is not being output, but its media time progresses
at the same speed as the currently playing alternative Media
Presentation"* (DASH §5.16.1). On completion the Player resumes at the
position `@returnOffset` determines.

`AlternativeMPDReplaceEventType` extends `AlternativeMPDEventType`, so
every attribute of §5.1.1 applies unchanged, plus three that are
exclusive to this element:

| Attribute | Required | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `@returnOffset` | no | `xs:unsignedLong` | — | Offset determining the playhead position at which primary content resumes after the alternative presentation. |
| `@clip` | no | `xs:boolean` | `true` | Fixes which side of the slot the cap binds. See below. It *"shall not be present if the @maxDuration attribute is absent"* (DASH §5.16.4, Table 62); this specification makes `@maxDuration` required on the declaring event (§5.1.1), so the restriction is satisfied here by construction. <!-- refine: v9-dash-conformance-audit.md#M1 --> |
| `@startWithOffset` | no | `xs:boolean` | `false` | When `true`, a delayed alternative presentation skips into its own timeline by the elapsed delay, staying aligned with the wall clock. When `false`, it starts from its first frame. It *"does not apply to a live alternative presentation and shall not be used if the @clip attribute value is 'false'"* (DASH §5.16.4, Table 62), so the two are not independent choices: `@clip="false"` excludes `@startWithOffset="true"`. <!-- refine: v9-dash-conformance-audit.md#M1 --> |

**`@clip` is two-sided, and the two sides differ by every millisecond
of lateness.** With `@clip="true"` — the default — the cap bounds
*until when*: the presentation terminates at the scheduled end of the
slot, so a late execution is shortened by the delay and the primary
content resumes exactly where the Publisher scheduled it. With
`@clip="false"` the cap bounds *how long*: the presentation runs one
cap-length from its **actual** start, so a late start moves the end.
The attribute carries no duration of its own; it selects which end of
the interval the single declared cap fixes.

`<InsertPresentation>` carries none of the three attributes above.

#### 5.1.3 `<svta:OverlayPresentation>` — overlay window, new

Declares a non-linear overlay opportunity. The window is the parent
`<Event>`'s `@presentationTime` and `@duration`. The Player resolves
the slot's `@uri` at or after the Earliest Resolution Time, validates
the candidates against the declarations below, and composites the
chosen form on or alongside the primary content, which keeps playing —
except where the selected option's `@layout` is `linear`, which
replaces the primary content for the length of the option and resumes
it where it stopped (§5.3.7.3).

**Why a new element rather than an extension of the alternative-MPD
events.** The operation is composition; the base specification's ad
machinery is substitutive by definition. *"An alternative Media
Presentation is a presentation that replaces the main Media
Presentation at a certain point on the media timeline for a duration of
time. … A key use case for this is advertisement, where alternative
Media Presentation represents the inserted advertisement"* (DASH
§5.16.1).
Both of its schemes swap: insertion *"may be inserted in the media time
of the Main Presentation, i.e. time-shift the part of the Main
Presentation which is played after the alternative Media
Presentation"*, and replacement leaves *"the main Media Presentation …
not being output"* (DASH §5.16.1). An overlay puts two things on the
screen at once, and an attribute added to either element would
contradict the semantics its own clause states.

Three constructs elsewhere in the base specification come close enough
to be weighed, and each is rejected for a stated reason:

- **The supplementary video descriptor** (`urn:mpeg:dash:supv:2022`,
  DASH §5.8.5.16) is the nearest miss: it exists precisely to signal
  two video surfaces of different sizes in one experience —
  *"Supplementary video services (sometimes referred to as
  picture-in-picture services) offer the ability to include a video
  with a smaller spatial resolution within a video with a bigger
  spatial resolution."* It describes two Adaptation Sets of the **same**
  presentation, authored together, whereas a non-linear ad arrives at
  runtime in a separate document. It hands the composition back rather
  than specifying it: *"Potential manipulation of the stream and the
  composition of the main video and the supplementary video are out of
  the scope of the DASH client."* And it admits no non-video surface at
  all, while several of the layouts of §3.2 need an image or an HTML
  surface.
- **The Spatial Relationship Description** (`urn:mpeg:dash:srd:2014`,
  DASH Annex H) positions encoded video tracks in a shared coordinate
  system for tiling and region-of-interest selection, and is confined
  to two elements: *"SRD information shall be contained exclusively in
  these two MPD elements (AdaptationSet and SubRepresentation)"* (DASH
  §H.1). Reusing it would misuse the construct and build the parallel
  layout system this specification declines to build (§1.3).
- **The non-linear playback scheme**
  (`urn:mpeg:dash:nonlinearplayback:2020`, DASH Annex L) is, despite
  the name, not about non-linear ads: *"This Annex provides Nonlinear
  Playback capabilities, to serve Interactive Storyline content with
  MPEG-DASH"* (DASH §L.1). It is cited in §5.1.4 as a precedent for a
  construct's shape, not reused as a carrier.

**Placement.** `<svta:OverlayPresentation>` is a child of `<Event>`,
authorised by the clause quoted in §5.1 above (DASH §5.10.2.1). Exactly
one appears per `<Event>`.

| Attribute | Required | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `@uri` | yes | `xs:anyURI` | — | URL the Player resolves at or after the Earliest Resolution Time. Resolves to the APS; the response conforms to §5.2.2. |
| `@maxDuration` | yes | `xs:unsignedLong` | — | The slot cap, in parent `<EventStream>@timescale` units, enforced against the cumulative rendered length of the forms the slot presents. Name, units and termination semantics are those of `@maxDuration` in DASH §5.16.5.2. |
| `@allowedLayouts` | yes | whitespace-separated token list | — | The layout tokens this slot admits, each drawn from §3.2. The Player matches a candidate option's `@layout` against this list by exact token. |
| `@earliestResolutionTimeOffset` | no | `xs:unsignedLong` | 60 s in `@timescale` units | Offset subtracted from the event's `@presentationTime` to obtain the Earliest Resolution Time. Same name, type and semantics as on the linear events. |
| `@executeOnce` | no | `xs:boolean` | `false` | When `true`, the window yields at most one overlay presentation for the session. |
| `@serviceDescriptionId` | no | `xs:unsignedInt` | — | The `@id` of a `ServiceDescription` applicable to this event and to the ad presentation this window initiates. That presentation is composited alongside the primary content, or shown while playback is paused; it does not replace the primary Media Presentation, so the alternative-presentation scope the base definition is written against (DASH §5.16.1) does not travel with the attribute here. <!-- refine: v9-dash-conformance-audit.md#M8 --> |

The element carries no child elements: every slot constraint is an
attribute.

**Encoding note.** `@allowedLayouts` is a single attribute carrying a
whitespace-separated token list rather than a nested element with one
child per token, matching the encoding the base specification uses for
the attributes it types as `StringVectorType`, an XML Schema list type
and therefore whitespace-delimited. The two attributes it
comma-separates — `MPD@profiles` and `@codecs` — are both constrained
to IETF RFC 6381 productions, which is why they are not the model. The
element form is warranted only when each item carries its own
attributes or children, which a layout token does not.

**On concurrency.** No maximum-concurrency attribute is declared on the
slot. At most one non-linear form is active at any instant (§4.5.8), so
an attribute whose only admissible value is `1` would restate a fixed
rule, and any other value would be unenforceable.

```xml
<EventStream xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
             schemeIdUri="urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026"
             timescale="1000">
  <Event id="201" presentationTime="600000" duration="30000">
    <svta:OverlayPresentation uri="https://aps.example.com/decision/overlay"
                              maxDuration="30000"
                              allowedLayouts="overlay-corner overlay-lower-third"
                              earliestResolutionTimeOffset="60000"/>
  </Event>
</EventStream>
```

**Legacy behaviour.** A Player that does not implement
`urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026` does not act on the stream, and
the foreign-namespace element is removed with its subtree under DASH
§5.2.1. What remains is a valid MPD whose primary content plays
uninterrupted, and no baseline element such a Player needs sits inside
the SGAI element.

#### 5.1.4 `<svta:PauseAdPresentation>` — pause-trigger window, new

Declares a **pause-trigger window**: an interval of the primary
timeline during which a viewer pause permits a pause ad. Outside the
window, a pause permits none.

**The window declares *where*, not how long.** The parent `<Event>`'s
`@presentationTime` and `@duration` bound the region of the timeline in
which a pause qualifies. They say nothing about how long the ad is
shown, and nothing about how long the pause lasts — the viewer sets
that.

**The trigger is the viewer's pause, not the playhead.** Every event in
the base specification is scheduled against presentation time, and a
pause stops it: *"the media time and the corresponding playhead can
change, for example, if the media engine is instructed with seek or
pause operations"* (DASH §4.2). There is no playhead-triggered
processing while the client is in listen mode, and processing resumes
at the playhead position it was left at (DASH §5.16.2.2.5)
`[inferred]`.

That split is the base specification's own, and DASH Annex L is the
precedent: there too an event's presentation time and duration
*"attributes are used to indicate the start and the duration of the
selection window"* (DASH §L.3.4.2), while the decision that resolves it
happens off the timeline, on a viewer action.

| Attribute | Required | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `@uri` | yes | `xs:anyURI` | — | URL the Player resolves to obtain the pause-ad resolution document (§5.2.2). |
| `@maxDuration` | yes | `xs:unsignedLong` | — | Maximum display duration of **one** pause ad before automatic dismissal, in parent `<EventStream>@timescale` units. It does not bound the pause slot, whose length the viewer sets. |
| `@allowedLayouts` | yes | whitespace-separated token list | — | The pause surfaces this window admits: `pause-fullscreen`, `pause-partial`, or both. |
| `@earliestResolutionTimeOffset` | no | `xs:unsignedLong` | 60 s in `@timescale` units | Offset subtracted from the event's `@presentationTime` to obtain the Earliest Resolution Time. The Player MAY resolve speculatively then, or lazily at the moment of pause (§8.5). |
| `@executeOnce` | no | `xs:boolean` | `false` | When `true`, the window yields at most one pause ad for the session. Consumed when a pause ad **begins rendering**. |
| `@serviceDescriptionId` | no | `xs:unsignedInt` | — | The `@id` of a `ServiceDescription` applicable to this event and to the ad presentation this window initiates. That presentation is composited alongside the primary content, or shown while playback is paused; it does not replace the primary Media Presentation, so the alternative-presentation scope the base definition is written against (DASH §5.16.1) does not travel with the attribute here. <!-- refine: v9-dash-conformance-audit.md#M8 --> |

**The once-per-session bound reuses the baseline capability with the
render event substituted for the playhead event.** `@executeOnce` keeps
its baseline name, type and default because it is the same capability:
the counter increments when a presentation successfully starts, and a
failed attempt leaves it untouched — *"counter E.c has not been
incremented due to the failure, consequently if E.c = 0 the event can
still be executed in the future even if the value of @executeOnce is
\"true\""* (DASH §5.16.2.2.6, NOTE 3). Here the event the counter
observes is the first rendered frame of a pause ad rather than the
playhead crossing a presentation time; that a window is consumed once
and only on success is inherited.

Stating the attribute is what makes the window determinate: without it
the capability is either inert on a pause window or caps it at one
pause ad, and those two readings differ by every pause after the
first.

```xml
<EventStream xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
             schemeIdUri="urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-pause-trigger:2026"
             timescale="1000">
  <Event id="301" presentationTime="0" duration="2520000">
    <svta:PauseAdPresentation uri="https://aps.example.com/decision/pause"
                              maxDuration="20000"
                              allowedLayouts="pause-fullscreen pause-partial"
                              executeOnce="true"/>
  </Event>
</EventStream>
```

**Legacy behaviour.** A Player that does not implement
`urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-pause-trigger:2026` does not act on the
stream, and a viewer pause produces no ad and no side effect. The
window is invisible to it.

#### 5.1.5 Hybrid slots

A hybrid slot is a linear slot and an overlay window whose ranges
coincide: an ad that takes over the screen, with a second ad composited
on top of it. It introduces nothing. It is authored as **two windows of
two different families**, in two `<EventStream>` elements — one per
family, because the scheme URI identifies the family and §5.1.6 puts
one family in one stream. The linear window carries
`<InsertPresentation>` or `<ReplacePresentation>`; the overlay window
carries `<svta:OverlayPresentation>` at the same
`@presentationTime`.

The two are independent at authoring time and at runtime: the Player
resolves the two `@uri` values separately, selects from each resolution
document independently, and composes the results. No construct links
them — a Publisher wanting the overlay restricted during a take-over
expresses it through that window's own `@allowedLayouts`, and one
wanting no overlay there declares no overlay window at that
position.

#### 5.1.6 Overlapping windows of the same family

**One family, one stream.** All windows of one family in a `<Period>`
are authored in a single `<EventStream>`, per the base specification's
own rule: *"all Events of one type shall be clustered in one Event
Stream"* (DASH §5.10.2.1).

Two or more windows of one family whose ranges overlap in time form a
**fallback chain**: the first is the window to serve, the rest are
backups reached whenever an attempt produces no ad. No attribute
declares the chain — the overlap is the declaration (§4.5.6).

**The order is by presentation time, oldest first, and ties break by
position in the stream.** The base specification orders its execution
queue by presentation time and does not say what happens when two
windows carry the same one; position inside the `<EventStream>` is this
specification's tie-break, consistent with the dispatch convention that
processes active events *"in the order they appear in the EventStream
element"* (DASH §5.10.2.1).

**Considered and not reused.** `BaseURL` alternatives offer alternative
locations for the **same** resource — origin-level robustness rather
than a chain of two opportunities. The MPD fallback scheme
`urn:mpeg:dash:fallback:2016` (DASH §5.11.3) chains whole presentations
on an unrecoverable playout error; its granularity is a presentation,
not an opportunity window. Its ordering convention is cited
affirmatively in §5.3.5.

### 5.2 Resolution documents

#### 5.2.1 Linear `ListMPD`

Reused from DASH §8.14: *"List MPDs shall have the value of the
MPD@type attribute set to \"list\". 2) List MPD shall be identified by
the URN \"urn:mpeg:dash:profile:list:2024\". This URN shall appear in
the MPD@profiles attribute"*. Each `<Period>` is one ad in the break,
played back-to-back in declared order: a playlist of ads the ADS
already chose and ordered, not a candidate set the Player selects
from.

**`MPD` attributes on a `ListMPD`:**

| Attribute | Required | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `@profiles` | yes | comma-separated list of `xs:anyURI` | — | Includes `urn:mpeg:dash:profile:list:2024`, per DASH §8.14 rule 2. |
| `@type` | yes | enum (`static` \| `dynamic` \| `list`) | `static` | `list` on this document, per DASH §8.14 rule 1. |
| `@minBufferTime` | yes | `xs:duration` | — | Baseline MPD attribute. |
| `@publishTime` | yes | `xs:dateTime` | — | The instant the APS produced the document. |

**Per-ad `<Period>` attributes:**

| Attribute | Required | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `@id` | yes | `xs:string` | — | Period identifier, unique within the document. |
| `@duration` | yes | `xs:duration` | — | Declared length of this ad, ISO 8601. The Player uses it for drop-before-play cap evaluation (§4.5.4) without fetching the sub-MPD first. |

**Per-ad `<Period>` children:**

| Child | Required | Cardinality | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `<ImportedMPD>` | yes, in the shape this specification uses | 1..1 | The sub-MPD carrying this ad's media and tracking (§5.4). The URL is the element's text content. |

`<ImportedMPD>` is reused verbatim from DASH §5.3.2.6: *"Linked Periods
are Periods which import their content from an external MPD, referred
to as imported MPD. Linked Periods are indicated by the ImportedMPD
element in a Period element. MPDs referenced in the ImportedMPD element
shall be restricted to the constraints of a single period profile as
defined in 8.15."* Its single attribute:

| Attribute | Required | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `@earliestResolutionTimeOffset` | no | `xs:double` | `60.0` | Seconds before this Period's start at which the Player MAY pre-fetch the sub-MPD, smoothing CDN load. **Seconds**, unlike the same-named attribute on a slot event — see §5.3.4. |

```xml
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:list:2024"
     type="list"
     minBufferTime="PT1S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:00:00Z">
  <BaseURL>https://ads.example.com/delivery/</BaseURL>
  <Period id="ad-01" duration="PT15S">
    <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="0">creative-101.mpd</ImportedMPD>
  </Period>
  <Period id="ad-02" duration="PT30S">
    <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="15">creative-102.mpd</ImportedMPD>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

**Three profile rules bound the reuse**, and each has a consequence
here:

- *"List MPDs shall not contain XLink attributes defined in subclause
  5.5"* (DASH §8.14, rule 3). Remote resolution inside a `ListMPD`
  therefore goes through `<ImportedMPD>` and nowhere else.
- *"List MPDs shall not contain Alternative MPD events"* (DASH §8.14,
  rule 5). A resolution document cannot declare a nested linear
  opportunity inside itself, so every ad opportunity of every family is
  signalled from the main MPD.
<!-- refine: v9-spec-validation.md#F9 -->
- The List profile is an extension of the ISO-BMFF CMAF profile (DASH
  §8.14), so an inline `<AdaptationSet>` on a `ListMPD`
  Period — a shape the profile admits, since *"it may also contain
  regular Periods"* (DASH §8.14, rule 4) — inherits the media-type
  constraint of §4.4. The `<ImportedMPD>` shape is the one used here,
  because it keeps each creative's metadata sharded per ad.

#### 5.2.2 Overlay Resolution Document — non-linear, new

The APS answers an overlay or pause slot's resolution request with an
**Overlay Resolution Document**: an MPD whose `@profiles` includes
`urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026`, carrying the candidate
list in the extension namespace inside one zero-duration `<Period>`.

**Why an MPD carrying the candidate list in the extension namespace,
and not a `ListMPD`.** Most of the `ListMPD` shape is reused: the
document is an MPD, declared order is the order of presentation, and
video creatives are still reached through `<ImportedMPD>` into a
sub-MPD bound to the Single-Period Static profile. What a `ListMPD`
cannot express is the one construct the non-linear case needs. Its
Periods are **a sequence to play**, not **alternatives to choose
among**, and the Player renders them all. A non-linear candidate offers
an ordered list of presentation options of which the Player renders
exactly one — the first its device and the window can satisfy (§5.3.5)
— and no construct in the base specification carries that meaning. The
candidate list is therefore carried in the extension namespace, inside
a document that is otherwise a conformant MPD.

**Why the single Period declares zero duration.** A Period with no
Adaptation Set is conformant only at zero duration: *"At least one
Adaptation Set shall be present in each Period unless the value of the
@duration attribute of the Period is set to zero"* (DASH §5.3.2.2,
Table 4). An Overlay Resolution Document carries no Adaptation Set —
each candidate's media is reached through its own presentation options
— so `PT0S` is what makes it conformant, and it states the truth: the
Period presents nothing and is the anchor the candidate list hangs
from. `<Period>` admits foreign-namespace children under DASH §5.2.1,
so the placement is conformant and under a canonical anchor.

**On the name.** "Overlay" in *Overlay Resolution Document*, in the
profile URI and in the root element `<svta:OverlayList>` names the
**non-linear family as a whole** — overlay and pause-trigger windows
alike — and not the `overlay` layout token of §3.2, which names one
spatial arrangement. A pause-ad document is an Overlay Resolution
Document and declares that profile URI.

**Why `static` and not `list`.** `MPD@type="list"` is available: DASH
§5.3.1.4 defines it in the main body, independently of the List
profile, and confers exactly two properties — *"For Media Presentations
with MPD@type set to \"list\" the constraints of a static Media
Presentation shall apply"* and *"MPDs of this type shall not use MPD
assembly as defined in 5.5, i.e. the attributes from namespace @xlink
shall not be present"*. Declaring it would not enrol the document in
the List profile, and this specification says so plainly rather than
closing the option on a premise the clause does not support. It
declines the value as a **design decision**: every other mention of
`list` binds it to Linked Periods and to the alternative-MPD flow, and
an Overlay Resolution Document is reached by neither. `static` is
declared directly, and the absence of XLink is a property of the
documents this specification defines.

Skeleton:

```xml
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT0S"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:00:00Z">
  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
    <svta:OverlayList>
      <svta:Candidate id="cand-1" duration="PT10S">
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image" layout="overlay-corner"
                              assetUrl="https://cdn.example.com/a.png"/>
        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0" id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=1</Event>
        </EventStream>
        <svta:Click clickThroughUrl="https://advertiser.example.com/landing">
          <svta:ClickTracking>https://tracker.example.com/click?ad=1</svta:ClickTracking>
        </svta:Click>
      </svta:Candidate>
    </svta:OverlayList>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

<!-- refine: v9-spec-validation.md#F9 -->
**What declaring a profile URI of this specification's own costs.** A
profile-conformance check modifies the MPD before checking it, and the
modification removes every extension-namespace element the declared
profile does not explicitly include (DASH §8.1). An
Overlay Resolution Document is therefore checked, for its own profile,
with its SGAI constructs gone. That leaves the document conforming —
DASH §5.2.1 guarantees what remains is valid — and fixes what the
profile URI is for: **it declares that a document carries these
constructs; it does not certify them.**

<!-- refine: v9-spec-validation.md#F9 -->
Certification would require an Interoperability Point that explicitly
included the extension namespace, and the base specification puts the
cost on whoever publishes it: the owner of such a URI is responsible
for providing sufficient semantics on its restrictions and permissions
(DASH §8.1) — authoring the restrictions, publishing the
URI, getting implementers to declare it, and maintaining all three
across editions. This specification has not taken that decision, so an
Overlay Resolution Document's conformance rests on this specification's
own rules and on the base schema rather than on a profile a third-party
validator already knows how to enforce.

<!-- refine: v9-dash-conformance-audit.md#M2 -->
**Which of the two conformance tests is being claimed.** DASH §8.1
defines both. An MPD conforms to a profile when the profile-specific
MPD validates and satisfies the profile's restrictions; a Media
Presentation conforms when, additionally, *"There is at least one
Representation in each Period in the profile-specific MPD for ProfA"*.
Every resolution document this specification defines — the Overlay
Resolution Document here, and both empty shapes of §5.2.3 — claims the
**first** test and not the second: a Period of zero duration carries no
Representation, by design. These documents are answers to a resolution
request rather than presentations to play, so the MPD-level test is the
one that carries the claim, and adding a Representation nobody would
render would buy nothing.

**`MPD` attributes on an Overlay Resolution Document:**

| Attribute | Required | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `@profiles` | yes | comma-separated list of `xs:anyURI` | — | Includes `urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026`. DASH §8.1 declares the value as *"a comma-separated list of profile identifiers"*, each identifier itself comma-free. |
| `@type` | yes | enum (`static` \| `dynamic` \| `list`) | `static` | `static` on this document, for the reason above. |
| `@minBufferTime` | yes | `xs:duration` | — | `PT0S` on this document, which buffers nothing itself. |
| `@mediaPresentationDuration` | yes | `xs:duration` | — | `PT0S`, consistent with the single zero-duration Period. The base specification makes the attribute conditional; this specification declares it unconditionally, which adds a constraint rather than relaxing one. |
| `@publishTime` | yes | `xs:dateTime` | — | The instant the APS produced the document. |

**`<svta:OverlayList>` — root of the candidate list.** Child of the
single `<Period>`.

| Attribute | Required | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `@onCandidatesExhausted` | no | enum (`repeat` \| `request-again` \| `stop`) | `stop` | What the Player does when the candidates run out while the viewer is still paused (§4.5.11). Inert on an overlay document, where the slot ends with its window. |

| Enum value | Description |
|---|---|
| `repeat` | Present the candidate sequence again from the start, for as long as the pause lasts. |
| `request-again` | Request a new resolution document for the same pause. A document carrying no candidates is then `stop` for the remainder of that pause. |
| `stop` | Present no further ad; the paused primary frame is shown. |

| Child | Required | Cardinality | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `<svta:Candidate>` | no | 0..n | The ads offered for the slot, in the order the ADS decided. Zero children is the empty resolution of §5.2.3. |

The exhaustion behaviour is declared by the APS because it is a
property of the answer rather than of the opportunity: it says what to
do with *these* candidates when they run out. `stop` is the default
because it is what the viewer would see if the mechanism did not exist,
and it is the behaviour every Player can perform.

**`<svta:Candidate>` — one ad offered for the slot.**

| Attribute | Required | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `@id` | yes | `xs:string` | — | Candidate identifier, unique within the document. Also the de-duplication scope for beacons (§5.5.4). |
| `@duration` | yes | `xs:duration` | — | The candidate's declared duration on the presentation timeline, ISO 8601. The Player uses it for drop-before-play cap evaluation (§4.5.4) and as the basis for the derived wall-clock on-screen length (§4.5.12). |

| Child | Required | Cardinality | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `<svta:RenderableAsset>` | yes | 1..n | The presentation options, ordered; document order is the preference order (§5.3.5). |
| `<EventStream>` of the callback scheme | no | 0..1 | The candidate's tracking schedule (§5.5). |
| `<svta:Click>` | no | 0..1 | The candidate's ClickThrough and its click-tracking (§5.6). |
| `<svta:AdSystem>`, `<svta:AdTitle>`, `<svta:Advertiser>`, `<svta:UniversalAdId>` | no | 0..1 each | Application-level metadata (§5.7). |

#### 5.2.3 The empty resolution document

An opportunity that resolved and produced **no ads** is expressed as a
resolution document carrying no candidates, served with HTTP `200` and
a parseable body — not a `204`, not a `404`, not an error status. The
distinction is between *a document that says nothing was sold* and *no
document at all*.

What follows at the Player is §4.5.6: the attempt produced no ad, so it
is a failed execution and the next overlapping window of the family is
attempted; where there is none, the primary content continues.
Expressing it as a document keeps an unfilled opportunity reportable as
unfilled and keeps one status code from meaning two things.

**Non-linear** — the document of §5.2.2 with an empty
`<svta:OverlayList>`:

```xml
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT0S"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:00:00Z">
  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
    <svta:OverlayList/>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

**Linear** — a `ListMPD` whose merge yields no available media, carried
as a single zero-duration Period with no `<ImportedMPD>`:

```xml
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:list:2024"
     type="list"
     minBufferTime="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:00:00Z">
  <Period id="no-fill" duration="PT0S"/>
</MPD>
```

<!-- refine: v9-spec-validation.md#F9 -->
Both shapes follow from the same pair of schema constraints: a document
carrying zero Periods does not validate, because `<Period>` has an
omitted `minOccurs` that defaults to `1` (DASH Annex B),
and a Period of non-zero duration needs an Adaptation Set *"unless the
value of the @duration attribute of the Period is set to zero"* (DASH
§5.3.2.2, Table 4). The answer is therefore the same shape for both
families: one Period of zero duration, carrying nothing to present.

### 5.3 `<svta:RenderableAsset>` — the presentation option

A `<svta:RenderableAsset>` is **one** renderable presentation option on
a candidate: a creative-carrier form paired with a layout. A candidate
carries its options as an ordered list of these elements, and the
Player renders exactly one of them.

**Why a new construct.** No construct in the base specification lets a
single ad candidate offer an ordered list of alternative presentations
and have the client render the first it can satisfy. Two express
ordered preference and are scoped elsewhere. `Preselection` (DASH
§5.3.11.1) elements *"define user experiences that can be selected by
the DASH Client"*, but a Preselection combines media components into
one jointly rendered experience rather than offering alternatives to
choose between. The MPD fallback scheme (DASH §5.11.3)
states this specification's ordering rule almost verbatim, but at the
granularity of whole MPD URLs and triggered by an unrecoverable playout
error rather than by a capability check. The third,
`@selectionPriority`, is weighed in §5.3.5.

#### 5.3.1 Attributes

| Attribute | Required | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `@form` | yes | enum (`video` \| `image` \| `html`) | — | The creative-carrier form (§3.3). Value space in §5.3.2. |
| `@layout` | yes | token | — | The layout, drawn from the enumeration of §3.2. The Player matches it against the slot's `@allowedLayouts` by exact token. Value space in §5.3.3. |
| `@assetUrl` | conditional | `xs:anyURI` | — | The creative's URL when `@form` is `image` or `html`. Absent when `@form` is `video`, where the creative is reached through the `<ImportedMPD>` child (§5.3.4). |

| Child | Required | Cardinality | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `<ImportedMPD>` | yes, when `@form` is `video` | 0..1 | The sub-MPD carrying the video creative (§5.3.4). |
| `<svta:BackgroundElement>` | yes, when `@layout` is `squeezeback-double-box-with-background` | 0..1 | The advertiser's background image (§5.3.7.2). Admissible on that layout only. |

#### 5.3.2 Enum: `@form`

| Enum value | Description |
|---|---|
| `video` | ISO-BMFF video, reached through the `<ImportedMPD>` child of this element (§5.3.4). Representations inside the sub-MPD carry `video/mp4`, `audio/mp4` or `application/mp4`. |
| `image` | Still image at `@assetUrl`. Concrete media types: `image/jpeg`, `image/png`, `image/webp`. |
| `html` | HTML document at `@assetUrl`. Concrete media type: `text/html`. Inline `<script>` MAY appear and runs under the device's HTML capability contract; the script is not a separate carrier. |

**Why the non-audiovisual asset URL rides on this element.** The media
axis is closed to non-MP4 media types along the whole resolution path
(§4.4): a document reached through `<ImportedMPD>` is *"restricted to
the constraints of a single period profile as defined in 8.15"* (DASH
§5.3.2.6.1), and under that profile *"The @mimeType attribute of each
Representation shall be provided according to IETF RFC 4337"* (DASH
§7.3.1). Four carriers remain open, and this specification uses the
first:

| Carrier | Clause | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **(a)** Foreign-namespace open content: the URL as an attribute on an element of this specification | DASH §5.2.1 | **Selected.** The option is one named element whose attributes bind form, layout and creative together, and whose position among its siblings is the preference order. |
| (b) An application-level `<Event>` whose text content carries the payload | DASH §5.10 | Rejected. The carrier's defining property is presentation-time alignment, and the asset URL is a one-fetch static value with no presentation time of its own. Using it would put the creative's address on a timeline it has no position on. |
| (c1) A `SupplementalProperty` whose `@value` carries the URL | DASH §5.8.4.9 | Rejected — see below. Its legacy cost is the lighter of the two: when the scheme or value is unrecognised *"the DASH Client is expected to ignore the descriptor"*, leaving the parent element intact. |
| (c2) An `EssentialProperty` whose `@value` carries the URL | DASH §5.8.4.8 | Rejected — see below, and its legacy cost is heavier: when the scheme or value is unrecognised *"the DASH Client is expected to ignore the parent element that contains the descriptor"*. The parent goes with it. |

**Why neither descriptor, and it is not placement.** A descriptor
hosted on an `<Event>`, an `<EventStream>` or an alternative-MPD event
element inherits **no** media-type constraint (§4.4), so the
descriptor axis is genuinely open here and a rejection resting on the
media-type registry would rest on a constraint that is not present at
that position.

The reason is what a descriptor can carry. A descriptor offers one
scheme URI and one value string, and a presentation option is three
inseparable facts — the form, the layout, and the creative that
realises them — plus, on one layout, a background element with a URL of
its own. Encoding that tuple into a single delimited value would invent
a micro-syntax inside an attribute where an element with named
attributes says the same thing in the schema, and it would leave the
pieces of one option where nothing binds them together as an option.

The ordered list needs the option to be an **element** for a second and
independent reason: order among elements is the preference order
(§5.3.5), whereas order among descriptors on a host element carries no
such convention in the base specification.

(c1) and (c2) are kept apart because their legacy costs differ, and
choosing between them is choosing what a client that does not implement
this specification loses. Neither is chosen: **no construct in this
specification uses `EssentialProperty` or `SupplementalProperty` as its
carrier.**

#### 5.3.3 Enum: `@layout`

The admissible values are exactly the tokens below, which map 1:1 onto
IAB-defined ad types and visual placements; this specification mints
none of them and accepts nothing outside this list.

| Enum value | Description |
|---|---|
| `linear` | Full-screen takeover of the primary content surface. On a non-linear slot it is the option of last resort; inside a linear slot it is the only form. |
| `overlay` | Plain image or HTML overlay with no named placement. |
| `overlay-corner` | Corner overlay. |
| `overlay-lower-third` | Lower-third overlay. |
| `squeezeback-l-shape` | L-shape: one full-frame ad creative with the shrunk primary content composited on top (§5.3.7.1). |
| `squeezeback-double-box` | Two boxes; the uncovered bands render as black (§5.3.7.2). |
| `squeezeback-double-box-with-background` | Two boxes plus an advertiser background image filling the uncovered bands (§5.3.7.2). |
| `pause-fullscreen` | Pause ad occupying the whole screen surface. |
| `pause-partial` | Pause ad composited over the paused primary frame, which stays visible underneath. |

Which of these are admissible **on a given slot** is the Publisher's
declaration in `@allowedLayouts`. An option whose `@layout` is absent
from that list fails the check, and the Player moves on to the next
option in document order (§4.5.3).

#### 5.3.4 `<ImportedMPD>` child, for a video form

When `@form` is `video` the creative is carried through an
`<ImportedMPD>` child, reused verbatim from DASH §5.3.2.6. The sub-MPD
URL is the element's **text content**, not an attribute.

| Attribute | Required | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `@earliestResolutionTimeOffset` | no | `xs:double` | `60.0` | Seconds before the option's start at which the Player MAY pre-fetch the sub-MPD. |

> **The unit trap, stated explicitly.**
> `@earliestResolutionTimeOffset` appears in two contexts with
> different unit bases, both inherited and both kept. On a slot event
> (§5.1.1 to §5.1.4) it is `xs:unsignedLong` in the parent
> `<EventStream>@timescale` units — `60000` at `timescale="1000"` is
> sixty seconds. On `<ImportedMPD>` it is `xs:double` in **seconds** —
> `60.0` is sixty seconds. The same name and the same intent carry two
> unit bases, and the parent element is what determines which.
> Authoring tools SHOULD surface the parent element alongside the
> value.

<!-- refine: v9-spec-validation.md#F9 -->
The element admits foreign-namespace **attributes** and no
foreign-namespace **child elements** (DASH §5.3.2.6.2), so
an extension that needs to travel on an `<ImportedMPD>` travels as an
attribute.

The child is a core-namespace element inside a foreign-namespace
parent, and that nesting is deliberate: it makes the creative opaque to
a Player that predates this specification (§5.3.6), which has no slot
to play it in. The reference processing model that combines an imported
MPD into a Linked Period does not apply here, because there is no
Period at this position; this specification supplies the resolution
behaviour itself (§4.6).

#### 5.3.5 Document order is the preference order

The order of the `<svta:RenderableAsset>` children inside a
`<svta:Candidate>` is the preference order: the Player renders the
first option whose form and layout its device can satisfy and whose
layout the window admits (§4.5.3).

**The convention is the base specification's own, and it is cited
affirmatively.** In its fallback scheme, *"If multiple URLs are
provided, the content author expresses the preferences of using one of
those by the order with the first one having the highest preference"*
(DASH §5.11.3). Expressing preference as document order is therefore
consistency with the base specification rather than a departure from
it.

**A ranking attribute is deliberately absent, and the construct weighed
and rejected is `@selectionPriority`.** The base specification
*"specifies the selection priority for the described data structures,
i.e. the one described by the containing element. In the absence of
other information, higher numbers are the preferred selection over
lower numbers"* (DASH §5.3.7.2). It runs in the **opposite** direction
from document order — higher wins, where first wins here — it is a hint
on a media-level type rather than on an ad-level choice, and it is
explicitly conditional on there being no other information, which on a
candidate there always is. Carrying both would put two orderings in one
element with no rule for which governs when they disagree.

A candidate carrying several options resolves on devices the ADS and
the APS know nothing about, which is what lets one decision serve a
heterogeneous population. Carrying exactly one is equally conformant
and leaves the choice with the APS; the Player-visible interface is
identical either way, and nothing in the document distinguishes "the
APS narrowed the list" from "this is all there was".

#### 5.3.6 Legacy behaviour and the required-sibling check

`<svta:RenderableAsset>` appears only inside a `<svta:Candidate>`,
inside a `<svta:OverlayList>`, inside a resolution document that a
Player predating this specification never requests — the window that
would have triggered the request was not acted on at the main-MPD level
(§5.1.3, §5.1.4). Parsed by such a Player regardless, the document
loses `<svta:OverlayList>` with its whole subtree and is left a valid
MPD carrying one zero-duration Period, which is what DASH §5.2.1
obliges the author to guarantee.

**The authoring lever: a baseline element nested inside an extension
element carries no legacy guarantee.** Removal is by namespace, and
removing an element removes what it contains. A baseline element placed
as a **sibling** of an SGAI element stays within reach of a Player that
predates this specification; one placed **inside** an SGAI element does
not. That is an authoring choice, not an accident: it is why the
`<ImportedMPD>` of §5.3.4 is nested, and why the tracking
`<EventStream>` inside a `<svta:Candidate>` (§5.5.2) is authored for
Players that implement this specification. No baseline element that a
legacy Player needs sits inside an SGAI element anywhere here.

#### 5.3.7 Layout composition

Spatial arrangement inside a layout is delegated to HTML5 and CSS and
to the IAB-defined layout the token names. **This specification
declares no positioning vocabulary**: no coordinates, no dimensions, no
anchors, no z-order attribute. What it fixes is which elements a layout
puts on screen and what each of them costs; where they sit inside the
frame is the IAB layout's and the renderer's.

##### 5.3.7.1 The L-shape

An L-shape presentation option carries exactly **one** ad creative — a
single URL carrying an image, a video or an HTML creative — and that
creative is placed full-frame in the background. The shrunk primary
content is composited **on top of** it, in one region of the screen;
the "L" is the band of the background creative that stays visible
around the shrunk primary content, commonly the side and the bottom.

The layout therefore puts **two** elements on screen: the full-frame ad
creative and the shrunk primary content over it. There is no third
element — the ad creative already covers the whole frame, so the region
around the shrunk primary content *is* the ad creative, which matches
the IAB squeezeback model, where assets are supplied in an underlay
format: a full-frame branded creative with a cutout for the content.

The shrunk primary content is not a creative the ADS or the APS
supplies; it is the main content the Player shrinks. An L-shape is a
presentation option like any other.

##### 5.3.7.2 The double box, and the double box with background

In a double-box layout the shrunk primary content and the ad are
composed as two on-screen boxes that leave bands uncovered. A
**background element** MAY fill those bands; where none is present they
render as black.

A candidate offering the double box **without** a background uses the
`squeezeback-double-box` token and carries no `<svta:BackgroundElement>`.
A candidate offering it **with** one uses
`squeezeback-double-box-with-background` and carries exactly one.

`<svta:BackgroundElement>` is a child of the `<svta:RenderableAsset>`
whose `@layout` is `squeezeback-double-box-with-background`:

| Attribute | Required | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `@assetUrl` | yes | `xs:anyURI` | — | URL of the advertiser's background image. Concrete media types as for `image` in §5.3.2. |

The background element is **always a still image** — never video and
never HTML — so it never consumes a video decoder. It is the
advertiser's creative, mirroring the IAB "Double Box Video +
Background" model.

**It is a composition attribute of the layout, not a presentation
option.** The Player does not walk it the way it walks the ordered
options of §5.3.5; it composites it as part of rendering the layout,
once that layout has been chosen. Modelling it as an option would put
it in a list meaning "render exactly one of these".

##### 5.3.7.3 The decoder-and-surface budget

The number and type of concurrent elements a layout puts on screen
determine which device classes can composite it. This table is what
makes a layout satisfiable or not on a device class: the Player
evaluates it in §4.5.3, and an APS holding capability parameters in
§5.8.2. Every layout-and-form pair has a row, so a Player of any class
has a defined outcome for every opportunity, and selects only an option
whose row admits its class.

| Layout | Form of the ad creative | Video decoders | Non-video surfaces | Satisfiable on |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `overlay`, `overlay-corner`, `overlay-lower-third` | `video` | 2 (primary + ad) | none | D1, D2 |
| `overlay`, `overlay-corner`, `overlay-lower-third` | `image` | 1 (primary) | image over video | D1, D3, D4 |
| `overlay`, `overlay-corner`, `overlay-lower-third` | `html` | 1 (primary) | HTML over video | D1, D3 |
| `squeezeback-l-shape` | `video` | 2 (full-frame creative + shrunk primary) | none | D1, D2 |
| `squeezeback-l-shape` | `image` | 1 (shrunk primary) | image surface for the full-frame creative | D1, D3, D4 |
| `squeezeback-l-shape` | `html` | 1 (shrunk primary) | HTML surface for the full-frame creative | D1, D3 |
| `squeezeback-double-box` | `video` | 2 (primary + ad) | none | D1, D2 |
| `squeezeback-double-box` | `image` | 1 (primary) | image surface for the ad | D1, D3, D4 |
| `squeezeback-double-box` | `html` | 1 (primary) | HTML surface for the ad | D1, D3 |
| `squeezeback-double-box-with-background` | `video` | 2 (primary + ad) | image surface for the background | D1 |
| `squeezeback-double-box-with-background` | `image` | 1 (primary) | image surface for the ad **and** image surface for the background | D1, D3, D4 |
| `squeezeback-double-box-with-background` | `html` | 1 (primary) | HTML surface for the ad **and** image surface for the background | D1, D3 |
| `pause-fullscreen` | `video` | 1, re-tasked from the paused primary content once its resources are released | none | D1..D5 <!-- refine: v9-spec-validation.md#T1 --> |
| `pause-fullscreen` | `image` | 0 in use for the ad; the paused frame may be released | image surface | D1, D3, D4 |
| `pause-fullscreen` | `html` | 0 in use for the ad; the paused frame may be released | HTML surface | D1, D3 |
| `pause-partial` | `video` | 1 (paused primary holds its frame) + 1 for the ad | none | D1, D2 |
| `pause-partial` | `image` | 1 (paused primary) | image over the paused frame | D1, D3, D4 |
| `pause-partial` | `html` | 1 (paused primary) | HTML over the paused frame | D1, D3 |
| `linear` (full-screen takeover) | `video` | 1, reused sequentially across ad and primary content | none | D1, D2, D3, D4, D5 |

Three readings are worth making explicit, because they are where the
outcome stops being obvious:

- **D2 owns two decoders and still declines the double box with
  background.** The blocker is the background element, an image surface
  D2 does not composite. The rule is element **type**, not element
  **count**.
- **Two rows are satisfiable on every class**, and for the same
  reason: neither needs concurrent composition. The full-screen
  takeover reuses one decoder sequentially across ad and primary
  content; the full-screen pause ad re-tasks the decoder the paused
  frame was holding. That is what makes each of them the useful last
  option on a candidate's ordered list.

<!-- refine: v9-spec-validation.md#T5 -->
A full-screen pause ad replaces the whole surface, so the Player MAY
release the primary content's resources (§7.6) and the decoder holding
the paused frame becomes free. No table row therefore rests on a
device-dependent re-tasking capability, and the `pause-fullscreen`
`video` row carries no conditional: it is satisfiable on every class,
which is what §3.4, §7.6 and Annex E already state.

### 5.4 Sub-MPD (Single-Period Static profile)

Every `<ImportedMPD>` — from a `ListMPD` Period (§5.2.1) or from a
video presentation option (§5.3.4) — points at a sub-MPD bound to the
Single-Period Static profile of DASH §8.15.

**Every video creative travels this way, and the binding is structural
rather than chosen.** A document reached through `<ImportedMPD>` is
*"restricted to the constraints of a single period profile as defined
in 8.15"* (DASH §5.3.2.6.1), and that profile is the base
specification's own home for an ad creative: the single-period static
MPDs *"correspond to common practices of storing individual
advertisement creatives"* (DASH §8.15). Routing video through it reuses
an existing interoperability surface, which is why this specification
defines no creative container of its own.

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| `@profiles` | includes `urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024` |
| `@type` | `static` |
| `@mediaPresentationDuration` | absent; the Period carries `@duration` instead |
| `@availabilityStartTime` | absent |
| `<Period>` | exactly one, carrying `@duration` |
| `<AdaptationSet>` / `<Representation>` | the ad's media; every Representation's `@mimeType` from the RFC 4337 registry, since *"The @mimeType attribute of each Representation shall be provided according to IETF RFC 4337"* (DASH §7.3.1) |
| `<EventStream>` of the callback scheme | optional, inside the `<Period>`; the ad's tracking schedule (§5.5.2) |

<!-- refine: v9-spec-validation.md#F9 -->
The profile admits no MPD-level `Metrics` and no MPD-level
`SupplementalProperty` (DASH §8.15.2). So the play-list
metric request lives on the main MPD (§5.9), and any descriptor a
sub-MPD carries sits inside the `<Period>`.

`@codecs` values in every MPD this specification defines follow RFC
6381, which makes the codec identifier case-sensitive and recommends
lowercase hexadecimal: `avc1.4d401f`.

**Reconciling the two declared durations.** The same ad is described by
a duration twice: the candidate's `@duration` (or the `ListMPD`
Period's) in the parent document, and the sub-MPD's `Period@duration`.
**The sub-MPD's `Period@duration` is canonical** — it describes the
media that exists, and travels with the creative wherever it is used.
The parent document's value is **derived from it** by the APS when the
resolution document is produced, and it is the value the Player reads
for drop-before-play cap evaluation (§4.5.4), because it is available
without a second fetch.

Duplicating a value across two documents invites silent drift, so the
consequence of a disagreement is fixed: the cap is enforced against
**actual rendered length** whatever either document declared. A stale
parent value changes what the Player can predict before playing, and
not what it enforces while playing.

### 5.5 Tracking carrier

Timeline-scheduled beacons — impression, start, quartiles, complete —
ride the callback event scheme of DASH §5.10.4.5. **This specification
mints no tracking scheme.** The callback scheme is already a beacon
carrier, down to discarding what comes back: *"the client is expected
to entirely ignore the response"* (DASH §5.10.4.5.1). Nothing in it
needed extending for ads, and a parallel scheme would have split
tracking across two carriers for no semantic gain.

#### 5.5.1 Carrier shape

```xml
<EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
             value="1" timescale="1000">
  <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=1</Event>
  <Event presentationTime="2500"  id="2">https://tracker.example.com/firstQuartile?ad=1</Event>
</EventStream>
```

`<EventStream>` attributes:

| Attribute | Required | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `@schemeIdUri` | yes | `xs:anyURI` | — | `urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015` for every tracking carrier under this specification. |
| `@value` | yes | `xs:string` | — | `1`, the value the base specification fixes for this scheme (DASH §5.10.4.5.3, Table 47). <!-- refine: v9-spec-validation.md#F9 --> |
| `@timescale` | yes | `xs:unsignedInt` | — | Time base for `<Event>@presentationTime`. `1000`, giving millisecond resolution, is the value used throughout this document. |

`<Event>` attributes:

| Attribute | Required | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `@presentationTime` | yes | `xs:unsignedLong` | `0` in the base schema | When the Player fires the beacon, in `@timescale` units, measured from the start of **this ad's** presentation (§5.5.3). |
| `@id` | yes | `xs:unsignedLong` | — | Identifier of the beacon. Optional in the base schema; **required here**, because de-duplication is keyed on it (§5.5.4) and an absent key makes the scope of §5.5.4 undecidable for that beacon. |

<!-- refine: v9-spec-validation.md#F9 -->
The beacon URL is the element's **text content**. It is carried there
and not in `@messageData`, which the 6th-edition schema prohibits and
deprecates in favour of carrying the message in the value space of the
event (DASH §5.10.2.3).

Which beacons exist, how many, and where they sit are the ADS's. This
specification fixes the carrier and the timebase; the Player executes
the schedule the document carries.

#### 5.5.2 Where the carrier lives

| Ad | Position of the tracking `<EventStream>` |
|---|---|
| Linear ad, video creative | Inside the sub-MPD's `<Period>`, one per ad |
| Non-linear candidate, `video` form | Inside the form's sub-MPD `<Period>`, **or** directly inside the `<svta:Candidate>` |
| Non-linear candidate, `image` or `html` form | Directly inside the `<svta:Candidate>`; there is no sub-MPD to host it |

Where both positions carry one, the Player fires the union, reduced by
§5.5.4.

**The consequence of the second position.** An `<EventStream>` inside a
`<svta:Candidate>` does not sit in a `<Period>`, so there is no Period
timeline for its `@presentationTime` values to resolve against: they
resolve against **that candidate's own presentation** instead (§5.5.3).
Two further consequences are stated because a reader arrives at neither
by default. The element sits outside the base schema at that position,
so the attribute constraints above are not enforced by schema
validation there while the identical carrier inside a sub-MPD
`<Period>` is fully validated; a Player conformant to this
specification validates those attributes itself. And tooling that
locates callback event streams by scanning `<Period>` children alone
misses every carrier attached to a non-video candidate, so tooling
implementing this specification scans inside `<svta:Candidate>` too,
and a validation report states which of the two it did.

<!-- refine: v9-dash-conformance-audit.md#M6 -->
A third consequence follows from the same position. The base
specification's clustering rule — *"all Events of one type shall be
clustered in one Event Stream"* (DASH §5.10.2.1) — is scoped to Event
Streams the base schema reaches, and it does not reach this one. A
resolution document carrying four candidates carries four streams of
the identical scheme / value pair, which inside a `<Period>` would
violate the rule and here does not, because after the removal of
§5.2.1 none of them is there. What stands in place of the guarantee
the rule would have carried is the per-candidate de-duplication scope
of §5.5.4.

The placement is admissible under the base specification's authoring
obligation — what remains after the extension namespace is removed is a
valid, conformant document (DASH §5.2.1) — and it is the authoring
lever of §5.3.6 in use: this carrier is authored for Players that
implement this specification.

#### 5.5.3 The timebase

Every `<Event>@presentationTime` inside a resolution document is
expressed **relative to the start of the ad's own presentation** —
never to the primary timeline, never to wall-clock. The Player adds the
ad's start position on the primary timeline to each relative value to
obtain the firing point.

A worked quartile schedule for a 10-second ad at `timescale="1000"`:

| Beacon | `@presentationTime` | Fires at |
|---|---|---|
| impression, start | `0` | the ad's first rendered frame |
| firstQuartile | `2500` | 25 % of the ad's presentation |
| midpoint | `5000` | 50 % |
| thirdQuartile | `7500` | 75 % |
| complete | `10000` | the ad's last frame |

An `image` or `html` form has no segment-aligned media to anchor the
origin, so the Player establishes the ad's presentation timeline at the
moment the asset becomes visible: offset `0` is the first rendered
frame of the image or of the HTML document.

Beacon scheduling runs on the presentation timeline, so primary content
playing at a speed other than 1× moves the wall-clock instants at which
beacons fire without changing their scheduled presentation times
(§4.5.12).

#### 5.5.4 De-duplication

De-duplication is **scoped to the candidate**. Within one
`<svta:Candidate>`, beacons sharing an `@id`, and beacons carrying the
same URL at the same `@presentationTime`, fire once. Across candidates
the scope does not reach: the same `@id` appearing in two candidates of
one resolution document is two distinct beacons, and the Player fires
both.

The scope is the candidate and not the document because a beacon
identifies an event in **one ad's** presentation. Widening it would
suppress the second impression of two ads that happened to share an
identifier, which an APS assembling a document from several upstream
decisions has no way to prevent.

### 5.6 ClickThrough carrier

The resolution document carries the ad's ClickThrough URL and its
click-tracking URLs in a single carrier, so that a Player conformant to
this specification reads them the same way.

**Why the callback scheme is right for impressions and quartiles and
wrong for a click.** A callback event fires at a **scheduled
presentation time**; a click activation has none, because it happens
when the viewer acts or never. Putting the click on the callback scheme
would give the activation a position on a timeline it has no position
on.

**The base specification is not silent about user action, and this
carrier follows what is there.** `@skipAfter` declares an offset after
which the application may skip the rest of an alternative presentation
*"in response to a user action"* (DASH §5.16.5.2) — a user action with
a normative consequence, declared in the document and resolved when the
viewer acts. That is the pattern followed here.

#### 5.6.1 `<svta:Click>`

Child of `<svta:Candidate>` on a non-linear slot, and foreign-namespace
open content on the `ListMPD` `<Period>` of the ad it belongs to on a
linear one.

<!-- refine: v9-dash-conformance-audit.md#M5 -->
**On a `ListMPD` `<Period>` the base schema fixes the position.**
`PeriodType` opens its sequence with `<ImportedMPD>` and closes it with
`<xs:any namespace="##other" processContents="lax"/>`, so a
foreign-namespace `<svta:Click>` is schema-valid only when authored
**after** every DASH-namespace child of that `<Period>` — not before
the `<ImportedMPD>` naming the ad it belongs to, which is the position
a reader might otherwise choose:

```xml
<Period id="ad-1" duration="PT15S">
  <ImportedMPD>https://adcdn.example.com/901/spot.mpd</ImportedMPD>
```
