   `@includeInRequests="altmpd"` scopes it to alternative-MPD requests (§5.8).
4. The APS answers `200` with the `ListMPD` of §A.3, which carries one period, so
   the resolution succeeded (§6.1).
5. The Player converts the candidate's declared duration into the cap's
   timescale: `PT20S → 20 000 ms`, already a whole unit. Cumulative duration is
   `0 + 20 000 = 20 000 ms`, and `20 000 ≤ 30 000`, so the ad is admitted (§4.5).
6. It fetches `creative-2201.mpd`, renders the creative full-viewport, and fires
   the six beacons at ad-local 0, 0, 5 000, 10 000, 15 000 and 20 000 ms.
7. The rendered length reaches 20 000 ms, which is 10 000 ms short of the cap, so
   the break ends when the ad ends rather than at the cap.
8. Because the opportunity is an insertion, the main timeline paused at media
   time 0 and resumes from where it paused: the programme starts at its first
   frame. The session lasts 20 s of ad plus 45 minutes of programme; no primary
   content was consumed by the break.
9. `executeOnce="true"` means that replaying the opening of the programme inside
   the same session does not produce a second pre-roll.

### A.6 Per-device-class behaviour

The outcome is identical on all five device classes D1 to D5 (§3.4). A linear ad
is a full-viewport takeover played in sequence with the primary content, never
alongside it, so it needs no second video decoder and no image or HTML surface
over video: a single decoder is released by the primary content, used for the
ad, and reused for the programme. The capability axes that separate the classes
— concurrent decoders, image overlay, HTML overlay — are not exercised, so the
Player on D5 reaches the same selection, the same cap arithmetic and the same
beacon schedule as the Player on D1.

## Annex B — Mid-roll (linear)

### B.1 The scenario

A viewer is watching a live channel. The Publisher has declared an ad
opportunity at media time 30:00, lasting 30 seconds, filled by one ad that takes
over the viewport. Because the content is live, the break is authored as a
**replacement**: the primary content is not output during the ad, but its media
time keeps advancing underneath, so the channel stays anchored to its schedule.
DASH §5.16.1 states that during a replacement "the main Media Presentation is not
being output, but its media time progresses at the same speed as the currently
playing alternative Media Presentation."

The scenario also shows what happens when the ad starts late: the presentation is
clipped so that it still ends at the scheduled end of the break.

### B.2 What the Publisher declares

<!-- refine: v9-dash-conformance-audit.md#N1 -->
```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:advanced-linear:2025"
     type="dynamic"
     availabilityStartTime="2026-09-18T11:00:00Z"
     minimumUpdatePeriod="PT2S"
     timeShiftBufferDepth="PT1M"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T11:29:00Z">

  <RequestParam includeInRequests="altmpd"
                queryTemplate="session_id=$urn:mpeg:dash:state:cmcd#sid$"/>

  <Period id="main" start="PT0S">

    <!-- The mid-roll opportunity (§5.1). -->
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:alternativeMPD:replace:2025"
                 timescale="1000">
      <Event id="201" presentationTime="1800000" duration="30000">
        <ReplacePresentation uri="https://ads.example.com/aps/midroll"
                             maxDuration="30000"
                             earliestResolutionTimeOffset="60000"
                             returnOffset="30000"
                             clip="true"
                             startWithOffset="false"/>
      </Event>
    </EventStream>

    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video"
                   segmentAlignment="true" startWithSAP="1">
      <SegmentTemplate timescale="1000" duration="2000"
                       initialization="https://cdn.example.com/live/init.mp4"
                       media="https://cdn.example.com/live/seg_$Number$.m4s"
                       startNumber="1"/>
      <Representation id="v1" mimeType="video/mp4" codecs="avc1.4d401f"
                      bandwidth="3000000" width="1280" height="720"/>
    </AdaptationSet>

  </Period>
</MPD>
```

`@returnOffset`, `@clip` and `@startWithOffset` are available on
`<ReplacePresentation>` only; the insertion construct of Annex A carries none of
them.

### B.3 What the APS returns

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:list:2024"
     type="list"
     minBufferTime="PT1S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T11:29:12Z">

  <BaseURL>https://cdn.example.com/creatives/</BaseURL>

  <Period id="ad-11" duration="PT30S">
    <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="0">creative-3310.mpd</ImportedMPD>
  </Period>

</MPD>
```

### B.4 The sub-MPD

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T11:29:12Z">

  <Period id="1" duration="PT30S">

    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                 value="1" timescale="1000">
      <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=3310</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="0"     id="2">https://tracker.example.com/start?ad=3310</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="7500"  id="3">https://tracker.example.com/firstQuartile?ad=3310</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="15000" id="4">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=3310</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="22500" id="5">https://tracker.example.com/thirdQuartile?ad=3310</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="30000" id="6">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=3310</Event>
    </EventStream>

    <AdaptationSet contentType="video"
                   segmentAlignment="true" startWithSAP="1">
      <Representation id="v1" mimeType="video/mp4" codecs="avc1.4d401f"
                      bandwidth="3000000" width="1280" height="720">
        <BaseURL>media/creative-3310.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-1104"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>

  </Period>
</MPD>
```

### B.5 The walk-through

1. The Player finds event `201` at presentation time 1 800 000 ms (30:00) with a
   cap of 30 000 ms.
2. Earliest Resolution Time:
   `ERT = 1 800 000 − 60 000 = 1 740 000 ms`, media time 29:00. The Player issues
   the resolution request at an instant of its choosing inside
   `[1 740 000, 1 800 000)`; here it resolves at 1 752 000 ms.
3. The APS answers `200` with the `ListMPD` of §B.3.
4. Cap check: `PT30S → 30 000 ms`, and `30 000 ≤ 30 000`. A converted duration
   equal to the cap is admitted (§4.5), so the ad is accepted.
5. The scheduled break span on the primary timeline is
   `[1 800 000, 1 800 000 + 30 000) = [1 800 000, 1 830 000)`.
6. A rebuffer delays the first ad frame to 1 806 000 ms, 6 000 ms into the break.
   `clip="true"` keeps the scheduled end fixed, so the ad is shortened rather
   than pushed out: rendered length
   `1 830 000 − 1 806 000 = 24 000 ms` of the creative's 30 000 ms.
   `startWithOffset="false"` means the creative still starts at its first frame,
   so ad-local time runs from 0 to 24 000 ms.
7. The Player fires every beacon whose ad-local time falls inside the rendered
   span: 0, 0, 7 500, 15 000 and 22 500 ms. The `complete` beacon is scheduled at
   30 000 ms, past the clip boundary, so it stays unfired.
8. Main media time advanced underneath the ad for the whole break. The playhead
   resumes at `1 800 000 + 30 000 = 1 830 000 ms`, the live position the channel
   reached while the ad was on screen `[inferred]` — the quoted material
   available for this build covers the media-time progression of DASH §5.16.1 but
   not the arithmetic of `@returnOffset` in DASH §5.16.5.2.
9. Had the Publisher authored `clip="false"`, the same late start would have
   produced the opposite shape: the presentation would run a full cap length from
   its actual start, ending at `1 806 000 + 30 000 = 1 836 000 ms`, moving the end
   of the break instead of shortening the ad (§6.1).

### B.6 Per-device-class behaviour

As in Annex A, the outcome is the same on D1 to D5. The replacement ad occupies
the whole viewport in sequence with the primary content, so it needs neither a
second video decoder nor an overlay surface, and the three capability axes that
separate the classes stay unexercised. On the single-decoder classes D3, D4 and
D5 the decoder is released by the live stream, used for the creative, and
reacquired for the live stream at the resumption point; a device with a second
decoder may pre-buffer the creative while the last primary frames play, which
changes nothing a viewer or an advertiser can observe.


## Annex C — Coexisting overlay (multi-form, multi-layout)

### C.1 The scenario

A live channel carries a forty-second stretch, starting ten minutes into the
programme, in which the Publisher allows an ad to be composited over the primary
content without interrupting it. The primary content keeps playing throughout:
whatever the slot presents is drawn on top of it, or around it, and then goes
away. The Publisher restricts the slot to three placements — a corner overlay, a
lower third, and an L-shape squeezeback — and caps the cumulative rendered length
at twenty seconds.

The APS returns two candidates. The first carries three presentation options
spanning three forms and three layouts; the second carries two. Neither candidate
offers a full-screen takeover, because the Publisher did not admit the `linear`
token for this slot. That single authoring choice is what makes the annex
interesting: a device with no overlay surface and one video decoder has nothing
to render here, and the annex follows that case through to its end.

### C.2 What the Publisher declares

The main MPD carries one `<EventStream>` for the overlay family, holding every
window of that family in the Period, and one primary-content Adaptation Set. The
base specification requires the clustering: *"all Events of one type shall be
clustered in one Event Stream"* (DASH §5.10.2.1).

The Event Stream's `@timescale` is 25, the frame rate of the primary content, so
every number the Publisher writes for this family — the window position, the
window length, the resolution offset and the cap — is a whole number of frames.

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     xsi:schemaLocation="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011 DASH-MPD.xsd"
     type="dynamic"
     availabilityStartTime="2026-09-18T09:00:00Z"
     minimumUpdatePeriod="PT2S"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:00:00Z"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:advanced-linear:2025">

  <Period id="1" start="PT0S">

    <!-- Overlay opportunity family. timescale=25 → one unit is one frame. -->
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026"
                 timescale="25">
      <!-- Window opens at 600 s (15000 frames), 40 s long (1000 frames). -->
      <Event id="201" presentationTime="15000" duration="1000">
        <svta:OverlayPresentation
            uri="https://aps.example.com/resolve/overlay?slot=201"
            maxDuration="500"
            allowedLayouts="overlay-corner overlay-lower-third squeezeback-l-shape"
            earliestResolutionTimeOffset="1500"
            executeOnce="false"/>
      </Event>
    </EventStream>

    <!-- Primary content -->
    <AdaptationSet id="1" mimeType="video/mp4" codecs="avc1.4d401f"
                   segmentAlignment="true" startWithSAP="1"
                   frameRate="25">
      <SegmentTemplate timescale="90000" duration="180000"
                       initialization="video/init.mp4"
                       media="video/seg_$Number$.m4s"
                       startNumber="1"/>
      <Representation id="v1" mimeType="video/mp4" bandwidth="2500000"
                      width="1280" height="720"/>
    </AdaptationSet>

  </Period>
</MPD>
```

What the declaration fixes:

- **Where.** The window runs from frame 15000 (600 s) for 1000 frames (40 s).
- **Which placements.** `@allowedLayouts` admits exactly three tokens from the
  layout enumeration of §3.2. An option whose `@layout` is one of those three is
  eligible for this slot; an option carrying any other token belongs to some
  other slot.
- **How long in total.** `@maxDuration="500"` is 500 frames, twenty seconds, and
  it bounds the cumulative rendered length of the forms this slot presents.
- **When to resolve.** `@earliestResolutionTimeOffset="1500"` puts the Earliest
  Resolution Time at frame 13500, sixty seconds ahead of the window. The Player
  resolves at or after that instant.
- **How often.** `@executeOnce="false"` leaves the window available each time the
  media time is traversed.

The `@uri` resolves to the APS. When the Player issues that request it MAY attach
the reserved capability parameters described in §5.8; the APS in this annex
receives none of them and returns the same document to every device, which is why
the whole of the device-awareness sits at the Player.

A Player that has never heard of this specification removes the foreign-namespace
content and is left with a valid, conformant manifest, so the primary content
plays through the window uninterrupted: *"the MPD shall be authored such that,
after XML attributes or elements in the other namespaces than the DASH namespace
are removed, the result is a valid XML document formatted according to that
schema and that conforms to this document"* (DASH §5.2.1).

### C.3 What the APS returns

The APS answers the resolution request with an Overlay Resolution Document: an
MPD under the profile of §5.2, `@type="static"`, with one Period of zero duration
whose only child is the candidate list. The zero duration is what makes a Period
carrying no Adaptation Set conformant — *"At least one Adaptation Set shall be
present in each Period unless the value of the `@duration` attribute of the
Period is set to zero"* (DASH §5.3.2.2, Table 4).

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT0S"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:09:12Z">

  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
    <svta:OverlayList>

      <!-- Candidate A: three forms, three layouts, in preference order. -->
      <svta:Candidate id="cand-a" duration="PT12.5S">

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="video" layout="squeezeback-l-shape">
          <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="15.0"
            >https://ads.example.com/creatives/a-lshape.mpd</ImportedMPD>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="html" layout="overlay-corner"
                              assetUrl="https://ads.example.com/creatives/a-corner.html"/>

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image" layout="overlay-lower-third"
                              assetUrl="https://ads.example.com/creatives/a-lower-third.png"/>

        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=a</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="2">https://tracker.example.com/start?ad=a</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="3125"  id="3">https://tracker.example.com/firstQuartile?ad=a</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="6250"  id="4">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=a</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="9375"  id="5">https://tracker.example.com/thirdQuartile?ad=a</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="12500" id="6">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=a</Event>
        </EventStream>

        <svta:Click clickThroughUrl="https://advertiser.example.com/campaign-a">
          <svta:ClickTracking>https://tracker.example.com/click?ad=a</svta:ClickTracking>
        </svta:Click>

        <svta:AdSystem>example-ads</svta:AdSystem>
        <svta:AdTitle>Campaign A — autumn</svta:AdTitle>
        <svta:Advertiser>Advertiser A</svta:Advertiser>
        <svta:UniversalAdId>example.com/AA-2026-0417</svta:UniversalAdId>
      </svta:Candidate>

      <!-- Candidate B: two forms, two layouts. -->
      <svta:Candidate id="cand-b" duration="PT7.48S">

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="video" layout="squeezeback-l-shape">
          <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="15.0"
            >https://ads.example.com/creatives/b-lshape.mpd</ImportedMPD>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image" layout="overlay-corner"
                              assetUrl="https://ads.example.com/creatives/b-corner.jpg"/>

        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"    id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=b</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="1870" id="2">https://tracker.example.com/firstQuartile?ad=b</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="3740" id="3">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=b</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="5610" id="4">https://tracker.example.com/thirdQuartile?ad=b</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="7480" id="5">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=b</Event>
        </EventStream>

        <svta:AdSystem>example-ads</svta:AdSystem>
        <svta:AdTitle>Campaign B — teaser</svta:AdTitle>
      </svta:Candidate>

    </svta:OverlayList>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

Three things about the document are worth naming.

**Document order is the preference order.** Neither `<svta:Candidate>` nor
`<svta:RenderableAsset>` carries a ranking attribute; the sequence in the
document is the ranking, which follows the base specification's own convention
for ordered alternatives — *"If multiple URLs are provided, the content author
expresses the preferences of using one of those by the order with the first one
having the highest preference"* (DASH §5.11.3).

**`@assetUrl` and `<ImportedMPD>` are alternatives, not companions.** The two
`video` options reach their creative through `<ImportedMPD>` and carry no
`@assetUrl`; the `image` and `html` options carry `@assetUrl` and no
`<ImportedMPD>`. The Linked-Period machinery is the base specification's:
*"Linked Periods are Periods which import their content from an external MPD,
referred to as imported MPD. Linked Periods are indicated by the ImportedMPD
element in a Period element"* (DASH §5.3.2.6.1).

**Beacon times belong to the candidate.** Each `<EventStream>` of the callback
scheme sits inside its own `<svta:Candidate>` and its `@presentationTime` values
are measured from the start of that candidate's own presentation, in
milliseconds. Candidate A and candidate B both use `@id` values 1..5; those are
two distinct sets of beacons and all of them fire, because de-duplication is
scoped to the candidate. Candidate B carries no `<svta:Click>`: the ADS supplied
no ClickThrough for that ad, and the element is simply absent.

### C.4 The sub-MPDs

Each `video` option imports a Single-Period Static sub-MPD: `@type="static"`,
the profile URN in `@profiles`, one `<Period>` carrying `@duration`, no
`@mediaPresentationDuration`, no `@availabilityStartTime`, and `@mimeType` on
every Representation — *"The `@mimeType` attribute of each Representation shall
be provided according to IETF RFC 4337"* (DASH §7.3.1).

Candidate A's L-shape creative, `a-lshape.mpd`:

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance"
     xsi:schemaLocation="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011 DASH-MPD.xsd"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:00:00Z">

  <Period id="1" duration="PT12.5S">
    <AdaptationSet mimeType="video/mp4" codecs="avc1.4d401f"
                   segmentAlignment="true" startWithSAP="1">
      <Representation id="v1" mimeType="video/mp4" bandwidth="2500000"
                      width="1280" height="720">
        <BaseURL>https://ads.example.com/media/a-lshape.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-912"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
    <AdaptationSet mimeType="audio/mp4" codecs="mp4a.40.2" lang="en">
      <Representation id="a1" mimeType="audio/mp4" bandwidth="128000"
                      audioSamplingRate="48000">
        <BaseURL>https://ads.example.com/media/a-lshape-audio.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-708"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

Candidate B's L-shape creative, `b-lshape.mpd`:

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance"
     xsi:schemaLocation="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011 DASH-MPD.xsd"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:00:00Z">

  <Period id="1" duration="PT7.48S">
    <AdaptationSet mimeType="video/mp4" codecs="avc1.4d401f"
                   segmentAlignment="true" startWithSAP="1">
      <Representation id="v1" mimeType="video/mp4" bandwidth="2200000"
                      width="1280" height="720">
        <BaseURL>https://ads.example.com/media/b-lshape.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-884"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

The tracking schedule is absent from both sub-MPDs. For the non-linear families
the beacons ride inside the candidate, where the Player that implements this
specification reads them, and the sub-MPD stays a plain creative.

### C.5 The Player's decision walk-through

Every option is checked twice: against the slot's `@allowedLayouts`, and against
the device budget of §3.4. All five options in this document carry a layout the
slot admits, so the layout check passes everywhere and the device check is what
separates the classes.

The budget each option consumes, stated once:

| Option | Form | Layout | Needs | Satisfiable on |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A-1 | `video` | `squeezeback-l-shape` | one decoder for the shrunk primary content plus a second for the full-frame creative | D1, D2 |
| A-2 | `html` | `overlay-corner` | one decoder plus an HTML surface over video | D1, D3 |
| A-3 | `image` | `overlay-lower-third` | one decoder plus an image surface over video | D1, D3, D4 |
| B-1 | `video` | `squeezeback-l-shape` | two decoders, as A-1 | D1, D2 |
| B-2 | `image` | `overlay-corner` | one decoder plus an image surface over video | D1, D3, D4 |

#### C.5.1 D1 — two or more decoders, image on video, HTML on video

- **Candidate A.** Option A-1 is the first in document order. The L-shape shrinks
  the primary content onto one decoder and puts the full-frame video creative on
  a second; D1 has both. Layout `squeezeback-l-shape` is in
  `@allowedLayouts`. A-1 wins, and A-2 and A-3 are never examined.
- **Candidate B.** Option B-1 for the same reason. B-1 wins.
- **What the viewer sees.** The primary content shrinks into the corner of an
  advertiser's full-frame video for 12.5 s, returns to full frame, then shrinks
  again into the second advertiser's L-shape for 7.48 s.

The two candidates are presented in sequence: candidate B starts when candidate A
ends. At no instant are both on screen, because at most one non-linear ad form is
active at a time.

#### C.5.2 D2 — two decoders, video composited on video, no non-video surface

- **Candidate A.** A-1 needs two decoders and nothing else; D2 has two. A-1 wins.
  Had the document order been reversed, A-2 and A-3 would each have been examined
  first and declined — D2 composites video on video and offers no image or HTML
  surface over video — and the walk would have reached A-1 anyway.
- **Candidate B.** B-1 wins on the same budget.
- **What the viewer sees.** The same two L-shapes D1 sees, in the same order. The
  classes converge here because the preferred option happens to be the one D2 can
  satisfy.

#### C.5.3 D3 — one decoder, image on video, HTML on video

- **Candidate A.** A-1 is examined first and declined: the shrunk primary content
  already holds the single decoder, and the full-frame `video` creative would
  need a second. A-2 is next. An HTML surface composited over video is exactly
  what D3 offers, and `overlay-corner` is in `@allowedLayouts`. A-2 wins.
- **Candidate B.** B-1 is declined for the same decoder arithmetic as A-1. B-2 is
  an image surface over video, which D3 has. B-2 wins.
- **What the viewer sees.** The primary content plays at full frame throughout.
  An interactive HTML panel sits in the corner for 12.5 s, disappears, and a
  static corner image takes its place for 7.48 s.

#### C.5.4 D4 — one decoder, image on video, no HTML on video

- **Candidate A.** A-1 declined: one decoder, and the L-shape with a `video`
  creative needs two. A-2 declined: D4 composites images over video and offers no
  HTML surface. A-3 is an image on the lower third, one decoder plus an image
  surface. A-3 wins.
- **Candidate B.** B-1 declined for the decoder count. B-2 is an image in the
  corner. B-2 wins.
- **What the viewer sees.** A static lower-third banner for 12.5 s, then a static
  corner banner for 7.48 s, over uninterrupted primary content. The HTML creative
  the APS returned is never fetched, and neither the Publisher nor the ADS learns
  which option was taken from the manifest exchange alone.

#### C.5.5 D5 — one decoder, no overlay surface of any kind

- **Candidate A.** A-1 declined: the L-shape needs a second decoder and D5 has
  one. A-2 declined: no HTML surface over video. A-3 declined: no image surface
  over video. Every option of candidate A has been walked and none is
  satisfiable, so the candidate is skipped and the Player moves to the next one.
- **Candidate B.** B-1 declined on the decoder count, B-2 declined for want of an
  image surface. Candidate B is skipped.
- **Candidates exhausted.** The list holds no further candidate. The Player
  presents no ad from this window and continues with the primary content.
- **What the viewer sees.** Nothing changes. The primary content plays through
  the forty-second window without a frame of interruption.

D5's outcome is a defined result of the walk, not an error: no beacon fires, no
placeholder is drawn, and the slot closes when its window does. The Publisher's
own authoring produced it — admitting the `linear` token would have given D5 a
full-screen takeover to fall back to, and the Publisher chose a slot that never
interrupts.

#### C.5.6 The walk at a glance

| Class | Candidate A | Candidate B | Total rendered |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | A-1, video L-shape | B-1, video L-shape | 19.98 s |
| D2 | A-1, video L-shape | B-1, video L-shape | 19.98 s |
| D3 | A-2, HTML corner | B-2, image corner | 19.98 s |
| D4 | A-3, image lower third | B-2, image corner | 19.98 s |
| D5 | skipped | skipped | none |

### C.6 The arithmetic

The cap and the candidate durations are expressed in different units. The cap,
`@maxDuration="500"`, is in the parent Event Stream's `@timescale` of 25 — 500
frames, twenty seconds. Each candidate's `@duration` is an ISO 8601 duration. The
Player converts the candidate's duration into the cap's timescale, rounding up to
the next whole unit, and compares there.

**Candidate A.** `PT12.5S` × 25 = 312.5 units, which rounds up to **313**.
Cumulative rendered length after candidate A: 313 of 500. Admitted, with 187
units left.

**Candidate B.** `PT7.48S` × 25 = 187.0 units exactly, so no rounding applies.
Cumulative: 313 + 187 = **500**, which equals the cap. A converted duration that
lands on the cap is admitted, so candidate B plays in full and the slot closes
having used exactly its twenty seconds.

The rounding direction is what makes the boundary decidable. Had the APS declared
candidate B as `PT7.5S`, the conversion would give 187.5 units, rounding up to
188, and the cumulative would be 501 — past the cap. The Player MAY drop such a
candidate before playback, in which case candidate A plays alone and the primary
content continues for the rest of the window. A candidate accepted whose
**actual** length runs past the cap is trimmed mid-render instead, and its
remaining beacons stop at the trim boundary.

Two properties of the arithmetic carry through the whole annex:

- **It runs on the presentation timeline.** If the viewer pauses while candidate
  A is on screen, the interval in which the presentation timeline does not
  advance does not accrue against the cap, and the form resumes with the budget
  it had.
- **It is independent of the option chosen.** Candidate A costs 313 units whether
  D1 renders its L-shape video or D4 renders its lower-third image: the duration
  belongs to the candidate, not to the form. At a playback speed of 1.5× the
  wall-clock length of candidate A is 12.5 / 1.5 ≈ 8.33 s, and the cap still
  accrues 313 units, because cap enforcement and beacon scheduling both run on
  the presentation timeline.

## Annex D — Hybrid: a linear ad with a concurrent overlay

### D.1 The scenario

Six minutes into a live programme the Publisher opens a thirty-second break in
which a linear ad replaces the primary content, and over that linear ad a second,
independent ad is composited. The two are different families and are declared
independently: the linear opportunity is an alternative-MPD replacement from the
base specification, the overlay opportunity is an overlay window of this
specification, and the two `<Event>` elements sit in two Event Streams in the same
Period because each family clusters into its own stream.

The ADS decides both portions without cross-referencing them, and the APS answers
each resolution request with the document that family expects: a `ListMPD` for the
linear slot, an Overlay Resolution Document for the overlay window. The
composition question — whether both survive on a given device — is decided at the
Player, per device class, and on some classes the answer is that only the linear
ad plays.

### D.2 What the Publisher declares

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     xsi:schemaLocation="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011 DASH-MPD.xsd"
     type="dynamic"
     availabilityStartTime="2026-09-18T09:00:00Z"
     minimumUpdatePeriod="PT2S"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:00:00Z"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:advanced-linear:2025">

  <Period id="1" start="PT0S">

    <!-- Family 1: linear replacement, inherited from the base specification. -->
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:alternativeMPD:replace:2025"
                 timescale="1000">
      <Event id="301" presentationTime="360000" duration="30000">
        <ReplacePresentation uri="https://aps.example.com/resolve/linear?slot=301"
                             maxDuration="30000"
                             earliestResolutionTimeOffset="60000"
                             returnOffset="0"
                             clip="true"
                             startWithOffset="false"/>
      </Event>
    </EventStream>

    <!-- Family 2: overlay window, coextensive with the linear break. -->
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026"
                 timescale="1000">
      <Event id="401" presentationTime="360000" duration="30000">
        <svta:OverlayPresentation
            uri="https://aps.example.com/resolve/overlay?slot=401"
            maxDuration="10000"
            allowedLayouts="overlay-lower-third overlay-corner"
            earliestResolutionTimeOffset="60000"
            executeOnce="false"/>
      </Event>
    </EventStream>

    <!-- Primary content -->
    <AdaptationSet id="1" mimeType="video/mp4" codecs="avc1.4d401f"
                   segmentAlignment="true" startWithSAP="1">
      <SegmentTemplate timescale="90000" duration="180000"
                       initialization="video/init.mp4"
                       media="video/seg_$Number$.m4s"
                       startNumber="1"/>
      <Representation id="v1" mimeType="video/mp4" bandwidth="2500000"
                      width="1280" height="720"/>
    </AdaptationSet>

  </Period>
</MPD>
```

The two windows cover the same range, 360 s to 390 s, and each binds the
candidates it serves with its own declarations. The overlay window admits two
layouts and caps at ten seconds; the linear window carries the replacement
attributes and caps at thirty. Neither set of declarations reaches the other
family: an overlay candidate is checked against `allowedLayouts="overlay-lower-third
overlay-corner"` and a ten-second cap whatever the linear slot decided, and the
linear ad is bounded by its own thirty-second cap whatever the overlay does.

The Publisher's restriction is deliberate. The overlay window admits a lower third
and a corner — placements that sit over the top of a full-frame ad — and admits no
squeezeback token, because shrinking a linear ad to make room for a second ad is
not the experience this break is for.

The Player resolves the two `@uri` values independently, each at or after its own
Earliest Resolution Time of 300 s.

### D.3 What the APS returns — the linear slot

For the linear family the resolution document is a `ListMPD`, one Period per ad,
each delegating to a per-ad sub-MPD.

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance"
     xsi:schemaLocation="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011 DASH-MPD.xsd"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:list:2024"
     type="list"
     minBufferTime="PT1S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:05:30Z">

  <BaseURL>https://ads.example.com/delivery/</BaseURL>

  <Period id="ad-linear-01" duration="PT30S">
    <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="30.0">creative-301.mpd</ImportedMPD>
  </Period>

</MPD>
```

And the sub-MPD that `ImportedMPD` resolves to. For the linear family the
tracking schedule rides inside the sub-MPD, in the inherited position:

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance"
     xsi:schemaLocation="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011 DASH-MPD.xsd"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:05:30Z">

  <Period id="1" duration="PT30S">

    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                 value="1" timescale="1000">
      <Event presentationTime="0"     id="10">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=301</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="7500"  id="11">https://tracker.example.com/firstQuartile?ad=301</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="15000" id="12">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=301</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="22500" id="13">https://tracker.example.com/thirdQuartile?ad=301</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="30000" id="14">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=301</Event>
    </EventStream>

    <AdaptationSet mimeType="video/mp4" codecs="avc1.4d401f"
                   segmentAlignment="true" startWithSAP="1">
      <Representation id="v1" mimeType="video/mp4" bandwidth="2500000"
                      width="1280" height="720">
        <BaseURL>media/linear-301.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-948"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
    <AdaptationSet mimeType="audio/mp4" codecs="mp4a.40.2" lang="en">
      <Representation id="a1" mimeType="audio/mp4" bandwidth="128000"
                      audioSamplingRate="48000">
        <BaseURL>media/linear-301-audio.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-712"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

### D.4 What the APS returns — the overlay window

The overlay window gets an Overlay Resolution Document with a single candidate
carrying three options.

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT0S"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:05:31Z">

  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
    <svta:OverlayList>

      <svta:Candidate id="ov-1" duration="PT10S">

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="video" layout="overlay-lower-third">
          <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="30.0"
            >https://ads.example.com/creatives/ov1-lower-third.mpd</ImportedMPD>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="html" layout="overlay-lower-third"
                              assetUrl="https://ads.example.com/creatives/ov1-lower-third.html"/>

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image" layout="overlay-corner"
                              assetUrl="https://ads.example.com/creatives/ov1-corner.png"/>

        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=ov1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="2500"  id="2">https://tracker.example.com/firstQuartile?ad=ov1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="5000"  id="3">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=ov1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="7500"  id="4">https://tracker.example.com/thirdQuartile?ad=ov1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="10000" id="5">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=ov1</Event>
        </EventStream>

        <svta:Click clickThroughUrl="https://advertiser.example.com/campaign-ov1">
          <svta:ClickTracking>https://tracker.example.com/click?ad=ov1</svta:ClickTracking>
        </svta:Click>

        <svta:AdSystem>example-ads</svta:AdSystem>
        <svta:AdTitle>Campaign OV1 — brand bar</svta:AdTitle>
        <svta:Advertiser>Advertiser C</svta:Advertiser>
      </svta:Candidate>

    </svta:OverlayList>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

### D.5 The sub-MPD for the overlay's video option

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance"
     xsi:schemaLocation="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011 DASH-MPD.xsd"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:05:31Z">

  <Period id="1" duration="PT10S">
    <AdaptationSet mimeType="video/mp4" codecs="avc1.4d401f"
                   segmentAlignment="true" startWithSAP="1">
      <Representation id="v1" mimeType="video/mp4" bandwidth="900000"
                      width="1280" height="240">
        <BaseURL>https://ads.example.com/media/ov1-lower-third.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-836"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

### D.6 The composition

During the break the linear ad **is** the alternative presentation, and the
primary content is not being output. The base specification is explicit about
what happens to the main client: *"while the main client will be paused or be in
a listen mode"* (DASH §4.2), and for a replacement, *"the main Media Presentation
is not being output, but its media time progresses at the same speed as the
currently playing alternative Media Presentation"* (DASH §5.16.1).

That sentence is the whole of the composition arithmetic. Two presentations do
not run at once: what changes is which access engine outputs media. The linear ad
occupies the decoder the primary content has just stopped using, so the budget
during the break is **one decoder for the linear ad plus whatever the overlay
needs on top of it** — exactly the budget an overlay consumes over primary
content outside a break.

The single-active-form bound is unaffected here. It bounds how many non-linear
forms are on screen at one instant, and there is one: the overlay. The linear ad
is the alternative presentation underneath it, not a second non-linear form.

The budget per option:

| Option | Form | Layout | Needs, on top of the linear ad | Satisfiable on |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OV-1 | `video` | `overlay-lower-third` | a second decoder | D1, D2 |
| OV-2 | `html` | `overlay-lower-third` | an HTML surface over video | D1, D3 |
| OV-3 | `image` | `overlay-corner` | an image surface over video | D1, D3, D4 |

Both layouts are in the window's `@allowedLayouts`, so every decline below is a
device decline.

#### D.6.1 D1 — two or more decoders, image on video, HTML on video

The linear ad takes the first decoder. Option OV-1 is first in document order and
needs a second, which D1 has. **OV-1 wins.** The viewer sees a full-screen linear
ad with a video brand bar animating across its lower third for ten seconds, after
which the bar disappears and the linear ad runs to its end.

#### D.6.2 D2 — two decoders, video composited on video, no non-video surface

The linear ad takes the first decoder. OV-1 needs a second decoder and nothing
else, and video composited on video is precisely what D2 does. **OV-1 wins**, and
the result is identical to D1's. Had the ADS supplied only the HTML and image
options, both would have been declined — D2 offers no non-video surface over
video — and the linear ad would have played alone.

#### D.6.3 D3 — one decoder, image on video, HTML on video

The linear ad holds the single decoder for the duration of the break. OV-1 is
declined: a second video needs a second decoder. OV-2 is next; an HTML surface
composited over video is what D3 offers, and the decoder count is unchanged by
it. **OV-2 wins.** The viewer sees the full-screen linear ad with an HTML brand
bar over its lower third.

The point worth holding: D3 is not excluded from a hybrid break. The exclusion
would follow from a budget of two decoders, and the budget is one — the primary
content released its decoder when the alternative presentation began.

#### D.6.4 D4 — one decoder, image on video, no HTML on video

OV-1 declined on the decoder count. OV-2 declined for a device property rather
than a count: D4 renders no HTML over video. OV-3 is a still image in the corner,
one image surface over the linear ad's video. **OV-3 wins.** The viewer sees the
full-screen linear ad with a static corner badge for ten seconds.

#### D.6.5 D5 — one decoder, no overlay surface of any kind

OV-1 declined: one decoder, held by the linear ad. OV-2 declined: no HTML
surface. OV-3 declined: no image surface. Every option of the only candidate has
been walked and none is satisfiable, so the candidate is skipped and the
candidates are exhausted. The Player presents no overlay.

**The linear ad plays alone, in full, and that is the defined outcome.** The
overlay window produced no presentation on this device; the linear window is a
separate window with its own resolution, its own candidates and its own cap, and
nothing about the overlay's result touches it. No beacon from the overlay
candidate fires, the overlay's ClickThrough is never armed, and the viewer sees a
thirty-second full-screen ad followed by the primary content resuming at the
playhead the replacement's `@returnOffset` determines.

#### D.6.6 The break at a glance

| Class | Linear portion | Overlay portion | Overlay option |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | plays, 30 s | composited, 10 s | OV-1, video lower third |
| D2 | plays, 30 s | composited, 10 s | OV-1, video lower third |
| D3 | plays, 30 s | composited, 10 s | OV-2, HTML lower third |
| D4 | plays, 30 s | composited, 10 s | OV-3, image corner |
| D5 | plays, 30 s | declined | none satisfiable |

### D.7 The arithmetic

The two windows are capped separately and the two caps are enforced separately.

**The linear cap.** `@maxDuration="30000"` in the linear Event Stream's
`@timescale` of 1000 is thirty seconds. The `ListMPD` declares one Period of
`PT30S`, which converts to 30000 units exactly — equal to the cap, and therefore
admitted. On a replacement slot the cap bounds *until when* rather than *how
long*: `@clip` is `true` here, so the presentation ends at the scheduled end of
the break, and a late start shortens the ad rather than extending the break. Had
the Publisher declared `@clip="false"`, the ad would have ended one cap-length
after its actual start.

**The overlay cap.** `@maxDuration="10000"` in the overlay Event Stream's
`@timescale` of 1000 is ten seconds. Candidate `ov-1` declares `PT10S`; the
conversion into the cap's timescale is 10 × 1000 = 10000 units exactly, needing
no rounding, and a converted duration equal to the cap is admitted. Cumulative
rendered length for the overlay window: 10000 of 10000. On a non-linear family
the cap bounds *how long* — the cumulative rendered length of the forms the slot
presents — so a second candidate, had the APS supplied one, would have been
measured against a remaining budget of zero and dropped before playback.

Had the candidate declared `PT10.4S`, the conversion would give 10400 units, past
the cap; the Player MAY drop such a candidate before playback, and a candidate it
accepts whose actual length runs past the cap is trimmed at 10000 units, with its
remaining beacons stopping at the trim boundary.

Nothing links the two numbers. The overlay's ten seconds are not subtracted from
the linear's thirty, and a linear ad that came back short would not lengthen the
overlay's budget: each window binds the candidates it serves with its own
declarations.

## Annex E — Pause-triggered ad

### E.1 The scenario

Twenty minutes into a live programme the Publisher opens a five-minute stretch in
which a viewer pause is allowed to bring up an ad. The window says **where on the
timeline** a pause triggers a resolution request; it says nothing about how long
the resulting slot lasts, because the viewer sets that by deciding when to press
play again. The base specification already treats a pause as something that moves
the playhead: *"the media time and the corresponding playhead can change, for
example, if the media engine is instructed with seek or pause operations"* (DASH
§4.2).

The Publisher admits both pause surfaces, caps one pause ad's display at fifteen
seconds, and marks the window as yielding at most one pause ad for the session.
The APS returns two candidates and declares what the Player does when they run
out while the viewer is still paused.

### E.2 What the Publisher declares

The pause family gets its own Event Stream. Its `@timescale` is 1 — one unit is
one second — so the cap, the window position and the window length are all read
directly in seconds.

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     xsi:schemaLocation="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011 DASH-MPD.xsd"
     type="dynamic"
     availabilityStartTime="2026-09-18T09:00:00Z"
     minimumUpdatePeriod="PT2S"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     timeShiftBufferDepth="PT1H"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:00:00Z"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:advanced-linear:2025">

  <Period id="1" start="PT0S">

    <!-- Pause-trigger family. timescale=1 → one unit is one second. -->
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-pause-trigger:2026"
                 timescale="1">
      <!-- A pause between 1200 s and 1500 s triggers the request. -->
      <Event id="501" presentationTime="1200" duration="300">
        <svta:PauseAdPresentation
            uri="https://aps.example.com/resolve/pause?slot=501"
            maxDuration="15"
            allowedLayouts="pause-partial pause-fullscreen"
            earliestResolutionTimeOffset="60"
            executeOnce="true"/>
      </Event>
    </EventStream>

    <!-- Primary content -->
    <AdaptationSet id="1" mimeType="video/mp4" codecs="avc1.4d401f"
                   segmentAlignment="true" startWithSAP="1">
      <SegmentTemplate timescale="90000" duration="180000"
                       initialization="video/init.mp4"
                       media="video/seg_$Number$.m4s"
                       startNumber="1"/>
      <Representation id="v1" mimeType="video/mp4" bandwidth="2500000"
                      width="1280" height="720"/>
    </AdaptationSet>

  </Period>
</MPD>
```

What the declaration fixes, and what it deliberately leaves open:

- **Where a pause counts.** `@presentationTime="1200"` and `@duration="300"`
  bound the region of the timeline in which a pause triggers a resolution
  request: from 20:00 to 25:00. A pause at 19:58 triggers nothing from this
  window.
- **How long one pause ad shows.** `@maxDuration="15"` is fifteen seconds and
  bounds the display of **one** pause ad before automatic dismissal. The pause
  slot itself has no declared length, because the viewer owns it.
- **Which surfaces.** `@allowedLayouts="pause-partial pause-fullscreen"` admits
  both pause placements from the layout enumeration of §3.2.
- **When to resolve.** The Earliest Resolution Time is 1200 − 60 = 1140 s. The
  Player MAY resolve speculatively from that instant — trading freshness for
  latency at the moment of pause — or lazily at the pause itself. Both are
  conformant; this annex shows the lazy resolution, because it makes the ordering
  of events easiest to read.
- **How often.** `@executeOnce="true"` makes the window yield at most one pause
  ad for the session.

#### E.2.1 What `@executeOnce` consumes, and when

The window is consumed when a pause ad **begins rendering**, not when the pause
occurs. The two come apart often enough to be worth stating:

- The viewer pauses at 21:10, the Player resolves, the APS returns a document
  carrying candidates, the first satisfiable option begins rendering. The window
  is consumed. A second pause at 23:40 triggers no request.
- The viewer pauses at 21:10, the Player resolves, and the response fails to
  arrive, or arrives carrying no candidates. No pause ad began rendering, so the
  window stays available; a pause at 23:40 resolves again. This follows the base
  specification's own treatment of the execution counter: *"counter E.c has not
  been incremented due to the failure, consequently if E.c = 0 the event can
  still be executed in the future"* (DASH §5.16.2.2.6 NOTE 3), which the same
  note applies to a once-only event.
- The viewer pauses at 21:10, the Player resolves, every candidate's options are
  declined on this device, and the candidates are exhausted without a render. The
  window stays available, for the same reason.

### E.3 What the APS returns

The document is an Overlay Resolution Document — "overlay" naming the non-linear
family here, not the `overlay` layout token — with the exhaustion behaviour
declared on the list.

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT0S"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:21:10Z">

  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
    <svta:OverlayList onCandidatesExhausted="repeat">

      <!-- Candidate P-A: both pause surfaces, three forms. -->
      <svta:Candidate id="pause-a" duration="PT15S">

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="html" layout="pause-partial"
                              assetUrl="https://ads.example.com/creatives/pa-partial.html"/>

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image" layout="pause-partial"
                              assetUrl="https://ads.example.com/creatives/pa-partial.png"/>

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="video" layout="pause-fullscreen">
          <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="5.0"
            >https://ads.example.com/creatives/pa-fullscreen.mpd</ImportedMPD>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>

        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=pa</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="3750"  id="2">https://tracker.example.com/firstQuartile?ad=pa</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="7500"  id="3">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=pa</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="11250" id="4">https://tracker.example.com/thirdQuartile?ad=pa</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="15000" id="5">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=pa</Event>
        </EventStream>

        <svta:Click clickThroughUrl="https://advertiser.example.com/campaign-pa">
          <svta:ClickTracking>https://tracker.example.com/click?ad=pa</svta:ClickTracking>
        </svta:Click>

        <svta:AdSystem>example-ads</svta:AdSystem>
        <svta:AdTitle>Campaign PA — pause takeover</svta:AdTitle>
        <svta:Advertiser>Advertiser D</svta:Advertiser>
        <svta:UniversalAdId>example.com/AD-2026-1104</svta:UniversalAdId>
      </svta:Candidate>

      <!-- Candidate P-B: shorter, two forms. -->
      <svta:Candidate id="pause-b" duration="PT7.5S">

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image" layout="pause-partial"
                              assetUrl="https://ads.example.com/creatives/pb-partial.jpg"/>

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="video" layout="pause-fullscreen">
          <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="5.0"
            >https://ads.example.com/creatives/pb-fullscreen.mpd</ImportedMPD>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>

        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"    id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=pb</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="3750" id="2">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=pb</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="7500" id="3">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=pb</Event>
        </EventStream>

        <svta:AdSystem>example-ads</svta:AdSystem>
        <svta:AdTitle>Campaign PB — pause filler</svta:AdTitle>
      </svta:Candidate>

    </svta:OverlayList>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

### E.4 The sub-MPDs

### E.5 The Player's decision walk-through

Both layout tokens are in the window's `@allowedLayouts`, so the layout check
passes for every option and the device check decides. The budgets differ between
the two surfaces, and that difference is the whole of this annex's walk:

- **`pause-partial`** keeps the paused primary frame visible underneath, so the
  ad is composited over held video and the surface budget is the overlay one: a
  `video` form needs a second decoder, an `image` form an image surface over
  video, an `html` form an HTML surface over video.
- **`pause-fullscreen`** replaces the whole surface, so the Player MAY release
  the primary content's resources and any overlay's. A `video` pause ad is
  therefore satisfiable on every class, including a class with one decoder: the
  decoder the paused primary frame was holding is free to be re-tasked, because
  nothing of the primary content is on screen any more. An `image` form still
  needs an image surface and an `html` form an HTML surface, so those two remain
  bounded by the device's surfaces rather than by its decoder count.

| Option | Form | Layout | Needs | Satisfiable on |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PA-1 | `html` | `pause-partial` | HTML surface over the held frame | D1, D3 |
| PA-2 | `image` | `pause-partial` | image surface over the held frame | D1, D3, D4 |
| PA-3 | `video` | `pause-fullscreen` | one decoder, primary released | D1, D2, D3, D4, D5 |
| PB-1 | `image` | `pause-partial` | image surface over the held frame | D1, D3, D4 |
| PB-2 | `video` | `pause-fullscreen` | one decoder, primary released | D1, D2, D3, D4, D5 |

#### E.5.1 D1 — two or more decoders, image on video, HTML on video

PA-1 is first in document order and D1 composites HTML over video. **PA-1 wins**,
and PA-2 and PA-3 are never examined. The viewer pauses, the paused frame stays
on screen, and an interactive HTML panel is composited over part of it. When
candidate P-A ends after fifteen seconds and the viewer is still paused,
candidate P-B starts: PB-1 is an image over the held frame and D1 takes it.

#### E.5.2 D2 — two decoders, video composited on video, no non-video surface

PA-1 declined: D2 composites no HTML over video. PA-2 declined: no image surface
over video. PA-3 is a full-screen video pause ad, which D2 renders on either
decoder with the primary content's frame released. **PA-3 wins.** The viewer
pauses and the paused frame is replaced by a fifteen-second full-screen ad
video. Candidate P-B then resolves to PB-2 on the same reasoning.

#### E.5.3 D3 — one decoder, image on video, HTML on video

PA-1 is first, and an HTML surface over the held frame is what D3 offers. The
single decoder stays where it is, holding the paused frame. **PA-1 wins**, and
the viewer sees the same HTML panel D1 sees. Candidate P-B resolves to PB-1, the
image over the held frame.

Note what D3 did *not* have to decide: it never had to re-task its one decoder,
because the preferred option is a surface composited over a frame the decoder is
already holding.

#### E.5.4 D4 — one decoder, image on video, no HTML on video

PA-1 declined: D4 renders no HTML over video. PA-2 is a still image over the held
frame, one image surface, no change to the decoder. **PA-2 wins.** The viewer
sees a static panel over the paused frame. Candidate P-B resolves to PB-1.

#### E.5.5 D5 — one decoder, no overlay surface of any kind

PA-1 declined: no HTML surface. PA-2 declined: no image surface. PA-3 is a
`pause-fullscreen` `video` option, and this is where the fullscreen surface earns
its place in the enumeration: because the ad occupies the whole screen, the
paused primary frame need not stay visible, and the Player MAY release the
primary content's decoder and buffers and give the decoder to the ad. D5's single
decoder is enough. **PA-3 wins.**

This is the one class where the pause family reaches a device that the coexisting
overlay family cannot reach at all. The viewer pauses, the frame gives way to a
full-screen fifteen-second ad, and on resume the primary content comes back. When
P-A ends and the viewer is still paused, candidate P-B resolves to PB-2 on the
same budget.

**What the release costs.** Releasing the primary content's decoder means the
paused frame is gone from the screen and its buffered media may have been
discarded; on resume the Player re-acquires the decoder and re-buffers from the
resumption point, which the viewer perceives as a short start-up. A Player that
prefers to keep the frame held MAY decline `pause-fullscreen` on a single-decoder
device and skip to the next candidate; the release is permitted, not required.

#### E.5.6 The walk at a glance

| Class | Candidate P-A | Candidate P-B | Paused frame during P-A |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | PA-1, HTML partial | PB-1, image partial | visible underneath |
| D2 | PA-3, video fullscreen | PB-2, video fullscreen | released |
| D3 | PA-1, HTML partial | PB-1, image partial | visible underneath |
| D4 | PA-2, image partial | PB-1, image partial | visible underneath |
| D5 | PA-3, video fullscreen | PB-2, video fullscreen | released |

### E.6 When the candidates run out

The viewer in this walk pauses for ninety seconds. Candidate P-A occupies fifteen
of them and candidate P-B seven and a half, so after 22.5 seconds the list is
exhausted and sixty-seven and a half seconds of pause remain.
`@onCandidatesExhausted` on `<svta:OverlayList>` is what decides the rest, and
the document above declares `repeat`. All three values are walked here against
the same ninety-second pause.

**`repeat`** — the declared value in this document. The Player presents the
sequence again from the start, for as long as the pause lasts. On D1 that is
P-A's HTML panel for fifteen seconds, P-B's image for seven and a half, then P-A
again, and so on. The fourth repetition is cut short by the resume rather than by
the list. Each presentation of a candidate is a fresh presentation for tracking:
P-A's impression beacon fires again at the start of the second repetition,
because de-duplication is scoped to one candidate's presentation.

**`request-again`** — the Player issues a new resolution request for the same
pause and presents whatever candidates come back. The new document is an ordinary
resolution document with its own candidates and its own exhaustion behaviour. A
document that comes back carrying no candidates ends the ad presentation for the
remainder of that pause, which is `stop` from that point on; that keeps the loop
bounded without the Player having to count requests.

**`stop`** — the default, and what a document that omits the attribute gets. The
Player presents no further ad and the paused primary frame is shown for the
remaining sixty-seven and a half seconds. On D2 and D5, where the pause ads were
`pause-fullscreen` and the primary frame was released, `stop` is the moment the
Player restores the paused frame to the screen.

The window's own bound is unaffected by any of the three: `@executeOnce="true"`
was consumed when candidate P-A began rendering, so no later pause in this session
triggers another request, whatever the exhaustion behaviour did inside this one.

### E.7 Dismissal on resume

The viewer presses play. The pause ad is dismissed within one rendering frame of
the resume, and the primary content continues from the paused position — 21:10 in
this walk — immediately, even if the ad was mid-presentation.

The beacons that fire are those whose presentation time the pause ad reached
while it was on screen. Suppose the resume arrives eight seconds into candidate
P-A on D1: the impression at 0 ms, the first quartile at 3750 ms and the midpoint
at 7500 ms have all been reached and fired; the schedule stops at the dismissal
boundary, so the third quartile at 11250 ms and the completion at 15000 ms stay
unfired. The click-tracking in `<svta:Click>` is on the same footing: it fires on
activation, so a ClickThrough never activated leaves it unfired.

On the classes that released the primary content's resources for a
`pause-fullscreen` ad, the resume is also where the Player re-acquires them. The
dismissal boundary is the same instant; what differs is that D2 and D5 have a
re-buffer to pay before the first frame of primary content appears, and D1, D3
and D4 have the frame already held.

Nothing else is restored, because nothing else was suspended: the pause ad in
this annex stands alone. Where a pause window overlaps an overlay window, the
pause ad is the only ad surface visible while the viewer is paused, the
coexisting form is suspended, and on resume it is restored only if its own window
is still open.

### E.8 The live variant — the presentation-time freeze

The content here is live, and the viewer pauses at 21:10 inside a window that runs
to 25:00. While the viewer is paused the Player's presentation time stays frozen
at 21:10 while the live edge advances in wall-clock time. The window is anchored
to presentation time, so a pause that started inside the window stays inside it:
a ninety-second pause does not walk the presentation time out the far end of the
window at 25:00, and the pause ad stays admissible for the pause.

**That freeze is bounded, and the bound is the time-shift buffer.** The manifest
above declares `timeShiftBufferDepth="PT1H"`, so a pause that outlives an hour
leaves the resumption point behind the buffer's trailing edge, and the Player
resumes from the edge rather than from 21:10. The base specification handles the
same situation for alternative presentations by *"clipping RT to the timeshift
buffer (i.e. the time interval between TSBS and PHPLE)"* (DASH §5.16.2.2.5), and
the pause resumption point is clipped the same way.

**This is stated as a bound rather than a guarantee on purpose.** What a Player
does with an ad presentation that is still on screen when the resumption point
has already fallen off the buffer is an open point: the ad has not been dismissed
because no resume has happened, and the position it would return to no longer
exists. The candidates behave the same either way — `@onCandidatesExhausted`
keeps deciding what is on screen — and the resumption point is the thing that
moved. This specification records the clipping and leaves the interaction between
a very long pause and the buffer edge to be settled with the working group rather
than promising an unbounded freeze it cannot deliver.

Two properties follow from the freeze. Cap enforcement runs on the presentation
timeline, and while the primary content's timeline is frozen it is the pause ad's
own that advances — which is what `@maxDuration` on this window bounds. And a
jump to the live edge after the resume sits outside the window entirely: the
resume dismisses the pause ad, and a seek that follows it is a later action.

### E.9 The arithmetic

The cap on a pause window bounds **one** pause ad's display before automatic
dismissal, rather than the slot. The slot's length is the viewer's, and nothing in
the manifest bounds it.

`@maxDuration="15"` is in the parent Event Stream's `@timescale` of 1 — fifteen
units, fifteen seconds. Each candidate's `@duration` is an ISO 8601 duration,
converted into that timescale and rounded up to the next whole unit before the
comparison.

**Candidate P-A.** `PT15S` × 1 = 15.0 units, needing no rounding. 15 equals the
cap, and a converted duration equal to the cap is admitted, so P-A plays to its
declared end and is dismissed automatically at fifteen seconds if the viewer is
still paused at that point.

**Candidate P-B.** `PT7.5S` × 1 = 7.5 units, which rounds **up** to **8**. Eight
is below the cap of fifteen, so P-B is admitted. The rounding is what makes the
comparison decidable in a timescale coarser than the duration it measures; it
costs half a second of budget and changes nothing about how long P-B renders.

**The sum is not measured against this cap.** P-A and P-B together occupy 22.5
seconds of the ninety-second pause, and 22.5 exceeding 15 is not an overrun: the
cap applies per pause ad. Under `@onCandidatesExhausted="repeat"` each repetition
is measured the same way — P-A's 15 against the cap, P-B's 8 against the cap —
and the total across a long pause grows without bound, because it is the viewer,
not the manifest, who decides when the pause ends.

Had the APS declared a candidate of `PT15.2S`, the conversion would give 15.2
units rounding up to 16, past the cap of 15; the Player MAY drop such a candidate
before playback and move to the next, and a candidate it accepts whose actual
length runs past the cap is trimmed at fifteen seconds, with the beacons after the
trim boundary staying unfired.

**Playback speed plays no part here.** The declared `duration` is a
presentation-timeline value for every form, including `image` and `html`, and the
wall-clock length on screen is derived as `duration / playback_speed`. During a
pause the primary content's speed is not advancing at all, so a pause ad renders
at its declared presentation-timeline length and the derived wall-clock length
equals it.

## Annex F — Multi-ad break

### F.1 The scenario

A viewer is watching an on-demand programme. The Publisher has declared one
mid-content break at media time 10:00, capped at 60 seconds in total, and the ADS
fills it with three ads played back to back with no primary content between them.
The Publisher expresses no preference on how many ads fit: it declares only the
cap, and the sum of what is presented stays inside it.

The third ad is authored so that its declared duration would push the break past
the cap. The annex walks both of the outcomes the specification admits: dropping
that ad before playback on its declared duration, and accepting it and trimming
it mid-render on its actual length.

### F.2 What the Publisher declares

<!-- refine: v9-dash-conformance-audit.md#N1 -->
```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:advanced-linear:2025"
     type="static"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT1H"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:00:00Z">

  <RequestParam includeInRequests="altmpd"
                queryTemplate="session_id=$urn:mpeg:dash:state:cmcd#sid$"/>

  <Period id="main" start="PT0S">

    <!-- The break: one window, capped against the sum of what fills it (§5.1). -->
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:alternativeMPD:insert:2025"
                 timescale="1000">
      <Event id="301" presentationTime="600000" duration="60000">
        <InsertPresentation uri="https://ads.example.com/aps/break-1"
                            maxDuration="60000"
                            earliestResolutionTimeOffset="60000"/>
      </Event>
    </EventStream>

    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video"
                   segmentAlignment="true" startWithSAP="1">
      <SegmentTemplate timescale="1000" duration="2000"
                       initialization="https://cdn.example.com/primary/init.mp4"
                       media="https://cdn.example.com/primary/seg_$Number$.m4s"
                       startNumber="1"/>
      <Representation id="v1" mimeType="video/mp4" codecs="avc1.4d401f"
                      bandwidth="2500000" width="1280" height="720"/>
    </AdaptationSet>

  </Period>
</MPD>
```

### F.3 What the APS returns

Three ads, in the order the ADS decided. DASH §8.14 requires that "List MPDs
shall have the value of the MPD@type attribute set to "list"" and that the
profile URN appears in `MPD@profiles`.

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:list:2024"
     type="list"
     minBufferTime="PT1S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:09:08Z">

  <BaseURL>https://cdn.example.com/creatives/</BaseURL>

  <Period id="ad-21" duration="PT15S">
    <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="0">creative-4401.mpd</ImportedMPD>
  </Period>

  <Period id="ad-22" duration="PT30S">
    <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="15">creative-4402.mpd</ImportedMPD>
  </Period>

  <Period id="ad-23" duration="PT25S">
    <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="45">creative-4403.mpd</ImportedMPD>
  </Period>

</MPD>
```

### F.4 The sub-MPDs

`creative-4401.mpd` — 15 seconds.

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024"
     type="static" minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:09:08Z">
  <Period id="1" duration="PT15S">
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                 value="1" timescale="1000">
      <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=4401</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="0"     id="2">https://tracker.example.com/start?ad=4401</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="3750"  id="3">https://tracker.example.com/firstQuartile?ad=4401</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="7500"  id="4">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=4401</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="11250" id="5">https://tracker.example.com/thirdQuartile?ad=4401</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="15000" id="6">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=4401</Event>
    </EventStream>
    <AdaptationSet contentType="video" segmentAlignment="true" startWithSAP="1">
      <Representation id="v1" mimeType="video/mp4" codecs="avc1.4d401f"
                      bandwidth="2500000" width="1280" height="720">
        <BaseURL>media/creative-4401.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-880"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

`creative-4402.mpd` — 30 seconds.

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024"
     type="static" minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:09:08Z">
  <Period id="1" duration="PT30S">
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                 value="1" timescale="1000">
      <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=4402</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="0"     id="2">https://tracker.example.com/start?ad=4402</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="7500"  id="3">https://tracker.example.com/firstQuartile?ad=4402</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="15000" id="4">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=4402</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="22500" id="5">https://tracker.example.com/thirdQuartile?ad=4402</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="30000" id="6">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=4402</Event>
    </EventStream>
    <AdaptationSet contentType="video" segmentAlignment="true" startWithSAP="1">
      <Representation id="v1" mimeType="video/mp4" codecs="avc1.4d401f"
                      bandwidth="2500000" width="1280" height="720">

      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

`creative-4403.mpd` — 25 seconds, the ad the cap acts on.

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024"
     type="static" minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:09:08Z">
  <Period id="1" duration="PT25S">
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                 value="1" timescale="1000">
      <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=4403</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="0"     id="2">https://tracker.example.com/start?ad=4403</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="6250"  id="3">https://tracker.example.com/firstQuartile?ad=4403</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="12500" id="4">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=4403</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="18750" id="5">https://tracker.example.com/thirdQuartile?ad=4403</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="25000" id="6">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=4403</Event>
    </EventStream>
    <AdaptationSet contentType="video" segmentAlignment="true" startWithSAP="1">
      <Representation id="v1" mimeType="video/mp4" codecs="avc1.4d401f"
                      bandwidth="2500000" width="1280" height="720">
        <BaseURL>media/creative-4403.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-1020"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

### F.5 The walk-through

1. The Player finds event `301` at presentation time 600 000 ms (10:00) with a
   cap of 60 000 ms.
2. Earliest Resolution Time: `ERT = 600 000 − 60 000 = 540 000 ms`, media time
   9:00. The Player resolves inside `[540 000, 600 000)`.
3. The APS answers `200` with the three-period `ListMPD` of §F.3. The document
   carries candidates, so the resolution succeeded.
4. Cap arithmetic, candidate by candidate, in the cap's timescale:

   | Candidate | Declared | Converted | Cumulative if admitted | Against cap 60 000 |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | `ad-21` | `PT15S` | 15 000 | 15 000 | inside |
   | `ad-22` | `PT30S` | 30 000 | 45 000 | inside |
   | `ad-23` | `PT25S` | 25 000 | 70 000 | past |

5. `ad-21` and `ad-22` play in declared order. The Player fires their beacons at
   their own ad-local times: 0, 0, 3 750, 7 500, 11 250, 15 000 for the first and
   0, 0, 7 500, 15 000, 22 500, 30 000 for the second. Cumulative rendered length
   is 45 000 ms, leaving 15 000 ms of the cap.
6. For `ad-23` the specification admits two outcomes (§4.5), and a Player may
   implement either.

   **Branch 1 — dropped before playback, on the declared duration.** The Player
   reads `PT25S`, sees that `45 000 + 25 000 = 70 000` exceeds 60 000, and drops
   the candidate before fetching `creative-4403.mpd`. The break renders 45 000 ms,
   no beacon of `ad-23` fires, and the primary content resumes.

   **Branch 2 — accepted, then trimmed mid-render on the actual length.** The
   Player admits `ad-23`, fetches its sub-MPD and starts rendering. The remaining
   budget is `60 000 − 45 000 = 15 000 ms`, so the trim boundary falls at ad-local
   15 000 ms, and the ad is cut there having rendered 15 s of its 25 s. The
   beacons scheduled at 0, 0, 6 250 and 12 500 ms fire because they fall inside
   the rendered span; the ones scheduled at 18 750 and 25 000 ms are past the trim
   boundary and stay unfired. Cumulative rendered length is exactly 60 000 ms.

7. In both branches the order the resolution document declared is the order the
   viewer sees: `ad-21`, then `ad-22`, then `ad-23` if it survives. The Player
   does not re-order the survivors to fit a shorter candidate into the remaining
   budget, and it does not collapse `ad-22` and `ad-23` even though they come from
   the same advertiser — each is a distinct candidate and each is presented.
8. The break is an insertion, so no primary content was consumed: the programme
   resumes at media time 600 000 ms, the point at which it paused.

### F.6 Per-device-class behaviour

The outcome is the same on D1 to D5, for the same reason as in Annexes A and B:
every ad in the break is a full-viewport takeover played in sequence, so no
second decoder and no overlay surface is required at any point. On D1 and D2 the
second decoder may pre-buffer ad N+1 while ad N plays; on D3, D4 and D5 the
single decoder is reused between ads and then handed back to the primary content.
The cap arithmetic of §F.5, the order of presentation and the beacon schedule are
identical across the five classes.

## Annex G — A Player that predates this specification

### G.1 The scenario

A viewer watches an on-demand programme on a Player built before this
specification existed. The Publisher has authored the manifest with every kind of
opportunity this specification introduces: an overlay window at media time 02:00,
a pause-trigger window covering most of the programme, and — because the content
is on demand — a baseline linear break at the start of the session using only
constructs the base specification already defines.

That Player has never heard of the extension namespace. This annex shows what it
receives, what remains of the document once everything in that namespace is
removed, and what the viewer therefore sees. The guarantee rests on DASH §5.2.1,
which requires that "the MPD shall be authored such that, after XML attributes or
elements in the other namespaces than the DASH namespace are removed, the result
is a valid XML document formatted according to that schema and that conforms to
this document."

### G.2 What the Publisher declares

<!-- refine: v9-dash-conformance-audit.md#N1 -->
```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:advanced-linear:2025"
     type="static"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT30M"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:00:00Z">

  <RequestParam includeInRequests="altmpd urn:svta:dash:request:sgai-resolution:2026"
                queryTemplate="session_id=$urn:mpeg:dash:state:cmcd#sid$"/>

  <Period id="main" start="PT0S">

    <!-- Baseline linear break: the legacy fallback the Publisher authored. -->
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:alternativeMPD:insert:2025"
```
