```xml
                 timescale="1000">
      <Event id="403" presentationTime="0" duration="15000">
        <InsertPresentation uri="https://ads.example.com/aps/preroll-baseline"
                            maxDuration="15000"
                            earliestResolutionTimeOffset="0"/>
      </Event>
    </EventStream>

    <!-- Overlay opportunity window (§5.1). -->
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026"
                 timescale="1000">
      <Event id="401" presentationTime="120000" duration="20000">
        <svta:OverlayPresentation uri="https://ads.example.com/aps/overlay"
                                  maxDuration="20000"
                                  allowedLayouts="overlay-corner overlay-lower-third"
                                  earliestResolutionTimeOffset="60000"/>
      </Event>
    </EventStream>

    <!-- Pause-trigger opportunity window (§5.1). -->
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-pause-trigger:2026"
                 timescale="1000">
      <Event id="402" presentationTime="60000" duration="1680000">
        <svta:PauseAdPresentation uri="https://ads.example.com/aps/pause"
                                  maxDuration="30000"
                                  allowedLayouts="pause-fullscreen pause-partial"
                                  earliestResolutionTimeOffset="60000"/>
      </Event>
    </EventStream>

    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video"
                   segmentAlignment="true" startWithSAP="1">
      <SegmentTemplate timescale="1000" duration="2000"
                       initialization="https://cdn.example.com/primary/init.mp4"
                       media="https://cdn.example.com/primary/seg_$Number$.m4s"
                       startNumber="1"/>
      <Representation id="v1" mimeType="video/mp4" codecs="avc1.4d401f"
                      bandwidth="2500000" width="1280" height="720"/>
    </AdaptationSet>

  </Period>
</MPD>
```

### G.3 The same document after removal of the extension namespace

Removing every element and attribute in `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026` — and the
prefix declaration that bound it — leaves this:

<!-- refine: v9-dash-conformance-audit.md#N1 -->
```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:advanced-linear:2025"
     type="static"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT30M"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:00:00Z">

  <RequestParam includeInRequests="altmpd urn:svta:dash:request:sgai-resolution:2026"
                queryTemplate="session_id=$urn:mpeg:dash:state:cmcd#sid$"/>

  <Period id="main" start="PT0S">

    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:alternativeMPD:insert:2025"
                 timescale="1000">
      <Event id="403" presentationTime="0" duration="15000">
        <InsertPresentation uri="https://ads.example.com/aps/preroll-baseline"
                            maxDuration="15000"
                            earliestResolutionTimeOffset="0"/>
      </Event>
    </EventStream>

    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026"
                 timescale="1000">
      <Event id="401" presentationTime="120000" duration="20000"/>
    </EventStream>

    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-pause-trigger:2026"
                 timescale="1000">
      <Event id="402" presentationTime="60000" duration="1680000"/>
    </EventStream>

    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video"
                   segmentAlignment="true" startWithSAP="1">
      <SegmentTemplate timescale="1000" duration="2000"
                       initialization="https://cdn.example.com/primary/init.mp4"
                       media="https://cdn.example.com/primary/seg_$Number$.m4s"
                       startNumber="1"/>
      <Representation id="v1" mimeType="video/mp4" codecs="avc1.4d401f"
                      bandwidth="2500000" width="1280" height="720"/>
    </AdaptationSet>

  </Period>
</MPD>
```

What remains is a valid, conformant document, and that is the whole of the
guarantee. The two `<EventStream>` elements of the extension's own schemes
survive removal because they belong to the DASH namespace, but they now carry
`<Event>` elements with no children, under scheme URIs that Player has no
handler for, so it passes over them the way it passes over any Event Stream
whose scheme it does not implement (DASH §5.10.2.1) `[inferred]`. The
`<RequestParam>` element also survives; the unknown request-type URI inside
its `@includeInRequests` is dropped by that Player before processing, which
Table I.4 of the base specification requires: "The client shall drop unknown
URIs from the @includeInRequests and @includeInHeaders strings prior to
processing them as specified in this Annex." What stays in the attribute is
`altmpd`, which that Player understands.

### G.4 What the APS returns, for the break that Player does play

The baseline break resolves like any other linear opportunity (§6.1).

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:list:2024"
     type="list"
     minBufferTime="PT1S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:00:02Z">
  <BaseURL>https://cdn.example.com/creatives/</BaseURL>
  <Period id="ad-31" duration="PT15S">
    <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="0">creative-5501.mpd</ImportedMPD>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

### G.5 The sub-MPD

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024"
     type="static" minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T12:00:02Z">
  <Period id="1" duration="PT15S">
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                 value="1" timescale="1000">
      <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=5501</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="0"     id="2">https://tracker.example.com/start?ad=5501</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="3750"  id="3">https://tracker.example.com/firstQuartile?ad=5501</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="7500"  id="4">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=5501</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="11250" id="5">https://tracker.example.com/thirdQuartile?ad=5501</Event>
      <Event presentationTime="15000" id="6">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=5501</Event>
    </EventStream>
    <AdaptationSet contentType="video" segmentAlignment="true" startWithSAP="1">
      <Representation id="v1" mimeType="video/mp4" codecs="avc1.4d401f"
                      bandwidth="2500000" width="1280" height="720">
        <BaseURL>media/creative-5501.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-880"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

### G.6 The walk-through

1. The Player parses the main MPD of §G.2 and keeps the elements it recognises.
   The elements in the extension namespace are foreign content it has no handler
   for, so what it works with is the document of §G.3.
2. It recognises the insertion scheme of event `403`, computes
   `ERT = 0 − 0 = 0 ms`, resolves `https://ads.example.com/aps/preroll-baseline`
   with `session_id` appended, receives the `ListMPD` of §G.4, checks
   `PT15S → 15 000 ms` against the cap of 15 000 ms, and plays the 15-second
   creative full-viewport. It fires the six beacons of §G.5 at ad-local 0, 0,
   3 750, 7 500, 11 250 and 15 000 ms. The programme then starts at its first
   frame.
3. It reaches media time 120 000 ms, where the overlay window sits. It holds no
   handler for `urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026`, issues no resolution
   request for it, fires no beacon for it, and the programme continues playing
   without interruption or visible artefact.
4. The viewer pauses at media time 300 000 ms, inside the pause-trigger window.
   The same thing happens: no handler, no resolution request, no beacon, and the
   paused frame stays on screen until the viewer resumes.
5. The programme plays to its end. The viewer saw one ad — the baseline break —
   and nothing about the two unexercised windows reached the screen or the
   tracker.

### G.7 The Publisher's authoring choice

A Publisher cannot tell from the manifest which Player a viewer will bring, so
the choice is made at authoring time and it depends on the content:

- **Live content.** The opportunity is an expected loss on that Player. Splicing
  a baseline break into a live stream costs real programme content, so the
  Publisher authors the extension constructs alone and accepts that the viewer
  on the older Player sees the live edge play through.
- **On-demand content.** The Publisher MAY author a standard linear break using
  only baseline constructs alongside the new constructs, exactly as event `403`
  above does. Inserting a break into on-demand content costs no programme
  content. A Player conformant to this specification takes the richer path the
  extension constructs offer; the older Player plays the standard break, so the
  opportunity is monetised on both.

### G.8 Construct by construct

| Construct | What that Player sees | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `<svta:OverlayPresentation>` (§5.1) | Foreign-namespace content inside an `<Event>` of an Event Stream whose scheme it has no handler for | Removes it; the surviving empty `<Event>` sits under an unhandled scheme, so the programme plays on |
| `<svta:PauseAdPresentation>` (§5.1) | The same, in the pause-trigger Event Stream | The same; a pause shows the paused frame and nothing else |
| `<svta:OverlayList>` (§5.2) | Never fetched — the document that carries it is reached only by resolving a window whose scheme that Player skips | Nothing; no resolution request is issued |
| `<svta:Candidate>` (§5.2) | Never fetched, for the same reason | Nothing |
| `<svta:RenderableAsset>` (§5.3) | Never fetched | Nothing |
| The `@layout` token space (§3.2) | Attribute values inside elements it never reads | Nothing |
| `<svta:BackgroundElement>` (§5.3) | Never fetched | Nothing |
| `<svta:Click>` (§5.6) | Never fetched | Nothing; no ClickThrough surface is offered and no click-tracking fires |
| `<svta:AdSystem>`, `<svta:AdTitle>`, `<svta:Advertiser>`, `<svta:UniversalAdId>` (§5.7) | Never fetched | Nothing; these are optional to read even for a conformant Player |
| The reserved capability parameters `sgaiVideoDecoders`, `sgaiImageOverlay`, `sgaiHtmlOverlay` (§5.8) | Parameters it never populates, on a request type it never issues | Nothing; the request-type URI naming the resolution request is dropped from `@includeInRequests` before processing, leaving `altmpd` |

### G.9 Per-device-class behaviour

The outcome is identical on D1 to D5, and for a different reason than in the
linear annexes: here it does not depend on the hardware at all. The three
capability axes that separate the classes — concurrent decoders, image surface
over video, HTML surface over video — are consulted when a Player selects a
presentation option, and this Player never reaches a selection step because it
issues no resolution request for the extension windows. A top-tier D1 device
running that Player produces the same screen as a D5 device running it: the
baseline break, then the programme, uninterrupted. What changes the outcome is
the Player's vintage and the content type, not the device.

## Annex H — An overlay window crossing a pause-trigger window

### H.1 The scenario

A live service declares two non-linear opportunity windows over the same
stretch of its timeline: an overlay window and a pause-trigger window. The
two overlap. The overlay window opens at presentation time 600.000 s and
closes 60 s later; the pause-trigger window opens at 570.000 s and closes
120 s later, so the whole of the overlay window sits inside it. An overlay
is on screen and the viewer presses pause.

Two families are in play at once and §7 gives the pause family priority
while the viewer is paused. The overlay is suspended, the pause ad becomes
the only ad surface visible whatever surface it occupies, and on resume the
pause ad is dismissed within one rendering frame. Whether the overlay comes
back depends on one thing only: whether its window is still open at the
presentation time playback resumes from. Both branches are walked below —
one where the resumption point is the instant the pause began, and one on a
live service where a pause longer than the time-shift buffer moves the
resumption point past the end of the overlay window.

### H.2 What the Publisher declares

One `<Period>` carrying one `<EventStream>` per family, each holding the
window of its family, plus the primary content. The two windows are
authored independently: neither names the other, and the interaction
between them is decided entirely by the Player at runtime.

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:isoff-live:2011"
     type="dynamic"
     availabilityStartTime="2026-09-18T09:00:00Z"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T09:00:00Z"
     minimumUpdatePeriod="PT2S"
     minBufferTime="PT4S"
     timeShiftBufferDepth="PT60S">

  <Period id="p1" start="PT0S">

    <!-- Overlay family. One Event Stream holds every overlay window of
         this Period. -->
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026"
                 timescale="1000">
      <Event id="201" presentationTime="600000" duration="60000">
        <svta:OverlayPresentation
            uri="https://aps.example.com/resolve/overlay?slot=201"
            maxDuration="20000"
            allowedLayouts="overlay-corner overlay-lower-third linear"
            earliestResolutionTimeOffset="60000"/>
      </Event>
    </EventStream>

    <!-- Pause-trigger family. Its own Event Stream, its own scheme. -->
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-pause-trigger:2026"
                 timescale="1000">
      <Event id="301" presentationTime="570000" duration="120000">
        <svta:PauseAdPresentation
            uri="https://aps.example.com/resolve/pause?slot=301"
            maxDuration="30000"
            allowedLayouts="pause-fullscreen pause-partial"
            earliestResolutionTimeOffset="60000"
            executeOnce="true"/>
      </Event>
    </EventStream>

    <!-- Primary content -->
    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video" mimeType="video/mp4"
                   codecs="avc1.4d401f" segmentAlignment="true"
                   startWithSAP="1">
      <SegmentTemplate timescale="1000" duration="2000"
                       initialization="https://cdn.example.com/live/video/init.mp4"
                       media="https://cdn.example.com/live/video/seg_$Number$.m4s"
                       startNumber="1"/>
      <Representation id="v1" bandwidth="3000000" width="1280" height="720"/>
    </AdaptationSet>

  </Period>
</MPD>
```

What the declarations fix, per §5.1:

- The overlay window runs from 600.000 s to 660.000 s. Its Earliest
  Resolution Time is `600000 − 60000 = 540000`, i.e. 540.000 s.
- The overlay cap is 20000 units at a timescale of 1000, so 20.000 s of
  cumulative rendered overlay, and it is enforced by the Player against
  everything that window presents.
- The overlay window admits three layout tokens and nothing else. A
  candidate option naming any other token is one the Player passes over
  for this window.
- The pause window runs from 570.000 s to 690.000 s. Its bounds say
  *where on the timeline a pause triggers a resolution request*; the
  length of the pause itself is set by the viewer.
- The pause cap of 30000 units bounds the display of **one** pause ad
  before automatic dismissal.
- `@executeOnce="true"` on the pause window makes it yield at most one
  pause ad for the session, consumed the moment a pause ad begins
  rendering. The overlay window carries the attribute's default of
  `false`.

Each window binds the candidates it serves with its own allowed layouts
and its own cap, per §4.5. The overlay window's cap never bounds a pause
ad and the pause window's allowed layouts never admit an overlay.

Pause delivery on this service is measured as described in §5.9, by
deriving the paused interval from the base specification's `PlayList`
metric; no XML for that declaration appears here.

### H.3 What the APS returns

Two resolution requests are issued against two different `@uri` values and
two Overlay Resolution Documents come back. Both follow the document shape
of §5.2.

#### H.3.1 The overlay window's document

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT0S"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T09:09:40Z">
  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
    <svta:OverlayList>

      <svta:Candidate id="h-ov-1" duration="PT12S">

        <!-- Preferred: a corner image overlay. -->
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image" layout="overlay-corner"
                              assetUrl="https://cdn.example.com/creatives/h-ov-1-corner.png"/>

        <!-- Last resort: a full-screen takeover carrying the same ad. -->
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="video" layout="linear">
          <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="30.0">https://cdn.example.com/creatives/h-ov-1-takeover.mpd</ImportedMPD>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>

        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=h-ov-1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="2">https://tracker.example.com/start?ad=h-ov-1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="3000"  id="3">https://tracker.example.com/firstQuartile?ad=h-ov-1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="6000"  id="4">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=h-ov-1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="9000"  id="5">https://tracker.example.com/thirdQuartile?ad=h-ov-1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="11960" id="6">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=h-ov-1</Event>
        </EventStream>

        <svta:Click clickThroughUrl="https://advertiser.example.com/h-ov-1">
          <svta:ClickTracking>https://tracker.example.com/click?ad=h-ov-1</svta:ClickTracking>
        </svta:Click>

        <svta:AdSystem>example-ads</svta:AdSystem>
        <svta:AdTitle>Corner card, twelve seconds</svta:AdTitle>
        <svta:Advertiser>Example Brand</svta:Advertiser>
        <svta:UniversalAdId>example.com/H-OV-1</svta:UniversalAdId>

      </svta:Candidate>

    </svta:OverlayList>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

The tracking schedule sits on the candidate and not on either of its two
options, per §5.5: a candidate fires the same beacons whichever of its
options the Player ends up rendering, and beacon times are relative to
that candidate's own presentation. Every beacon time here lies inside the
declared 12 s: the completion beacon is placed one 40 ms frame before the
end rather than on the boundary.

#### H.3.2 The pause window's document

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT0S"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T09:10:07Z">
  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
    <svta:OverlayList onCandidatesExhausted="stop">

      <svta:Candidate id="h-pa-1" duration="PT20S">

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image" layout="pause-partial"
                              assetUrl="https://cdn.example.com/creatives/h-pa-1-panel.jpg"/>

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="video" layout="pause-fullscreen">
          <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="30.0">https://cdn.example.com/creatives/h-pa-1-full.mpd</ImportedMPD>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>

        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=h-pa-1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="5000"  id="2">https://tracker.example.com/firstQuartile?ad=h-pa-1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="10000" id="3">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=h-pa-1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="15000" id="4">https://tracker.example.com/thirdQuartile?ad=h-pa-1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="19960" id="5">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=h-pa-1</Event>
        </EventStream>

        <svta:Click clickThroughUrl="https://advertiser.example.com/h-pa-1">
          <svta:ClickTracking>https://tracker.example.com/click?ad=h-pa-1</svta:ClickTracking>
        </svta:Click>

      </svta:Candidate>

      <svta:Candidate id="h-pa-2" duration="PT15S">

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image" layout="pause-partial"
                              assetUrl="https://cdn.example.com/creatives/h-pa-2-panel.jpg"/>

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image" layout="pause-fullscreen"
                              assetUrl="https://cdn.example.com/creatives/h-pa-2-full.jpg"/>

        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=h-pa-2</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="7500"  id="2">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=h-pa-2</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="14960" id="3">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=h-pa-2</Event>
        </EventStream>

      </svta:Candidate>

    </svta:OverlayList>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

`@onCandidatesExhausted="stop"` is stated explicitly here even though it is
the default, because on a pause slot the attribute is live: the viewer, not
the Publisher, decides when the slot ends, so the document has to say what
happens when the two candidates run out before the viewer resumes. `repeat`
would present the pair again from the start for as long as the pause lasts;
`request-again` would fetch a fresh document for the same pause. With `stop`
the paused primary frame is what the viewer sees for the remainder.

### H.4 The sub-MPDs

Two options across the two documents carry `@form="video"` and therefore
reach their creative through `<ImportedMPD>`. Each imports a Single-Period
Static MPD: one `<Period>` carrying `@duration`, no
`@mediaPresentationDuration`, no `@availabilityStartTime`, and `@mimeType`
on every Representation, per §5.4.

`https://cdn.example.com/creatives/h-ov-1-takeover.mpd` — the overlay
candidate's `linear` option:

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T08:00:00Z">
  <Period id="1" duration="PT12S">
    <BaseURL>https://cdn.example.com/creatives/h-ov-1/</BaseURL>
    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video" mimeType="video/mp4"
                   codecs="avc1.4d401f" segmentAlignment="true"
                   startWithSAP="1">
      <Representation id="v1" bandwidth="2500000" width="1280" height="720">
        <BaseURL>takeover_720p.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-912"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
    <AdaptationSet id="2" contentType="audio" mimeType="audio/mp4"
                   codecs="mp4a.40.2" lang="en">
      <Representation id="a1" bandwidth="128000" audioSamplingRate="48000">
        <BaseURL>takeover_audio.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-784"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

`https://cdn.example.com/creatives/h-pa-1-full.mpd` — the pause
candidate's `pause-fullscreen` option:

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T08:00:00Z">
  <Period id="1" duration="PT20S">
    <BaseURL>https://cdn.example.com/creatives/h-pa-1/</BaseURL>
    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video" mimeType="video/mp4"
                   codecs="avc1.4d401f" segmentAlignment="true"
                   startWithSAP="1">
      <Representation id="v1" bandwidth="2500000" width="1280" height="720">
        <BaseURL>pausead_720p.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-912"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
    <AdaptationSet id="2" contentType="audio" mimeType="audio/mp4"
                   codecs="mp4a.40.2" lang="en">
      <Representation id="a1" bandwidth="128000" audioSamplingRate="48000">
        <BaseURL>pausead_audio.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-784"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

The base specification describes the imported document as restricted to a
single-period profile — DASH §5.3.2.6.1: *"Linked Periods are Periods which
import their content from an external MPD, referred to as imported MPD.
Linked Periods are indicated by the ImportedMPD element in a Period
element. MPDs referenced in the ImportedMPD element shall be restricted to
the constraints of a single period profile as defined in 8.15."* Here the
`<ImportedMPD>` element sits inside a `<svta:RenderableAsset>` rather than
inside a `<Period>`, and it names the creative for one presentation option;
the restriction on what the imported document looks like is the one §5.4
carries.

Neither sub-MPD carries a callback Event Stream. The beacon schedule is a
property of the candidate and lives in the resolution document, so the two
options of one candidate share one schedule.

### H.5 The Player's decision walk, per device class

Two walks run: one over the overlay candidate's two options against the
overlay window's allowed layouts, and one over the pause candidates'
options against the pause window's allowed layouts. Both walk document
order and stop at the first option satisfying both the device and the
window, per §4.5.

#### H.5.1 D1 — two or more video decoders, images on video, HTML on video

*Overlay.* Option 1 is `overlay-corner` with an `image` form. The token is
in the window's allowed list. An image overlay needs one image surface
composited over the playing primary content; D1 composites images on
video. Satisfied — the Player renders it and stops walking. Option 2 is
never reached.

*Pause.* Candidate `h-pa-1`, option 1 is `pause-partial` with an `image`
form. The token is allowed. A partial pause ad leaves the paused frame
visible underneath, so its budget is the overlay budget: one image surface
over video, which D1 has. Satisfied. Candidate `h-pa-2` follows in
sequence, and its option 1 is satisfied on D1 for the same reason.

#### H.5.2 D2 — two video decoders, video on video only, no non-video surface

*Overlay.* Option 1 is an `image` overlay and needs an image surface over
video, which D2 does not composite: passed over. Option 2 is `linear` with
a `video` form — a full-screen takeover reusing the single video pipeline
sequentially, satisfiable on every class, and `linear` is in the window's
allowed list. Satisfied. D2 therefore shows a full-screen takeover where
D1 shows a corner card, from the same candidate.

*Pause.* Candidate `h-pa-1`, option 1 is `pause-partial` with an `image`
form: a non-video surface D2 does not composite, so it is passed over.
Option 2 is `pause-fullscreen` with a `video` form. A full-screen pause ad
replaces the whole surface, so the Player is free to release the primary
content's resources; a video pause ad is satisfiable on every class.
Satisfied. Candidate `h-pa-2` offers `pause-partial` with an `image` form
and `pause-fullscreen` with an `image` form; both need a non-video surface,
so the candidate has no satisfiable option on D2 and the Player moves past
it. With the candidates exhausted and `@onCandidatesExhausted="stop"` in
force, the paused primary frame is what the viewer sees for the rest of
the pause.

#### H.5.3 D3 — one video decoder, images on video, HTML on video

*Overlay.* Option 1 needs one image surface over the playing primary
content and one decoder for that content. D3 has both. Satisfied.

*Pause.* `h-pa-1` option 1, `pause-partial` with an `image` form, needs the
overlay surface budget: satisfied. `h-pa-2` option 1 likewise.

#### H.5.4 D4 — one video decoder, images on video, no HTML on video

Identical to D3 on both walks. Every option reached in this annex is
`image` or `video` in form, and the HTML surface D4 lacks is never asked
for. Overlay: option 1. Pause: `pause-partial` image on both candidates.

#### H.5.5 D5 — one video decoder, no overlay surface of any kind

*Overlay.* Option 1 needs an image surface over video, which D5 does not
composite: passed over. Option 2 is `linear` with a `video` form, needing
one decoder used sequentially and no compositing at all. Satisfied. The
viewer sees a full-screen takeover.

*Pause.* `h-pa-1` option 1 is `pause-partial` and needs a surface over the
paused frame: passed over. Option 2 is `pause-fullscreen` with a `video`
form: the pause ad takes the whole surface, so one decoder suffices.
Satisfied. `h-pa-2` offers only image forms and has no satisfiable option
on D5; the Player moves past it and the paused frame is shown for the
remainder.

#### H.5.6 What the walk produced

| Class | Overlay renders | First pause ad renders | Second pause ad |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | `overlay-corner`, image | `pause-partial`, image | `pause-partial`, image |
| D2 | `linear`, video | `pause-fullscreen`, video | no satisfiable option |
| D3 | `overlay-corner`, image | `pause-partial`, image | `pause-partial`, image |
| D4 | `overlay-corner`, image | `pause-partial`, image | `pause-partial`, image |
| D5 | `linear`, video | `pause-fullscreen`, video | no satisfiable option |

### H.6 The cross-family priority, step by step

The sequence below is written for D1, where the overlay is a corner image
and the pause ad is a partial panel. The D2 and D5 column differs in one
respect worth stating: there the overlay is a `linear` full-screen
takeover occupying the primary content surface, and §7 has the Player
suspend it and resume it from where it stopped exactly as it suspends and
restores an overlay.

At every instant below, one non-linear ad form is active on the screen and
no more. The overlay and the pause ad never share the screen; what changes
is which of the two is the active form.

1. **540.000 s** — the overlay window's Earliest Resolution Time. The
   Player resolves `https://aps.example.com/resolve/overlay?slot=201` at a
   moment of its choosing between this instant and 600.000 s, and receives
   the document of H.3.1.
2. **600.000 s** — the overlay window opens. The Player walks the
   candidate's options, selects `overlay-corner`, and begins rendering.
   The impression and start beacons fire.
3. **603.000 s** — the first-quartile beacon fires, 3.000 s into the
   candidate's own presentation.
4. **607.000 s** — the viewer presses pause. The playhead is inside the
   pause-trigger window, which opened at 570.000 s. The overlay has
   rendered for 7.000 s.
5. The Player suspends the overlay. Its surface is released, its beacon
   schedule holds at 7.000 s, and the primary content's presentation
   timeline stops advancing.
6. The Player resolves `https://aps.example.com/resolve/pause?slot=301`
   and receives the document of H.3.2. The pause window carries
   `@executeOnce="true"`, and the attribute is consumed at the next step,
   when a pause ad begins rendering — not at resolution.
7. The pause ad `h-pa-1` begins rendering as a partial panel over the
   paused primary frame. It is now the only ad surface visible. Its
   impression beacon fires.
8. **+20.000 s of pause** — `h-pa-1` reaches its declared duration and
   ends. Its completion beacon fired 40 ms earlier. `h-pa-2` begins,
   because §7 presents several candidates in one slot in sequence, each
   starting when the previous ends.
9. **+25.000 s of pause** — the viewer presses resume, 5.000 s into
   `h-pa-2`. The Player dismisses `h-pa-2` within one rendering frame and
   its remaining beacons stop: the midpoint beacon at 7500 and the
   completion beacon at 14960 do not fire. The Player returns to the
   primary content immediately, mid-presentation.

What happens next is where the two branches part.

#### H.6.1 Branch A — the overlay window is still open

The resumption point is the presentation time the pause began at,
607.000 s. The overlay window runs to 660.000 s, so
607.000 < 660.000 and the window is open. The Player restores the
overlay, which continues from 7.000 s into its 12 s, and:

- **610.000 s** — the third-quartile beacon fires at 9.000 s into the
  candidate.
- **611.960 s** — the completion beacon fires.
- **612.000 s** — the candidate ends. The overlay slot has accrued
  12.000 s of its 20.000 s cap.
- **660.000 s** — the overlay window closes with no further candidate
  presented.

The overlay ended because its candidate ended, not because of the pause.

#### H.6.2 Branch B — the window expired during the pause

Same sequence to step 9, but the viewer leaves the service paused for 15
minutes on a live stream whose `@timeShiftBufferDepth` is `PT60S`.

While the presentation is paused the Player's presentation time stays
frozen inside the pause window, and that freeze is bounded by the
time-shift buffer. The base specification describes the clipping in
DASH §5.16.2.2.5: *"clipping RT to the timeshift buffer (i.e. the time interval
between TSBS and PHPLE). — Playhead is moved to RT. — QE is reset, as
described in clause 5.16.2.2.3 above."*

The arithmetic on this service:

- Live edge at the moment of pause: about 610.000 s.
- Pause length: 900.000 s of wall clock.
- Live edge at the moment of resume: about 1510.000 s.
- Buffer start at that moment: `1510.000 − 60.000 = 1450.000 s`.
- The resumption point is clipped to the buffer edge: 1450.000 s.

`1450.000 > 660.000`, so the overlay window closed while the viewer was
paused, in the sense that the resumption point lands past its end. The
Player restores no overlay. The candidate's remaining beacons — third
quartile at 9000 and completion at 11960 — stop. The overlay slot ends
having accrued 7.000 s of rendered length against a 20.000 s cap, and the
unspent 13.000 s is not carried anywhere: a cap bounds one slot and
expires with it.

Inside the pause itself, the two candidates ran for 35.000 s of the 900 s
and then the candidate list was exhausted. With `stop`, the paused primary
frame was shown for the remaining 865 s.

### H.7 The arithmetic

**Timescale conversion on the overlay window.** The cap is `20000` in the
parent Event Stream's timescale of 1000, so 20.000 s. The candidate's
declared duration is `PT12S`. Converting into the cap's timescale:
`12 × 1000 = 12000`, already a whole unit, so no rounding applies.
`12000 ≤ 20000` and the candidate is admitted with 8000 units of headroom.
Had a second candidate of `PT8S` followed, its conversion would give
`8000`, the cumulative would be `12000 + 8000 = 20000`, equal to the cap
and therefore admitted.

**The cap runs on the presentation timeline.** The overlay accrued 7.000 s
before the pause. The pause is an interval in which the primary content's
presentation timeline does not advance, so it accrues nothing: the
suspended overlay resumes in branch A with `20000 − 7000 = 13000` units
of cap, the same figure it was suspended with, whether the pause lasted
25 s or 15 minutes. This is what makes the two branches differ in one
respect only. What the long pause changed was the resumption *point*, not
the cap.

**The pause cap bounds one ad, not the slot.** `@maxDuration="30000"` on
the pause window is 30.000 s of display for one pause ad. `h-pa-1`
declares `PT20S`, converting to `20000 ≤ 30000`: admitted and run to its
own end. `h-pa-2` declares `PT15S`, converting to `15000 ≤ 30000`:
admitted. The two together occupied 35.000 s of the pause, which is longer
than the cap and entirely conformant, because the cap was never a bound on
the slot. A candidate declaring `PT45S` would convert to `45000 > 30000`
and be dismissed automatically at 30.000 s of display, its remaining
beacons stopping at that boundary.

**Beacon placement.** Every beacon time in H.3 falls inside its
candidate's declared duration, and the times are relative to that
candidate's own presentation rather than to any `<Period>`. For `h-ov-1`
the schedule spans 0 to 11960 inside `PT12S`; for `h-pa-1`, 0 to 19960
inside `PT20S`; for `h-pa-2`, 0 to 14960 inside `PT15S`.

## Annex I — One ad, ordered options, resolved across the device classes

### I.1 The scenario

One ad is offered for one overlay window. It carries four presentation
options as an ordered list, and document order is the preference order:
first a double box with a video ad and an advertiser background, then an
L-shape with an image creative, then an image banner across the lower
third, then a full-screen takeover of last resort. The Publisher declares one
device-agnostic set of allowed layouts for the window, covering all four
tokens.

Nothing upstream of the Player holds a view of the device. The Player
attaches none of the reserved capability parameters of §5.8 to its
resolution request — no `sgaiVideoDecoders`, no `sgaiImageOverlay`, no
`sgaiHtmlOverlay` — so the APS answers without knowing what the screen can
do, and the ADS decided this ad and these four options for every viewer
alike. There is no device matrix in the manifest and none in the
resolution document. Five device classes nevertheless land on three
different layouts, and they land there because each Player walked the same
ordered list against its own decoder and surface budget. That emergence is
what this annex works through.

### I.2 What the Publisher declares

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:isoff-live:2011"
     type="static"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT45M"
     minBufferTime="PT4S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T10:00:00Z">

  <Period id="p1" start="PT0S">

    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026"
                 timescale="1000">
      <Event id="401" presentationTime="300000" duration="90000">
        <svta:OverlayPresentation
            uri="https://aps.example.com/resolve/overlay?slot=401"
            maxDuration="20000"
            allowedLayouts="squeezeback-double-box-with-background squeezeback-l-shape overlay-lower-third linear"
            earliestResolutionTimeOffset="30000"/>
      </Event>
    </EventStream>

    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video" mimeType="video/mp4"
                   codecs="avc1.4d401f" segmentAlignment="true"
                   startWithSAP="1">
      <SegmentTemplate timescale="1000" duration="4000"
                       initialization="https://cdn.example.com/vod/video/init.mp4"
                       media="https://cdn.example.com/vod/video/seg_$Number$.m4s"
                       startNumber="1"/>
      <Representation id="v1" bandwidth="3000000" width="1280" height="720"/>
    </AdaptationSet>

  </Period>
</MPD>
```

One declaration, four tokens, no device anywhere in it. The window opens at
300.000 s and closes at 390.000 s; its Earliest Resolution Time is
`300000 − 30000 = 270000`, i.e. 270.000 s. The cap is 20000 units at a
timescale of 1000.

The allowed-layout list is the Publisher's whole say in the matter. It
states which surfaces this screen tolerates at this point of the
programme, and it says nothing about which of them any given device can
produce.

### I.3 What the APS returns

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT0S"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T10:04:35Z">
  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
    <svta:OverlayList>

      <svta:Candidate id="i-1" duration="PT19.9999S">

        <!-- 1. Double box, video ad, advertiser background. -->
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="video"
                              layout="squeezeback-double-box-with-background">
          <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="20.0">https://cdn.example.com/creatives/i-1-dbx.mpd</ImportedMPD>
          <svta:BackgroundElement assetUrl="https://cdn.example.com/creatives/i-1-bands.jpg"/>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>

        <!-- 2. L-shape, image creative filling the frame. -->
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image"
                              layout="squeezeback-l-shape"
                              assetUrl="https://cdn.example.com/creatives/i-1-lshape.jpg"/>

        <!-- 3. Image banner across the lower third. -->
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image"
                              layout="overlay-lower-third"
                              assetUrl="https://cdn.example.com/creatives/i-1-banner.png"/>

        <!-- 4. Full-screen takeover, video. Last resort. -->
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="video" layout="linear">
          <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="20.0">https://cdn.example.com/creatives/i-1-takeover.mpd</ImportedMPD>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>

        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=i-1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="2">https://tracker.example.com/start?ad=i-1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="5000"  id="3">https://tracker.example.com/firstQuartile?ad=i-1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="10000" id="4">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=i-1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="15000" id="5">https://tracker.example.com/thirdQuartile?ad=i-1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="19959" id="6">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=i-1</Event>
        </EventStream>

        <svta:Click clickThroughUrl="https://advertiser.example.com/i-1">
          <svta:ClickTracking>https://tracker.example.com/click?ad=i-1</svta:ClickTracking>
        </svta:Click>

        <svta:AdSystem>example-ads</svta:AdSystem>
        <svta:AdTitle>Four ways to show one ad</svta:AdTitle>
        <svta:Advertiser>Example Brand</svta:Advertiser>
        <svta:UniversalAdId>example.com/I-1</svta:UniversalAdId>

      </svta:Candidate>

    </svta:OverlayList>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

Three things about the document are worth naming. `@assetUrl` appears on
options 2 and 3, whose forms are both `image`, and is absent from
options 1 and 4, whose form is `video` and whose creative is reached
through `<ImportedMPD>`. `<svta:BackgroundElement>` appears on option 1 and
nowhere else, because it is admissible only under
`squeezeback-double-box-with-background`. And the one tracking schedule
sits on the candidate, so the same beacons fire at the same offsets
whichever of the four options the device ends up rendering — a Player that
lands on the takeover reports the same ad as a Player that lands on the
double box.

Every one of the four `@layout` values appears in the window's
`@allowedLayouts`, so none of the five classes below is turned away by the
Publisher's declaration. Everything that follows is capability.

### I.4 The sub-MPDs

`https://cdn.example.com/creatives/i-1-dbx.mpd` — the double box's video
creative. It is the ad box only; the shrunk primary content and the
background band are composited by the Player around it.

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T07:00:00Z">
  <Period id="1" duration="PT19.9999S">
    <BaseURL>https://cdn.example.com/creatives/i-1/</BaseURL>
    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video" mimeType="video/mp4"
                   codecs="avc1.4d401e" segmentAlignment="true"
                   startWithSAP="1">
      <Representation id="v1" bandwidth="1200000" width="640" height="360">
        <BaseURL>dbx_360p.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-864"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
    <AdaptationSet id="2" contentType="audio" mimeType="audio/mp4"
                   codecs="mp4a.40.2" lang="en">
      <Representation id="a1" bandwidth="128000" audioSamplingRate="48000">
        <BaseURL>dbx_audio.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-784"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

`https://cdn.example.com/creatives/i-1-takeover.mpd` — the full-screen
takeover, at full frame size.

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T07:00:00Z">
  <Period id="1" duration="PT19.9999S">
    <BaseURL>https://cdn.example.com/creatives/i-1/</BaseURL>
    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video" mimeType="video/mp4"
                   codecs="avc1.4d401f" segmentAlignment="true"
                   startWithSAP="1">
      <Representation id="v1" bandwidth="2500000" width="1280" height="720">
        <BaseURL>takeover_720p.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-912"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
    <AdaptationSet id="2" contentType="audio" mimeType="audio/mp4"
                   codecs="mp4a.40.2" lang="en">
      <Representation id="a1" bandwidth="128000" audioSamplingRate="48000">
        <BaseURL>takeover_audio.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-784"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

Options 2 and 3 import nothing: their creatives are still images fetched
from their `@assetUrl`.

### I.5 The Player's decision walk, per device class

Each class walks options 1 to 4 in document order and stops at the first
one satisfying both the device check and the allowed-layouts check. The
allowed-layouts check passes for all four options on every class, so only
the device check is at issue below. The budgets are those of §3.4.

#### I.5.1 D1 — two or more video decoders, images on video, HTML on video

**Option 1** — `squeezeback-double-box-with-background` with a `video`
form. The shrunk primary content holds one decoder, the ad video takes a
second, and the background image takes an image surface. D1 has two
decoders and composites images on video. Satisfied at the first step; the
Player renders it and the walk ends. Options 2, 3 and 4 are never
evaluated.

*On screen:* the primary content shrinks into one box, the ad video plays
in the other, and the advertiser's image fills the bands the two boxes
leave uncovered.

#### I.5.2 D2 — two video decoders, video on video only, no non-video surface

**Option 1** — the two decoders are available, and this is the instructive
part: D2 has exactly the decoders the double box's two videos need. The
third element is what stops it. The background is a still image and needs
a non-video surface, which D2 does not composite. Passed over. The budget
failure is one of element **type**, not element **count**.

**Option 2** — `squeezeback-l-shape` with an `image` form. The shrunk
primary content holds the one decoder it always costs, and the full-frame
image creative takes a surface instead of a second decoder. D2 composites
no non-video surface. Passed over.

**Option 3** — `overlay-lower-third` with an `image` form. A banner across
the lower third of the playing primary content needs one decoder for that
content plus an image surface. Passed over, for the same reason as option
2: the surface it needs is a non-video one.

**Option 4** — `linear` with a `video` form. The takeover replaces the
primary content on the single surface, so one decoder is reused
sequentially and nothing is composited. Satisfied.

*On screen:* a full-screen video ad, then the primary content resumes.

#### I.5.3 D3 — one video decoder, images on video, HTML on video

**Option 1** — the shrunk primary content costs one decoder and the ad
video costs a second. D3 has one. Passed over, and the background image —
which D3 could have composited — never gets weighed.

**Option 2** — `squeezeback-l-shape` with an `image` form. One decoder for
the shrunk primary content, one image surface for the full-frame creative.
D3 has both. Satisfied, and the walk stops there. Option 3 is equally
within D3's budget — a banner is one image surface over one decoder — and
is never reached, because document order put the L-shape ahead of it.

*On screen:* the primary content shrinks and sits on top of an image
creative filling the whole frame; the band of that creative visible around
the shrunk content is the L.

#### I.5.4 D4 — one video decoder, images on video, no HTML on video

**Option 1** — a second decoder, which D4 does not have. Passed over.

**Option 2** — one decoder plus one image surface. D4 composites images on
video. Satisfied, and the walk stops there. Option 3 is within D4's budget
too, and never reached, for the same reason it is never reached on D3. Had
option 2 carried an `html` form instead of an `image` one, D4 would have
passed it over and landed on option 3 rather than on the takeover, and the
ordering of this list would have produced a different outcome on a device
whose hardware did not change.

*On screen:* the same L-shape D3 shows.

#### I.5.5 D5 — one video decoder, no overlay surface of any kind

**Option 1** — a second decoder and an image surface, neither of which D5
has. Passed over on both counts.

**Option 2** — one image surface for the full-frame creative. Passed over.

**Option 3** — one image surface for the banner. Passed over.

**Option 4** — `linear` with a `video` form: one decoder, no compositing.
Satisfied. This is what makes the takeover the option of last resort on a
non-linear slot — it is the one every class can produce.

*On screen:* the same full-screen video D2 shows, reached by a different
path. D2 ran out of surface types with two decoders in hand; D5 ran out of
everything.

#### I.5.6 What the walk produced

| Class | Option taken | Why the earlier options were passed over |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | 1 — double box + background, video | none |
| D2 | 4 — takeover, video | 1: background needs a non-video surface; 2: image surface; 3: image surface |
| D3 | 2 — L-shape, image | 1: second decoder |
| D4 | 2 — L-shape, image | 1: second decoder |
| D5 | 4 — takeover, video | 1: second decoder and image surface; 2: image surface; 3: image surface |

Three layouts across five classes, from one ordered list emitted
identically to every viewer and one allowed-layout set declared once. The
ADS held no device matrix; the APS held none either, because the
resolution request disclosed no capability. The Publisher declared which
layouts the screen tolerates, and each Player resolved which of them its
hardware produces. The base specification uses order as preference in the
same way for its own fallback lists — DASH §5.11.3: *"If multiple URLs are
provided, the content author expresses the preferences of using one of
those by the order with the first one having the highest preference."*

### I.6 The arithmetic

**Conversion, with rounding up.** The candidate declares
`duration="PT19.9999S"`. The cap is `20000` in the window's Event Stream
timescale of 1000. Converting the candidate into that timescale:
`19.9999 × 1000 = 19999.9` units, which is not a whole unit, so it rounds
up to `20000`.

**Equality is admitted.** The converted duration is `20000` and the cap is
`20000`. A converted duration equal to the cap is admitted, so the
candidate plays in full. Had the rounding been done downward the figure
would have been `19999` and the comparison would have passed for a
different reason; rounding up is what makes the boundary case a decision
rather than an accident.

**Cumulative headroom.** This candidate consumes the whole 20000, so a
second candidate in the same document would be dropped before playback
whatever its duration.

**The arithmetic is the same on all five classes.** The declared duration
is a presentation-timeline value for every form, so the still images of
options 2 and 3 are bounded by the same `PT19.9999S` as the video of
options 1 and 4, and all five classes spend the same 20000 units of cap.
A device that plays at a speed other than the primary content's
presentation speed does not change the figure either: the on-screen
wall-clock length is derived as the declared duration divided by the
playback speed, and cap enforcement and beacon scheduling stay on the
presentation timeline.

**Beacon placement.** The schedule spans 0 to 19959 inside a declared
`PT19.9999S`, so every beacon time falls inside the candidate's own
presentation with the completion beacon one 40 ms frame short of the end.

## Annex J — Side-by-side / double box, the three-element layout

### J.1 The scenario

The double box puts three elements on the screen at once: the primary
content shrunk into one box, the ad in the other, and — when the
advertiser supplies one — a still image filling the bands the two boxes
leave uncovered on a 16:9 frame. Two layout tokens describe the pair.
`squeezeback-double-box-with-background` carries the background image as a
composition attribute of the layout; `squeezeback-double-box` is the same
arrangement without it, and the uncovered bands render as black.

The resolution document below shows both tokens inside one candidate, so
the difference between them is visible in a single document and is a
difference in what the layout composites rather than in what the ad is.
The background is never a presentation option: the Player does not walk it
the way it walks the ordered options, it composites it as part of
rendering the layout once that layout has been chosen. It is also always a
still image, never video and never HTML, which is what decides the device
walk below — a layout with a background asks for an image surface on top
of whatever the ad itself asks for, so a class that composites no
non-video surface never produces it, whatever form the ad takes.

Where inside the layout the boxes and the bands sit is not declared here.
Spatial arrangement is delegated to the IAB-defined layout and to
HTML5/CSS, and §1 records it among the exclusions.

### J.2 What the Publisher declares

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:isoff-live:2011"
     type="static"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT30M"
     minBufferTime="PT4S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T11:00:00Z">

  <Period id="p1" start="PT0S">

    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026"
                 timescale="1000">
      <Event id="501" presentationTime="120000" duration="120000">
        <svta:OverlayPresentation
            uri="https://aps.example.com/resolve/overlay?slot=501"
            maxDuration="45000"
            allowedLayouts="squeezeback-double-box-with-background squeezeback-double-box"
            earliestResolutionTimeOffset="60000"/>
      </Event>
    </EventStream>

    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video" mimeType="video/mp4"
                   codecs="avc1.4d401f" segmentAlignment="true"
                   startWithSAP="1">
      <SegmentTemplate timescale="1000" duration="4000"
                       initialization="https://cdn.example.com/vod2/video/init.mp4"
                       media="https://cdn.example.com/vod2/video/seg_$Number$.m4s"
                       startNumber="1"/>
      <Representation id="v1" bandwidth="3000000" width="1280" height="720"/>
    </AdaptationSet>

  </Period>
</MPD>
```

The window opens at 120.000 s and closes at 240.000 s, with an Earliest
Resolution Time of `120000 − 60000 = 60000`. It admits the two double-box
tokens and nothing else: this Publisher wants the primary content to stay
on screen throughout, so no takeover of last resort is on offer here. The
cap is 45000 units at a timescale of 1000.

### J.3 What the APS returns

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT0S"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T11:01:12Z">
  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
    <svta:OverlayList>

      <!-- Video ad. Both tokens, background first. -->
      <svta:Candidate id="j-1" duration="PT20S">

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="video"
                              layout="squeezeback-double-box-with-background">
          <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="30.0">https://cdn.example.com/creatives/j-1-box.mpd</ImportedMPD>
          <svta:BackgroundElement assetUrl="https://cdn.example.com/creatives/j-1-bands.jpg"/>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="video" layout="squeezeback-double-box">
          <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="30.0">https://cdn.example.com/creatives/j-1-box.mpd</ImportedMPD>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>

        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=j-1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="5000"  id="2">https://tracker.example.com/firstQuartile?ad=j-1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="10000" id="3">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=j-1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="15000" id="4">https://tracker.example.com/thirdQuartile?ad=j-1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="19960" id="5">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=j-1</Event>
        </EventStream>

        <svta:Click clickThroughUrl="https://advertiser.example.com/j-1">
          <svta:ClickTracking>https://tracker.example.com/click?ad=j-1</svta:ClickTracking>
        </svta:Click>

        <svta:AdSystem>example-ads</svta:AdSystem>
        <svta:AdTitle>Double box, moving</svta:AdTitle>
        <svta:Advertiser>Example Brand</svta:Advertiser>

      </svta:Candidate>

      <!-- Image ad. Both tokens again. -->
      <svta:Candidate id="j-2" duration="PT25S">

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image"
                              layout="squeezeback-double-box-with-background"
                              assetUrl="https://cdn.example.com/creatives/j-2-box.png">
          <svta:BackgroundElement assetUrl="https://cdn.example.com/creatives/j-2-bands.jpg"/>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image" layout="squeezeback-double-box"
                              assetUrl="https://cdn.example.com/creatives/j-2-box.png"/>

        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=j-2</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="12500" id="2">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=j-2</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="24960" id="3">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=j-2</Event>
        </EventStream>

        <svta:Click clickThroughUrl="https://advertiser.example.com/j-2">
          <svta:ClickTracking>https://tracker.example.com/click?ad=j-2</svta:ClickTracking>
        </svta:Click>

      </svta:Candidate>

      <!-- A short third, for the cap arithmetic. -->
      <svta:Candidate id="j-3" duration="PT10S">

        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image" layout="squeezeback-double-box"
                              assetUrl="https://cdn.example.com/creatives/j-3-box.png"/>

        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"    id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=j-3</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="9960" id="2">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=j-3</Event>
        </EventStream>

      </svta:Candidate>

    </svta:OverlayList>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

`<svta:BackgroundElement>` appears under the two
`squeezeback-double-box-with-background` options and under nothing else. On
`j-1` the two options import the same sub-MPD and differ only in the token
and in the presence of the background: same ad, same creative, same
beacons, two ways of filling the bands.

### J.4 The sub-MPD

`https://cdn.example.com/creatives/j-1-box.mpd`, imported by both of
`j-1`'s options. It carries the ad box alone, at the box's own resolution;
the shrunk primary content and the bands are the Player's composition.

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T06:00:00Z">
  <Period id="1" duration="PT20S">
    <BaseURL>https://cdn.example.com/creatives/j-1/</BaseURL>
    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video" mimeType="video/mp4"
                   codecs="avc1.4d401e" segmentAlignment="true"
                   startWithSAP="1">
      <Representation id="v1" bandwidth="1200000" width="640" height="360">
        <BaseURL>box_360p.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-864"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
    <AdaptationSet id="2" contentType="audio" mimeType="audio/mp4"
                   codecs="mp4a.40.2" lang="en">
      <Representation id="a1" bandwidth="128000" audioSamplingRate="48000">
        <BaseURL>box_audio.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-784"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

`j-2` and `j-3` import nothing: their creatives are still images fetched
from `@assetUrl`, and the background images likewise.

### J.5 The Player's decision walk, per device class

Both tokens are in the window's allowed list, so every option below passes
the allowed-layouts check and the device check decides alone. The budgets
are those of §3.4: the shrunk primary content always costs one decoder; a
`video` ad box costs a second; an `image` or `html` ad box costs a surface
instead; and a background costs one image surface on top of that.
Candidates are presented in sequence, so a candidate with no satisfiable
option is passed over and the next one is evaluated on its own terms.

#### J.5.1 D1 — two or more video decoders, images on video, HTML on video

`j-1` option 1: one decoder for the shrunk primary content, a second for
the ad video, an image surface for the background. All three available.
Satisfied, and the walk stops at the first option.

`j-2` option 1: one decoder for the shrunk primary content, an image
surface for the ad box, an image surface for the background. Satisfied.

`j-3`: see the cap arithmetic in J.6 — it is dropped before playback on
this class.

*On screen:* the primary content in one box, the ad in the other, the
advertiser's image in the bands, twice in a row.

#### J.5.2 D2 — two video decoders, video on video only, no non-video surface

`j-1` option 1: the two decoders are there. The background is a still
image and needs a non-video surface, which D2 does not composite. Passed
over. This is the whole point of the layout's budget — the decoder count
was never the obstacle.

`j-1` option 2: `squeezeback-double-box` without a background. One decoder
for the shrunk primary content, a second for the ad video, and no
non-video element anywhere in the composition. Satisfied.

`j-2`: both options carry an `image` ad box, which needs an image surface.
Both passed over; the candidate has no satisfiable option and the Player
moves to the next.

`j-3`: an `image` ad box again. Passed over. The candidates are exhausted
and the primary content continues at full frame for the rest of the
window.

*On screen:* the primary content in one box, the ad video in the other,
and black bands where D1 shows the advertiser's image. Then the primary
content returns to full frame.

#### J.5.3 D3 — one video decoder, images on video, HTML on video

`j-1` option 1: the shrunk primary content takes the one decoder D3 has,
and the ad video needs a second. Passed over. Option 2 needs the same
second decoder and is passed over for the same reason; dropping the
background changed nothing, because the background was not what D3 lacked.
The candidate has no satisfiable option.

`j-2` option 1: one decoder for the shrunk primary content, an image
surface for the ad box, an image surface for the background. D3 has all
three. Satisfied.

`j-3`: one decoder, one image surface, no background. Satisfied, and the
cap admits it — see J.6.

*On screen:* the video ad of `j-1` is never shown; the image double box of
`j-2` is, with the advertiser's bands, followed by `j-3`.

#### J.5.4 D4 — one video decoder, images on video, no HTML on video

Identical to D3 throughout. Every ad box and every background in this
document is `video` or `image` in form, and the HTML surface D4 lacks is
never asked for: `j-1` is passed over on the second decoder, `j-2` renders
with its background, `j-3` follows. Had `j-2`'s first option carried an
`html` ad box, D4 would have passed it over and fallen to its second
option — and the bands would have rendered black on D4 while D3 showed the
advertiser's image.

#### J.5.5 D5 — one video decoder, no overlay surface of any kind

`j-1` option 1: a second decoder and an image surface, neither available.
Option 2: a second decoder. Both passed over.

`j-2` option 1: an image surface for the ad box and another for the
background. Option 2: an image surface for the ad box. Both passed over.

`j-3`: an image surface for the ad box. Passed over.

The candidates are exhausted with nothing rendered, and the primary
content plays uninterrupted through the whole window. This is the case the
layout's budget makes unavoidable: the double box composites the primary
content and the ad side by side by construction, so a class that
composites nothing alongside video produces neither variant, and the
window is left unfilled.

#### J.5.6 What the walk produced

| Class | `j-1` | `j-2` | `j-3` |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | option 1 — video box, advertiser bands | option 1 — image box, advertiser bands | dropped by the cap |
| D2 | option 2 — video box, black bands | no satisfiable option | no satisfiable option |
| D3 | no satisfiable option | option 1 — image box, advertiser bands | option 1 — image box, black bands |
| D4 | no satisfiable option | option 1 — image box, advertiser bands | option 1 — image box, black bands |
| D5 | no satisfiable option | no satisfiable option | no satisfiable option |

The background is what separates the D1 row from the D2 row on `j-1`, and
the second decoder is what separates the D2 row from the D3 row. Those are
two different budgets and they fail independently.

### J.6 The arithmetic

The cap is `45000` units at a timescale of 1000, so 45.000 s of cumulative
rendered length, and it accrues what the window actually presents.

**D1.** `j-1` converts as `20 × 1000 = 20000`; `20000 ≤ 45000`, admitted,
cumulative 20000. `j-2` converts as `25000`; the cumulative would be
`20000 + 25000 = 45000`, equal to the cap and therefore admitted,
cumulative 45000. `j-3` converts as `10000`; the cumulative would be
`45000 + 10000 = 55000`, past the cap, so the Player drops the candidate
before playback and its beacons never fire.

**D3 and D4.** `j-1` rendered nothing, so it accrued nothing: the cap
tracks rendered length, not the declared duration of candidates that were
passed over. `j-2` accrues 25000. `j-3` would bring the cumulative to
`25000 + 10000 = 35000`, inside the cap, so it is admitted and rendered.
The same document therefore ends with `j-3` shown on D3 and D4 and dropped
on D1, from one cap and one set of declared durations.

**D2.** `j-1` accrues 20000 and the other two have no satisfiable option.
The window closes with 25000 units of cap unspent, which is not carried
anywhere.

**D5.** Nothing accrues.

**A candidate that overruns.** Every duration here is declared honestly.
Had `j-2` declared `PT25S` and its image stayed on screen past 25.000 s,
the Player would trim it at the cap boundary mid-render and stop its
remaining beacons there; the trim is what keeps the cumulative figure
true.

**Beacon placement.** Each schedule lies inside its candidate's declared
duration: 0 to 19960 within `PT20S` for `j-1`, 0 to 24960 within `PT25S`
for `j-2`, 0 to 9960 within `PT10S` for `j-3`. The times are relative to
each candidate's own presentation, and the identifier `1` recurring across
the three candidates is three distinct beacons, since de-duplication is
scoped to the candidate.

## Annex K — ClickThrough

### K.1 The scenario

An ad is on screen in a non-linear slot. Its resolution document carries
the ad's ClickThrough destination together with the click-tracking URLs
that accompany it. The viewer activates the click — a select on a CTV
remote, a tap on a phone — and at that moment the Player opens, or hands
off to the platform, the destination and fires each accompanying
click-tracking URL once. The ClickThrough travels with the ad and not
with the slot: it arrives inside the resolution document, alongside the
creative that carries it, and the window declaration says nothing about
it. Of the two candidates below, the first carries two click-tracking
URLs and the second none.

### K.2 What the Publisher declares

<!-- refine: v9-dash-conformance-audit.md#N2 -->
```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:isoff-live:2011"
     type="static"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT42M"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T09:00:00Z">
  <Period id="p1" start="PT0S">
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026"
                 timescale="1000">
      <Event id="1101" presentationTime="900000" duration="30000">
        <svta:OverlayPresentation
            uri="https://aps.example.com/decision/overlay?slot=1101"
            earliestResolutionTimeOffset="20000"
            maxDuration="25000"
            allowedLayouts="overlay-lower-third overlay-corner linear"/>
      </Event>
    </EventStream>
    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video" mimeType="video/mp4"
                   codecs="avc1.4d401f" segmentAlignment="true"
                   startWithSAP="1" par="16:9">
      <SegmentTemplate timescale="90000" duration="360000" startNumber="1"
                       initialization="$RepresentationID$/init.mp4"
                       media="$RepresentationID$/seg-$Number$.m4s"/>
      <Representation id="v-720" bandwidth="2500000"
                      width="1280" height="720" sar="1:1"/>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

The window caps the slot at 25 000 in the stream's millisecond timescale
and admits three layouts. Nothing in it mentions clicks.

### K.3 What the APS returns

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT0S"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T09:14:41Z">
  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
    <svta:OverlayList>
      <svta:Candidate id="cand-k1" duration="PT15S">
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image"
                              layout="overlay-lower-third"
                              assetUrl="https://adcdn.example.com/k1/strip.png"/>
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="html"
                              layout="overlay-corner"
                              assetUrl="https://adcdn.example.com/k1/corner.html"/>
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="video" layout="linear">
          <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="10.0">https://adcdn.example.com/k1/takeover.mpd</ImportedMPD>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>
        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=k1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="7500"  id="2">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=k1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="15000" id="3">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=k1</Event>
        </EventStream>
        <svta:Click clickThroughUrl="https://advertiser.example.com/autumn?utm_source=ctv">
          <svta:ClickTracking>https://tracker.example.com/click?ad=k1</svta:ClickTracking>
          <svta:ClickTracking>https://thirdparty.example.com/c?cid=k1</svta:ClickTracking>
        </svta:Click>
        <svta:AdSystem>example-ads</svta:AdSystem>
        <svta:AdTitle>Autumn campaign — lower third</svta:AdTitle>
      </svta:Candidate>
      <svta:Candidate id="cand-k2" duration="PT10S">
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image"
                              layout="overlay-corner"
                              assetUrl="https://adcdn.example.com/k2/corner.png"/>
        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=k2</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="10000" id="2">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=k2</Event>
        </EventStream>
        <svta:Click clickThroughUrl="https://offers.example.com/spring"/>
      </svta:Candidate>
    </svta:OverlayList>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

One `<svta:Click>` per candidate, sitting beside the presentation
options rather than inside one, so the destination is a property of the
ad and survives whichever option is selected: the same landing page is
reached from the lower third, from the corner and from the takeover.
Each candidate's completion beacon sits at the final instant of its
declared duration.

### K.4 The sub-MPD the video option imports

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T09:14:41Z">
  <Period id="1" duration="PT15S">
    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video" mimeType="video/mp4"
                   codecs="avc1.4d401f" segmentAlignment="true"
                   startWithSAP="1">
      <Representation id="v1" bandwidth="2500000" width="1280" height="720">
        <BaseURL>https://adcdn.example.com/k1/takeover-v1.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-912"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
    <AdaptationSet id="2" contentType="audio" mimeType="audio/mp4"
                   codecs="mp4a.40.2" lang="en" segmentAlignment="true">
      <Representation id="a1" bandwidth="128000" audioSamplingRate="48000">
        <BaseURL>https://adcdn.example.com/k1/takeover-a1.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-708"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

The sub-MPD carries the media and nothing else: the candidate holds the
tracking schedule and the click, so a callback stream here would be a
second copy of a schedule the Player is already running.

### K.5 Two kinds of tracking, two carriers

| | Timeline beacons | Click-tracking |
|---|---|---|
| Carrier | `<Event>` entries in a callback `<EventStream>` (§5.5) | `<svta:ClickTracking>` inside `<svta:Click>` (§5.6) |
| Trigger | The playhead reaching a scheduled presentation time | The viewer activating the click |
| Timing | `@presentationTime`, relative to the candidate's own first rendered frame | None: it fires when the viewer acts, or never |
| Fires when the viewer does nothing | Yes | No |
| Who declares it | The ADS, transcribed by the APS | The advertiser, carried by the APS |

The split follows from what the base specification's event machinery
schedules against:
*"Events are timed, i.e. each event starts at a specific media
presentation time"* (DASH §5.10.1). A click activation has no presentation
time to reach — it may never occur — so the callback scheme carries
impressions and quartiles and a carrier of its own carries the click.

### K.6 The walk-through

1. **The window resolves.** At or after 880 000 the Player issues
   `GET https://aps.example.com/decision/overlay?slot=1101` and receives
   the document of §K.3. Both candidates' layouts are in the window's
   `@allowedLayouts`, and their durations convert to 15 000 and 10 000
   against the cap of 25 000, so both fit and present in document order,
   one at a time.

2. **The beacons run on the timeline.** For `cand-k1` the Player fires
   the impression at its first rendered frame, the midpoint at 7 500 and
   the completion at 15 000, each measured on the candidate's own
   presentation. The response of each is discarded — *"the client is
   expected to entirely ignore the response"* (DASH §5.10.4.5.1).

3. **The viewer activates the click at 6 200 into `cand-k1`.** The
   Player opens, or hands to the platform, the value of
   `@clickThroughUrl`, and fires both `<svta:ClickTracking>` URLs once.
   The timeline beacons are untouched: the midpoint still fires at
   7 500 and the completion at 15 000.

4. **The viewer does not activate the click on `cand-k2`.** Its
   ClickThrough never fires and its two timeline beacons fire as
   scheduled — the whole of the difference between the two carriers.

A Player conformant to this specification reads `@clickThroughUrl` and
fires each `<svta:ClickTracking>` URL once on activation (§4.5, §5.6).
Scoping the guarantee to conformant Players is the only scoping
available to an extension of the base specification, which leaves client
behaviour unspecified: *"as DASH Client operation is not specified
normatively in this document ... profiles merely specify restrictions on
MPD and Segments rather than DASH Client behaviour"* (§8.1, NOTE 1). A
Player that predates this specification removes the foreign-namespace
element with the rest of the extension content (base specification,
§5.2.1) and has nothing to activate; the click is inert.

### K.7 A ClickThrough with no click-tracking

`cand-k2` carries `<svta:Click>` with no `<svta:ClickTracking>` child:
the Player opens the destination and fires nothing. Whether a
ClickThrough carries any click-tracking is the advertiser's decision, and
the empty form is a complete declaration rather than a degraded one.

### K.8 Device classes

Which option renders varies by class; what happens on activation does
not. Activation needs an input mechanism — a remote select, a tap — and
consults none of the three axes that separate D1 from D5 (§3.4).

| Class | `cand-k1` renders | `cand-k2` renders | On activation |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | option 1, image lower third | yes, image corner | Destination opened, both trackers fired |
| D2 | option 3, `linear` takeover | skipped: no image surface | Identical |
| D3 | option 1, image lower third | yes, image corner | Identical |
| D4 | option 1, image lower third | yes, image corner | Identical |
| D5 | option 3, `linear` takeover | skipped: no overlay surface | Identical |

Wherever an ad is on the screen, the five classes read the same carrier,
open the same destination and fire the same URLs.
