## Annex L — Overlapping windows of the same family, with fallback

### L.1 The scenario

Three overlay windows overlap in time in one Period of the main MPD.
They are windows of one family, so they are not three concurrent
opportunities: they are a chain. The Player serves the first of them and
reaches the second only when the attempt on the first produced no ad;
the third stands behind the second on the same terms. Exactly one window
is served, and the ones behind it are never requested once one in front
has been answered with candidates.

### L.2 What the Publisher declares

All windows of one family in one Period are authored as `<Event>`
entries inside a **single** `<EventStream>`, following the base
specification's rule that *"all Events of one type shall be clustered in
one Event Stream"* (DASH §5.10.2.1). Two sibling streams carrying the same
`@schemeIdUri` in one `<Period>` is therefore not the shape, and it
would also leave the tie-break of §L.3 undefined: document position
orders events inside one stream and says nothing across two. The three
`@uri` values point at three different APS endpoints, which is the
ordinary reason to declare a chain.

<!-- refine: v9-dash-conformance-audit.md#N2 -->
```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:isoff-live:2011"
     type="static"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT42M"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T10:00:00Z">
  <Period id="p1" start="PT0S">
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026"
                 timescale="1000">
      <!-- Served first: earliest presentation time. -->
      <Event id="1201" presentationTime="600000" duration="30000">
        <svta:OverlayPresentation
            uri="https://aps-a.example.com/decision/overlay?slot=1201"
            earliestResolutionTimeOffset="20000"
            maxDuration="15000"
            allowedLayouts="overlay-lower-third overlay-corner"/>
      </Event>
      <!-- First backup. Ties with 1203 on presentation time and stands
           ahead of it by position in this stream. -->
      <Event id="1202" presentationTime="605000" duration="25000">
        <svta:OverlayPresentation
            uri="https://aps-b.example.com/decision/overlay?slot=1202"
            earliestResolutionTimeOffset="20000"
            maxDuration="10000"
            allowedLayouts="overlay-corner"/>
      </Event>
      <!-- Second backup: admits the full-screen takeover as the option
           of last resort, with the tightest cap. -->
      <Event id="1203" presentationTime="605000" duration="25000">
        <svta:OverlayPresentation
            uri="https://aps-c.example.com/decision/overlay?slot=1203"
            earliestResolutionTimeOffset="20000"
            maxDuration="8000"
            allowedLayouts="overlay-corner linear"/>
      </Event>
    </EventStream>
    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video" mimeType="video/mp4"
                   codecs="avc1.4d401f" segmentAlignment="true"
                   startWithSAP="1" par="16:9">
      <SegmentTemplate timescale="90000" duration="360000" startNumber="1"
                       initialization="$RepresentationID$/init.mp4"
                       media="$RepresentationID$/seg-$Number$.m4s"/>
      <Representation id="v-720" bandwidth="2500000"
                      width="1280" height="720" sar="1:1"/>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

### L.3 The order the Player takes them in

Presentation time, oldest first: 1201 at 600 000 stands ahead of the
other two. Position inside the `<EventStream>` separates the two that
declare 605 000 — events are processed *"in the order they appear in the
EventStream element"* (DASH §5.10.2.1) — so the order is 1201, 1202, 1203,
fixed at authoring time by the two declarations already present. No
attribute declares the chain: the overlap plus the shared family is the
declaration. The windows differ in what they admit and in what they cap
— `overlay-lower-third` only under 1201, `linear` only under 1203, caps
of 15 000, 10 000 and 8 000 — and §L.7 turns on those differences.

### L.4 The walk — one failure mode per attempt

**Attempt 1 — the APS does not respond.**

```
GET https://aps-a.example.com/decision/overlay?slot=1201
→ connection timed out, no response
```

Nothing was obtained, so the attempt produced no ad. It is a failed
execution and the Player moves to 1202.

**Attempt 2 — a final HTTP status other than `200`.**

```
GET https://aps-b.example.com/decision/overlay?slot=1202
→ 503 Service Unavailable
```

A transport failure and a non-`200` are different events on the wire and
the same thing to the chain. The Player moves to 1203.

**Attempt 3 — `200` with a body that does not parse.**

```
GET https://aps-c.example.com/decision/overlay?slot=1203
→ 200 OK, Content-Type: application/dash+xml
```

```
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
```

The response arrived with a body, and the body is not a document: the
root element is unterminated and the parse fails. The base specification
groups this with the rest — *"Alternative MPD is unavailable or
invalid"* (DASH §5.16.2.2.6) — and the chain treats it alike.

**The chain is exhausted.** Every window has been attempted and none
produced an ad, so the Player continues with the primary content, which
never stopped: *"If no event can be successfully executed, the playback
continues uninterrupted"* (DASH §5.16.2.2.5). The viewer sees an
uninterrupted programme and no artefact of any kind.

No window was consumed by any of it. The base specification counts
executions rather than attempts: *"counter E.c has not been incremented
due to the failure, consequently if E.c = 0 the event can still be
executed in the future even if the value of @executeOnce is "true""*
(DASH §5.16.2.2.6, NOTE 3). An opportunity that produced no ad remains
available later in the session.

### L.5 Variant — a well-formed document of the wrong family

The walk restarts. This time 1201 answers `200` with a document that
parses cleanly and is a resolution document for the **linear** family:

```
GET https://aps-a.example.com/decision/overlay?slot=1201
→ 200 OK
```

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:list:2024"
     type="list"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T10:09:12Z">
  <Period id="ad-1" duration="PT15S">
    <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="10.0">https://adcdn.example.com/x1/ad.mpd</ImportedMPD>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

An overlay window was asked and a `ListMPD` came back. It is well-formed
and valid on its own terms, and it answers a different question: periods
to play in sequence rather than candidates to choose among, with nothing
in it to check against the window's `@allowedLayouts`. The window was
not resolved, so the attempt is a failed execution and the chain
advances to 1202, which answers with an overlay candidate:

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT0S"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T10:09:13Z">
  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
    <svta:OverlayList>
      <svta:Candidate id="cand-b1" duration="PT8S">
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image"
                              layout="overlay-corner"
                              assetUrl="https://adcdn.example.com/b1/corner.png"/>
        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"    id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=b1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="8000" id="2">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=b1</Event>
        </EventStream>
        <svta:Click clickThroughUrl="https://advertiser.example.com/b1"/>
      </svta:Candidate>
    </svta:OverlayList>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

The Player validates that candidate against **1202**: `overlay-corner`
is in that window's `@allowedLayouts`, and `PT8S` converts to 8 000
against its cap of 10 000. It renders on any class that composites an
image over video, and 1203 is never requested.

Wrong family and empty resolution arrive with the same status code and
say opposite things: the first leaves the window unresolved, the second
is a resolution that sold nothing. Both advance the chain, and only the
second tells the Publisher that the market was asked.

### L.6 Variant — a document carrying no candidates

The walk restarts again. Both 1201 and 1202 answer `200` with the empty
resolution:

```
GET https://aps-a.example.com/decision/overlay?slot=1201
→ 200 OK
```

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT0S"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T10:22:04Z">
  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
    <svta:OverlayList/>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

The opportunity was offered and nothing was sold. That is a complete,
well-formed answer, served `200` with a body, and still an attempt that
produced no ad, so it is a failed execution and the chain advances. 1202
answers the same way, and the chain reaches 1203, the one window of the
three that admits `linear`:

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT0S"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T10:22:06Z">
  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
    <svta:OverlayList>
      <svta:Candidate id="cand-c1" duration="PT8S">
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image"
                              layout="overlay-corner"
                              assetUrl="https://adcdn.example.com/c1/corner.png"/>
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="video" layout="linear">
          <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="5.0">https://adcdn.example.com/c1/takeover.mpd</ImportedMPD>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>
        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"    id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=c1</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="8000" id="2">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=c1</Event>
        </EventStream>
      </svta:Candidate>
    </svta:OverlayList>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

The candidate's second option is the takeover, and its sub-MPD is:

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024"
     type="static"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T10:22:06Z">
  <Period id="1" duration="PT8S">
    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video" mimeType="video/mp4"
                   codecs="avc1.4d401f" segmentAlignment="true"
                   startWithSAP="1">
      <Representation id="v1" bandwidth="2500000" width="1280" height="720">
        <BaseURL>https://adcdn.example.com/c1/takeover-v1.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-880"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
    <AdaptationSet id="2" contentType="audio" mimeType="audio/mp4"
                   codecs="mp4a.40.2" lang="en" segmentAlignment="true">
      <Representation id="a1" bandwidth="128000" audioSamplingRate="48000">
        <BaseURL>https://adcdn.example.com/c1/takeover-a1.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-690"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

`PT8S` converts to 8 000, equal to 1203's cap exactly, and is admitted.
A class that composites an image over video renders the first option;
one that composites nothing over video passes it over and renders the
takeover — the reason the last link in a chain is worth authoring with
`linear` in its allowed set.

### L.7 Each window binds what it serves with its own declarations

A window carries its own `@allowedLayouts` and its own `@maxDuration`,
and the candidates it serves are validated against those values. A
window inherits nothing from the window it stands in for. Take
`cand-b1` of §L.5 and a second candidate offering `overlay-lower-third`
at `PT12S`:

| Candidate | Under 1201 (`overlay-lower-third overlay-corner`, cap 15 000) | Under 1202 (`overlay-corner`, cap 10 000) | Under 1203 (`overlay-corner linear`, cap 8 000) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `overlay-corner`, `PT8S` | admitted | admitted | admitted, at the cap exactly |
| `overlay-lower-third`, `PT12S` | admitted | layout not in the set; 12 000 over the cap | layout not in the set; 12 000 over the cap |

The same document is therefore worth different things depending on which
window obtained it. This is a Player obligation and not an illustration:
the check runs against the declarations of the window that served the
document (§4.5, §7).

### L.8 Device classes

Which window is served does not vary across D1 to D5: the chain is
decided by presentation times, by document position and by what each
attempt returned, and none of the axes that separate the classes is
consulted while it is walked. What the class changes is which option
inside the winning document renders — §L.6 shows both outcomes — after
the chain has already ended.

## Annex M — One ad, Player-declared capabilities, resolved by the APS

### M.1 The scenario

One non-linear candidate is offered for one overlay slot. Behind the APS
the ADS holds four presentation options in a fixed order: a double-box
squeezeback with a video ad and an advertiser background; an L-shape
squeezeback with a still-image full-frame creative; an image banner in
the lower third; and a full-screen takeover video. The Publisher
declares one device-agnostic allowed-layout set that admits all four.

What varies here is where the capability check is resolved. Each Player
attaches the reserved capability parameters it is willing to disclose to
the resolution request (§5.8.2), and the APS answers with a candidate
carrying **exactly one** option — except for the class that discloses
nothing, which gets the full ordered list. The Player checks whatever
arrives before rendering it.

### M.2 What the Publisher declares

<!-- refine: v9-dash-conformance-audit.md#N2 -->
```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:isoff-live:2011"
     type="static"
     mediaPresentationDuration="PT42M"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T11:00:00Z">
  <Period id="p1" start="PT0S">
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026"
                 timescale="1000">
      <Event id="901" presentationTime="480000" duration="20000">
        <svta:OverlayPresentation
            uri="https://aps.example.com/decision/overlay?slot=901"
            earliestResolutionTimeOffset="15000"
            maxDuration="15000"
            allowedLayouts="squeezeback-double-box-with-background squeezeback-l-shape overlay-lower-third linear"/>
      </Event>
    </EventStream>
    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video" mimeType="video/mp4"
                   codecs="avc1.4d401f" segmentAlignment="true"
                   startWithSAP="1" par="16:9">
      <SegmentTemplate timescale="90000" duration="360000" startNumber="1"
                       initialization="$RepresentationID$/init.mp4"
                       media="$RepresentationID$/seg-$Number$.m4s"/>
      <Representation id="v-720" bandwidth="2500000"
                      width="1280" height="720" sar="1:1"/>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

Nothing in the manifest asks for a capability parameter, and nothing has
to: which reserved parameters travel is the Player's own runtime
decision. Query parameters the Publisher wants on the request travel by
a separate mechanism (§5.8), drawing their names from outside the
reserved set.

### M.3 Five requests, five answers

**D1 — discloses everything, and one parameter of its own.**

```
GET /decision/overlay?slot=901&sgaiVideoDecoders=2&sgaiImageOverlay=true&sgaiHtmlOverlay=true&x-acme-panelDiagonalCm=165
Host: aps.example.com
```

`x-acme-panelDiagonalCm` is not a reserved parameter, so it carries the
`x-<vendor>-` prefix that keeps it clear of the reserved names.

<!-- refine: v9-detail-review.md#flag-4 -->
```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
     type="static" minBufferTime="PT0S" mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T11:12:40Z">
  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
    <svta:OverlayList>
      <svta:Candidate id="cand-901" duration="PT15S">
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="video"
                              layout="squeezeback-double-box-with-background">
          <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="10.0">https://adcdn.example.com/901/box.mpd</ImportedMPD>
          <svta:BackgroundElement assetUrl="https://adcdn.example.com/901/box-bg.jpg"/>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>
        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=901</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="7500"  id="2">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=901</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="15000" id="3">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=901</Event>
        </EventStream>
        <svta:Click clickThroughUrl="https://advertiser.example.com/901">
          <svta:ClickTracking>https://tracker.example.com/click?ad=901</svta:ClickTracking>
        </svta:Click>
      </svta:Candidate>
    </svta:OverlayList>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

Two decoders for the two videos and an image surface for the background:
option 1 survives and the APS emits it alone.

**D2 — two decoders, no non-video surface.**

```
GET /decision/overlay?slot=901&sgaiVideoDecoders=2&sgaiImageOverlay=false&sgaiHtmlOverlay=false
Host: aps.example.com
```

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
     type="static" minBufferTime="PT0S" mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T11:13:02Z">
  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
    <svta:OverlayList>
      <svta:Candidate id="cand-901" duration="PT15S">
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="video" layout="linear">
          <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="10.0">https://adcdn.example.com/901/takeover.mpd</ImportedMPD>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>
        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=901</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="7500"  id="2">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=901</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="15000" id="3">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=901</Event>
        </EventStream>
        <svta:Click clickThroughUrl="https://advertiser.example.com/901">
          <svta:ClickTracking>https://tracker.example.com/click?ad=901</svta:ClickTracking>
        </svta:Click>
      </svta:Candidate>
    </svta:OverlayList>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

The decoder count is not what rules out option 1 here: the background is
an image element and this device composites no image on video. Options 2
and 3 fall for the same reason, and option 4 — one decoder used
sequentially, no surface at all — is emitted alone.

**D3 — declares an axis it knows and omits one it does not.**

```
GET /decision/overlay?slot=901&sgaiVideoDecoders=1&sgaiImageOverlay=true
Host: aps.example.com
```

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
     type="static" minBufferTime="PT0S" mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T11:13:29Z">
  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
    <svta:OverlayList>
      <svta:Candidate id="cand-901" duration="PT15S">
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image"
                              layout="squeezeback-l-shape"
                              assetUrl="https://adcdn.example.com/901/lshape.jpg"/>
        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=901</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="7500"  id="2">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=901</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="15000" id="3">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=901</Event>
        </EventStream>
        <svta:Click clickThroughUrl="https://advertiser.example.com/901">
          <svta:ClickTracking>https://tracker.example.com/click?ad=901</svta:ClickTracking>
        </svta:Click>
      </svta:Candidate>
    </svta:OverlayList>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

`sgaiHtmlOverlay` is absent because the Player could not determine that
axis when it issued the request, and a parameter it cannot populate is
omitted rather than sent empty. **An absent parameter means
undetermined, never unsupported** (§5.8.2). Option 1 is ruled out on the
declared decoder count; option 2 needs one decoder plus the declared
image surface, depends on no undetermined axis, and survives. How an APS
resolves an axis it was not told is its own policy — and no option here
depends on the HTML axis, so a conservative APS and a permissive one
produce the same document.

**D4 — discloses nothing.**

```
GET /decision/overlay?slot=901
Host: aps.example.com
```

<!-- refine: v9-detail-review.md#flag-4 -->
```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
     type="static" minBufferTime="PT0S" mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T11:14:07Z">
  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
    <svta:OverlayList>
      <svta:Candidate id="cand-901" duration="PT15S">
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="video"
                              layout="squeezeback-double-box-with-background">
          <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="10.0">https://adcdn.example.com/901/box.mpd</ImportedMPD>
          <svta:BackgroundElement assetUrl="https://adcdn.example.com/901/box-bg.jpg"/>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image"
                              layout="squeezeback-l-shape"
                              assetUrl="https://adcdn.example.com/901/lshape.jpg"/>
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image"
                              layout="overlay-lower-third"
                              assetUrl="https://adcdn.example.com/901/banner.png"/>
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="video" layout="linear">
          <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="10.0">https://adcdn.example.com/901/takeover.mpd</ImportedMPD>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>
        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=901</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="7500"  id="2">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=901</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="15000" id="3">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=901</Event>
        </EventStream>
        <svta:Click clickThroughUrl="https://advertiser.example.com/901">
          <svta:ClickTracking>https://tracker.example.com/click?ad=901</svta:ClickTracking>
        </svta:Click>
      </svta:Candidate>
    </svta:OverlayList>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

Sending a reserved parameter is optional and this Player sends none. The
APS holds no device information and answers anyway, unnarrowed: all four
options in the order the ADS gave them. The Player walks them in
document order — option 1 needs a second decoder it does not have,
option 2 needs one decoder plus an image surface, which it does have —
renders option 2 and stops. A Player that discloses nothing leaves the
APS unable to narrow, and an APS that cannot narrow emits the full list.

**D5 — one decoder, no surface of any kind.**

```
GET /decision/overlay?slot=901&sgaiVideoDecoders=1&sgaiImageOverlay=false&sgaiHtmlOverlay=false
Host: aps.example.com
```

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
     type="static" minBufferTime="PT0S" mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T11:14:52Z">
  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
    <svta:OverlayList>
      <svta:Candidate id="cand-901" duration="PT15S">
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="video" layout="linear">
          <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="10.0">https://adcdn.example.com/901/takeover.mpd</ImportedMPD>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>
        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=901</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="7500"  id="2">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=901</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="15000" id="3">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=901</Event>
        </EventStream>
        <svta:Click clickThroughUrl="https://advertiser.example.com/901">
          <svta:ClickTracking>https://tracker.example.com/click?ad=901</svta:ClickTracking>
        </svta:Click>
      </svta:Candidate>
    </svta:OverlayList>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

Every axis is declared and each is negative for concurrent composition,
so options 1, 2 and 3 are ruled out and option 4 is emitted alone — the
same single option the APS emitted for D2, reached from a different
declaration.

### M.4 The sub-MPDs the video options import

`https://adcdn.example.com/901/box.mpd` — the creative inside one box of
the squeezeback, video only: it plays beside the primary content, which
keeps its own audio.

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024"
     type="static" minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T11:12:40Z">
  <Period id="1" duration="PT15S">
    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video" mimeType="video/mp4"
                   codecs="avc1.4d401f" segmentAlignment="true"
                   startWithSAP="1">
      <Representation id="v1" bandwidth="1500000" width="960" height="540">
        <BaseURL>https://adcdn.example.com/901/box-v1.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-864"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

`https://adcdn.example.com/901/takeover.mpd` — the full-screen creative,
which occupies the surface on its own and carries its audio.

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024"
     type="static" minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T11:12:40Z">
  <Period id="1" duration="PT15S">
    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video" mimeType="video/mp4"
                   codecs="avc1.4d401f" segmentAlignment="true"
                   startWithSAP="1">
      <Representation id="v1" bandwidth="2500000" width="1280" height="720">
        <BaseURL>https://adcdn.example.com/901/takeover-v1.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-912"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
    <AdaptationSet id="2" contentType="audio" mimeType="audio/mp4"
                   codecs="mp4a.40.2" lang="en" segmentAlignment="true">
      <Representation id="a1" bandwidth="128000" audioSamplingRate="48000">
        <BaseURL>https://adcdn.example.com/901/takeover-a1.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-708"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

### M.5 The walk-through, and the outcome

Every class runs the same steps: resolve the window at or after 465 000,
attach whichever reserved parameters it discloses, check what arrives
against its own capabilities and the window's `@allowedLayouts`, render
the first option that satisfies both. `PT15S` converts to 15 000, equal
to the cap and admitted.

| Class | Parameters on the request | Options emitted | Rendered | What the viewer sees |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | decoders 2, image true, HTML true, plus one vendor-prefixed parameter | 1 | Option 1 | Primary content shrunk into one box, the ad video in the other, the advertiser's background filling the bands |
| D2 | decoders 2, image false, HTML false | 1 | Option 4 | A full-screen video ad of bounded duration, then the primary content resumes |
| D3 | decoders 1, image true; HTML axis omitted | 1 | Option 2 | Primary content shrunk over a full-frame image creative, whose visible band forms the "L" |
| D4 | none | 4 | Option 2 | The same as D3 |
| D5 | decoders 1, image false, HTML false | 1 | Option 4 | The same as D2 |

### M.6 The two points this annex exists for

**The Player-visible interface is identical either way.** Nothing in a
resolution document distinguishes "the APS narrowed the list" from "this
is all there was". The D1 and D4 documents carry a different number of
options and are otherwise the same answer: same profile, same candidate
identifier, same duration, same tracking, same click. A Player reading a
single-option document learns nothing about why it is single and needs
nothing — it runs the check it would run on four.

**The suitability of that single option sits with the APS, or with the
ADS that handed it one.** Narrowing to one is answering for that option
being renderable on the device that asked. What the Player owes is
unchanged: it checks what arrives against its own capabilities and the
window's declarations, renders it when both pass, and passes over the
candidate when they do not — the device's state may have moved since the
request, or the derivation may simply have been wrong. Disclosing
narrows what arrives; it does not make what arrives authoritative.

## Annex N — A non-linear ad over a replacement that is not advertising

### N.1 The scenario

The Publisher replaces a bounded span of a live timeline with content
that is not an ad: a regional blackout slate with its own audio,
resolved from the Publisher's own endpoint with no ad decision behind
it. Over the same span it declares an overlay opportunity, and the
overlay does carry advertising. For two minutes the screen holds a
non-advertising substitution with an ad composited on top of it, and the
two portions share nothing but the surface.

<!-- refine: v9-spec-validation.md#F9 -->
The point of the annex is what the replacement construct is. The base
specification defines it without advertising semantics — *"An
alternative Media Presentation is a presentation that replaces the main
Media Presentation at a certain point on the media timeline for a
duration of time. ... A key use case for this is advertisement"*
(DASH §5.16.1) — and a key use case is a use, not a definition. This
specification uses the construct for break-style ads; the same construct
carries a slate here, and the Player behaves identically because nothing
in it can observe the difference. The Advanced Linear profile names
blackouts alongside advertisement insertion (DASH §8.13.1). How
the Publisher blacks out, what the slate contains and how rights are
enforced are outside this specification.

### N.2 What the Publisher declares

Two windows over the same span, in two event streams because they are
two schemes:

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:advanced-linear:2025"
     type="dynamic"
     availabilityStartTime="2026-09-18T10:00:00Z"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T11:58:00Z"
     minimumUpdatePeriod="PT10S"
     timeShiftBufferDepth="PT1H"
     minBufferTime="PT4S">
  <Period id="p1" start="PT0S">
    <!-- The blacked-out span: resolves to the Publisher's own slate,
         with no ADS, no APS and no ad candidate involved. -->
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:alternativeMPD:replace:2025"
                 timescale="1000">
      <Event id="1401" presentationTime="7200000" duration="120000">
        <ReplacePresentation uri="https://publisher.example.com/blackout/region-7"
                             earliestResolutionTimeOffset="30000"
                             maxDuration="120000"
                             returnOffset="0"
                             clip="true"/>
      </Event>
    </EventStream>
    <!-- The overlay opportunity, covering the same span. -->
    <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026"
                 timescale="1000">
      <Event id="1402" presentationTime="7200000" duration="120000">
        <svta:OverlayPresentation
            uri="https://aps.example.com/decision/overlay?slot=1402"
            earliestResolutionTimeOffset="20000"
            maxDuration="20000"
            allowedLayouts="overlay-corner overlay-lower-third"/>
      </Event>
    </EventStream>
    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video" mimeType="video/mp4"
                   codecs="avc1.4d401f" segmentAlignment="true"
                   startWithSAP="1" par="16:9">
      <SegmentTemplate timescale="90000" duration="360000" startNumber="1"
                       initialization="$RepresentationID$/init.mp4"
                       media="$RepresentationID$/seg-$Number$.m4s"/>
      <Representation id="v-720" bandwidth="2500000"
                      width="1280" height="720" sar="1:1"/>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

The two windows belong to different families, so their coincidence is
not a fallback chain: both are live at 7 200 000 and each resolves on
its own terms through its own `@uri`.

### N.3 What the Publisher's endpoint returns for the replacement

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:list:2024"
     type="list"
     minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T11:59:32Z">
  <Period id="slate" duration="PT2M">
    <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="15.0">https://publisher.example.com/blackout/region-7/slate.mpd</ImportedMPD>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

No tracking `<EventStream>`, no `<svta:Click>`, no creative metadata and
no extension namespace at all: there is no ad in it and no ADS or APS
took part in producing it.

### N.4 What the APS returns for the overlay

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     xmlns:svta="urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026"
     profiles="urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026"
     type="static" minBufferTime="PT0S" mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T11:59:45Z">
  <Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">
    <svta:OverlayList>
      <svta:Candidate id="cand-1402" duration="PT20S">
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="video" layout="overlay-corner">
          <ImportedMPD earliestResolutionTimeOffset="5.0">https://adcdn.example.com/1402/corner.mpd</ImportedMPD>
        </svta:RenderableAsset>
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="html"
                              layout="overlay-lower-third"
                              assetUrl="https://adcdn.example.com/1402/strip.html"/>
        <svta:RenderableAsset form="image"
                              layout="overlay-corner"
                              assetUrl="https://adcdn.example.com/1402/corner.png"/>
        <EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015"
                     value="1" timescale="1000">
          <Event presentationTime="0"     id="1">https://tracker.example.com/impression?ad=1402</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="10000" id="2">https://tracker.example.com/midpoint?ad=1402</Event>
          <Event presentationTime="20000" id="3">https://tracker.example.com/complete?ad=1402</Event>
        </EventStream>
        <svta:Click clickThroughUrl="https://advertiser.example.com/regional-offer">
          <svta:ClickTracking>https://tracker.example.com/click?ad=1402</svta:ClickTracking>
        </svta:Click>
      </svta:Candidate>
    </svta:OverlayList>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

Nothing in it refers to the replacement: the APS was asked to fill an
overlay window and answered that question.

### N.5 The sub-MPD the video option imports

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<MPD xmlns="urn:mpeg:dash:schema:mpd:2011"
     profiles="urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024"
     type="static" minBufferTime="PT2S"
     publishTime="2026-09-18T11:59:45Z">
  <Period id="1" duration="PT20S">
    <AdaptationSet id="1" contentType="video" mimeType="video/mp4"
                   codecs="avc1.4d401f" segmentAlignment="true"
                   startWithSAP="1">
      <Representation id="v1" bandwidth="900000" width="640" height="360">
        <BaseURL>https://adcdn.example.com/1402/corner-v1.mp4</BaseURL>
        <SegmentBase indexRange="0-840"/>
      </Representation>
    </AdaptationSet>
  </Period>
</MPD>
```

### N.6 The walk-through

1. **Both windows become current at 7 200 000**, in different families,
   so the Player holds two live opportunities rather than a primary and
   a backup.

2. **The replacement takes the surface.** The Player resolves its `@uri`
   at or after the ERT, receives the document of §N.3 and plays the
   slate in place of the primary content, whose media time keeps
   advancing underneath: *"the main Media Presentation is not being
   output, but its media time progresses at the same speed as the
   currently playing alternative Media Presentation"* (DASH §5.16.1). One
   access engine outputs media, so the slate occupies the decoder the
   primary content released, exactly as a linear ad would.

3. **The overlay is resolved independently** — a separate request, a
   separate document, a separate validation, sharing no state with the
   replacement.

4. **The options are checked against the overlay window and the
   device.** The Player walks the three options in document order and
   takes the first whose layout the window admits and whose form and
   layout the device can satisfy — the same walk it runs for an overlay
   over primary content, and neither check mentions what is on the
   surface.

5. **The chosen form composites over whatever is there.** No attribute
   of the window, the candidate or the option describes what is
   underneath, and the compositing step takes the surface as it finds it
   — here, holding the slate.

6. **Each portion ends on its own schedule.** The overlay ends when its
   rendered length reaches 20 000; the slate runs to the end of the
   replaced span. Where no option is satisfiable, the candidate is
   skipped and the viewer sees the slate alone.

### N.7 The cap and the layouts come from the overlay window

`@maxDuration="20000"` and
`@allowedLayouts="overlay-corner overlay-lower-third"` are declared on
the overlay window, and they are what the candidate is checked against:
`PT20S` converts to 20 000, equal to the cap exactly and admitted, and
both layout tokens it uses are in that set. The replacement's own
`@maxDuration="120000"` bounds the slate and says nothing about the
overlay; had the overlay window declared 8 000, this candidate would
have been over the cap while the slate stayed untouched.

The asymmetry runs through everything else. The overlay holds the
tracking schedule, the ClickThrough and the cap; the replacement holds
none of the three, because no ad decision produced it, no beacon belongs
to it and no click leads away from it. A reader who took the replacement
for an advertising construct would expect the slate to carry an ad's
obligations, and would have to decide what an ad with no tracking means.
It means nothing, because the slate is not one.

### N.8 Per device class

The budget is the budget of any coexisting overlay, because a decoder is
a decoder whatever it is decoding:

| Class | Option selected | What the viewer sees |
|---|---|---|
| **D1** | 1 — `video` / `overlay-corner` | The corner video ad composited over the slate on a second decoder. |
| **D2** | 1 — `video` / `overlay-corner` | The same: this option needs a second decoder and no non-video surface. |
| **D3** | 2 — `html` / `overlay-lower-third` | Option 1 is passed over for want of a second decoder; the HTML strip composites over the slate on one decoder plus one surface. |
| **D4** | 3 — `image` / `overlay-corner` | Options 1 and 2 are passed over — no second decoder, no HTML surface — and the still image composites over the slate. |
| **D5** | none | No option is satisfiable, the candidate is skipped, and the slate plays alone for the span. Declining is a defined outcome rather than a failure. |

The same five rows hold when the overlay sits over primary content. The
class decides which option renders and never decides whether what is
underneath is advertising — a Player cannot observe whether the video it
is decoding is an ad, a slate or the programme, so no rule can be
written against that distinction.

## Annex O — Test cases and conformance criteria

This annex lists, per chapter, what an implementer can test against. Each test
case carries a stable identifier, the actor under test, the setup, the action
and the pass criterion. The identifiers are this annex's own (`T-nn`) and they
are what a conformance report cites. The annex is the test surface of the
normative chapters; it restates no obligation and adds none.

### O.1 How to read a test case

| Column | What it holds |
|---|---|
| ID | `T-nn`, unique across this annex and stable across editions of it. |
| Under test | The actor whose behaviour the case exercises: Publisher, ADS, APS or Player. |
| Setup | The documents, the device class and the state the case starts from. |
| Action | The single event that triggers the behaviour. |
| Pass criterion | What an observer records. |

A pass criterion is an **observable**, not a restatement of the obligation. It
names something a harness can see from outside the implementation: bytes on the
wire, a request that was or was not issued, a frame on the screen, an entry in a
log, a document that a schema processor accepted. A criterion that reads "the
Player enforces the cap" restates §4.5; a criterion that reads "rendering of the
second candidate stops at 15 000 ms of cumulative presentation time and the
primary content is on screen at the next frame" is testable.

**What a Player test establishes, and what it does not.** The obligations of §4.5
bind a Player conformant to this specification. The base specification specifies
no client behaviour normatively — *"as DASH Client operation is not specified
normatively in this document, it is also unspecified how a DASH Client conforms
to a particular profile"* (DASH §8.1, NOTE 1) — so a Player that does not
implement this specification is not under test in §O.2 through §O.6. What is
under test for such a Player is §O.7 alone: that the constructs are skipped
silently and that observable playback continues. Every other row assumes a
Player that implements this specification.

Device classes are cited as D1..D5 per §3.4. Where a case names no class, the
outcome is the same on all five.

### O.2 Chapter 4 — conformance

#### O.2.1 Publisher

Publisher obligations are authoring-time, so every case here is document-level:
the harness reads the main MPD and needs no runtime.

| ID | Under test | Setup | Action | Pass criterion |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T-01 | Publisher | A main MPD carrying at least one opportunity window of each family present in the service. | Read every opportunity declaration. | Every declaration carries `@maxDuration` with a value. |
| T-02 | Publisher | A main MPD carrying overlay and pause-trigger windows. | Read `@allowedLayouts` on each. | Every token is a member of the enumeration of §3.2, and every token on a pause-trigger window is `pause-fullscreen` or `pause-partial`. |
| T-03 | Publisher | A main MPD whose `<Period>` carries several windows of the same family. | Count the `<EventStream>` elements per family per `<Period>`. | Exactly one `<EventStream>` per family per `<Period>` holds all windows of that family, per the base specification's rule that *"all Events of one type shall be clustered in one Event Stream"* (DASH §5.10.2.1). |
| T-04 | Publisher | A main MPD carrying a pause-trigger window. | Read the `<Event>`'s `@presentationTime` and `@duration`. | Both are present and bound a region of the primary timeline; `@maxDuration` on the window is read as the display bound of one pause ad. |
| T-05 | Publisher | Content carrying at least one pause-trigger window. | Read the `<Metrics>` element of the main MPD. | A `<Metrics>` element requests the `PlayList` metric, whose entries record *"the time interval between a user action and whichever occurs soonest of the next user action, the end of playback or a failure that stops playback"* (DASH Annex D.4.6). |
| T-06 | Publisher | A main MPD declaring query parameters for the resolution request. | Read the `<RequestParam>` elements under `urn:mpeg:dash:urlparam:2025`. | Each carries `@includeInRequests` naming `altmpd` for linear windows, `urn:svta:dash:request:sgai-resolution:2026` for non-linear windows, or both. |

#### O.2.2 ADS

The ADS is reached only through the APS, so these cases observe the APS's output
and the Player's response to it; what they establish about the ADS is that
nothing in this specification constrains its decision.

| ID | Under test | Setup | Action | Pass criterion |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T-07 | ADS | A slot whose cap is 30 s; the ADS returns candidates whose declared durations total 45 s. | Run the conformance check over the resulting resolution document. | The check reports the document conformant. Cumulative overflow alone produces no failure; the cap is exercised on the Player in T-22. |
| T-08 | ADS | A candidate whose tracking schedule carries beacons at 0 s, 25 % and completion. | Read the candidate's tracking carrier. | The beacon set and its relative times are exactly those the decision carried; the Player-side execution of that schedule is T-35. |
| T-09 | ADS | A decision that fills no ad for the opportunity. | Issue the resolution request. | The APS answers `200` with a body carrying a well-formed resolution document holding no candidates, per T-47. |
| T-10 | ADS | Two runs of the same scenario, one against an ADS emitting VAST and one against an ADS emitting a decision format of its own. | Complete the flow of §6.2 in both runs. | The two runs produce the same Player-visible resolution documents and the same rendered sequence; the Player issues no request to the ADS in either run. |

#### O.2.3 APS

| ID | Under test | Setup | Action | Pass criterion |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T-11 | APS | Any resolution request. | Validate the returned document against the DASH schema and against the extension namespace `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026`. | The validator reports the document valid with the foreign-namespace subtree included in the walk, per T-49. |
| T-12 | APS | An overlay window and a pause-trigger window on the same content. | Issue each window's resolution request. | The document returned to the overlay window and the document returned to the pause-trigger window each match the family of the window that asked. |
| T-13 | APS | A no-fill decision. | Issue the resolution request. | The response is `200`, carries a body, and the body parses as a resolution document whose `<svta:OverlayList>` holds no `<svta:Candidate>` children. |
| T-14 | APS | A candidate the ADS offers in three creative variants. | Read the candidate. | The candidate carries three `<svta:RenderableAsset>` children in the intended preference order, matching the base specification's rule that where multiple alternatives are provided *"the content author expresses the preferences of using one of those by the order with the first one having the highest preference"* (DASH §5.11.3). |
| T-15 | APS | A decision carrying an image creative and an HTML creative. | Read the resulting document. | Each non-video creative URL travels on `<svta:RenderableAsset>@assetUrl`, and no `@mimeType` outside the RFC 4337 registry appears on any Representation, the base specification requiring that *"The @mimeType attribute of each Representation shall be provided according to IETF RFC 4337"* (DASH §7.3.1). |
| T-16 | APS | A pause-trigger resolution request. | Read the returned document. | `<svta:OverlayList>` carries `@onCandidatesExhausted` with one of `repeat`, `request-again` or `stop`, or omits it and is read as `stop`. |
| T-17 | APS | Three resolution requests: one carrying all three reserved capability parameters, one carrying `sgaiVideoDecoders` alone, one carrying none. | Issue all three. | All three are answered `200` with a document carrying candidates; no response depends on a parameter being present. |
| T-18 | APS | A candidate carrying beacons. | Read its tracking carrier. | The carrier is an `<EventStream schemeIdUri="urn:mpeg:dash:event:callback:2015" value="1" timescale="1000">`; every `<Event>` carries `@presentationTime` and `@id` and holds the beacon URL as element text content. |
| T-19 | APS | A decision carrying a ClickThrough destination and two click-tracking URLs. | Read the candidate. | One `<svta:Click>` carries `@clickThroughUrl` and two `<svta:ClickTracking>` children; no click URL appears elsewhere in the document. |

#### O.2.4 Player

| ID | Under test | Setup | Action | Pass criterion |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T-20 | Player | A window whose `@allowedLayouts` is `overlay-corner`; a document whose only candidate offers `overlay-lower-third` alone. | Resolve and attempt presentation. | Nothing is rendered from that candidate; the primary content is on screen at the next frame; the Player attempts no further window, the document having carried candidates. |
| T-21 | Player | Two overlapping windows: the first caps at 10 s and allows `overlay-corner`, the second caps at 30 s and allows `linear`. The first fails to resolve. | Resolve the second window and present its candidates. | The candidates are checked against 30 s and `linear`, the declarations of the window that served them, and against nothing carried by the first window. |
| T-22 | Player | An overlay slot capped at 15 000 ms in a timescale of 1000; three candidates of 6 s, 6 s and 6 s. | Present the sequence. | The first two candidates render whole; the third renders for 3 s of presentation time and stops there; the beacons scheduled after the stop are not issued; the primary content is on screen at the next frame. |
| T-23 | Player | A cap of 10 000 ms in a timescale of 1000; a candidate declaring `PT9.9994S` and a candidate declaring `PT10S`. | Compare each against the cap. | `PT9.9994S` converts to 10 000 units by rounding up and is admitted; `PT10S` converts to 10 000 units and is admitted. |
| T-24 | Player | An opportunity window declaring `@maxDuration="0"`. | Reach the window's presentation time. | No resolution request is issued and the primary content continues, the base specification stating that *"If the value of @maxDuration is zero, the event is not executed"* (DASH §5.16.5.2). |
| T-25 | Player | An opportunity declaration carrying no `@maxDuration`. | Reach the declaration's presentation time. | No ad is presented from that declaration and the primary content continues. This is the narrowing §4.5 declares of the base specification, which reads an absent value as *"assumed to be infinity"* (DASH §5.16.5.2); the narrowing is provisional and open with the working group, and a harness records the outcome rather than treating it as settled. |
| T-26 | Player | An overlay of 20 s presentation duration, suspended at 8 s by a pause of 40 s of wall-clock. | Resume playback. | The overlay resumes with 12 s of cap remaining; the paused interval accrues nothing against the cap. |
| T-27 | Player | A replacement slot scheduled at t with a cap of 30 s, resolved 5 s late; run once with `@clip` absent and once with `@clip="false"`. | Play the slot in both runs. | With `@clip` absent the presentation ends at t + 30 s and runs 25 s; with `@clip="false"` it ends at t + 35 s and runs 30 s. |
| T-28 | Player | A candidate offering, in document order, `video`/`overlay`, `html`/`overlay-corner` and `image`/`overlay-corner`; device class D4; window allowing `overlay-corner`. | Present the candidate. | The image option renders: the video option needs a second decoder and the HTML option an HTML surface, neither of which D4 has, and the first option that satisfies both the device and `@allowedLayouts` is the image. |
| T-29 | Player | Four candidates C1..C4 in document order; C2 has no satisfiable option; C4 would exceed the cap. | Present the slot. | C1 and C3 render in that order; C2 and C4 do not; no candidate is re-ordered and no candidate is de-duplicated. |
| T-30 | Player | A slot carrying two candidates of 8 s each. | Present the slot. | At every sampled frame at most one non-linear form is on screen; the second candidate starts at the frame after the first ends. |
| T-31 | Player | An overlay rendering at 6 s of its 20 s, inside a pause-trigger window; a pause ad resolved. | Pause playback. | The overlay is suspended and off screen while the pause ad is on screen; the pause ad is the only ad surface visible; the overlay has issued no further beacons during the pause. |
| T-32 | Player | A pause ad on screen, its schedule carrying a beacon 10 s ahead. | Resume playback. | The pause ad is off screen within one rendering frame of the resume; the 10 s beacon is not issued; the suspended overlay is restored where its window is still open and is not restored where the window has closed. |
| T-33 | Player | Three pause documents of two candidates each, declaring `repeat`, `request-again` and `stop`; a pause long enough to exhaust the sequence in each run. | Hold the pause past exhaustion. | Under `repeat` the sequence starts again from the first candidate; under `request-again` a second resolution request is issued, and a document with no candidates then leaves the paused primary frame on screen for the rest of that pause; under `stop` the paused primary frame is on screen. |
| T-34 | Player | A pause-trigger window carrying `@executeOnce="true"`; two pauses inside the window; the first resolution returns candidates. | Pause twice. | A pause ad renders on the first pause; the second pause is uninterrupted; the window counts as consumed from the frame the first pause ad began rendering. |
| T-35 | Player | One document, two candidates; within the first, two `<Event>` entries sharing `@id="7"` and a third repeating one URL at the same presentation time; the second candidate also carries `@id="7"`. | Present both candidates. | Within the first candidate the shared `@id` produces one request and the repeated URL at the same time produces one request; the second candidate's `@id="7"` produces a further request, so the harness records two requests for `@id="7"` across the document. |
| T-36 | Player | A candidate carrying `<svta:Click>` with `@clickThroughUrl` and one `<svta:ClickTracking>`. | Activate the click. | The click-tracking URL is requested at the moment of activation and the ClickThrough destination is opened or handed to the platform. |
| T-37 | Player | A candidate declaring `PT10S`, played at a primary content speed of 2×, with a beacon at 5 s. | Present the candidate. | The candidate occupies 5 s of wall-clock; the beacon is issued at 5 s of presentation time, that is 2.5 s of wall-clock; cap accounting records 10 s. |
| T-38 | Player | A main MPD carrying an `<EventStream>` of a scheme this specification does not define and an element of an unknown foreign namespace. | Play the content. | Neither construct produces a rendered artefact, the whole subtree of each is skipped, and playback of the primary content is uninterrupted. |
| T-39 | Player | An accepted candidate whose creative returns `503` on its second segment. | Present the candidate. | Rendering of that candidate stops, the primary content is on screen at the next frame with no blank frame and no error overlay, and no beacon is issued for the aborted opportunity. |
| T-40 | Player | Three overlay windows of one family overlapping at the same presentation time, in one `<EventStream>`; the first two fail to resolve. | Reach the overlap. | The windows are attempted oldest presentation time first with ties broken by position inside the `<EventStream>`, the third serves the ad, and the base specification's outcome holds where all fail: *"If no event can be successfully executed, the playback continues uninterrupted"* (DASH §5.16.2.2.5). |

### O.3 Chapter 5 — syntax

Every case here is document-level: a schema processor plus a reader of §5. A
document that fails any of them is a document the Player is not obliged to
render.

| ID | Under test | Setup | Action | Pass criterion |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T-41 | Publisher | An `<Event>` of scheme `urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026` carrying `<svta:OverlayPresentation>` with `@uri`, `@maxDuration`, `@allowedLayouts`, `@earliestResolutionTimeOffset`, `@executeOnce` and `@serviceDescriptionId`. | Validate. | The document is valid; every attribute holds a value of its declared type; the layout tokens are from §3.2. |
| T-42 | Publisher | An `<Event>` of scheme `urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-pause-trigger:2026` carrying `<svta:PauseAdPresentation>` with all six attributes and the `<Event>`'s own `@presentationTime` and `@duration`. | Validate. | The document is valid and `@allowedLayouts` carries only `pause-fullscreen`, `pause-partial`, or both. |
| T-43 | APS | A `ListMPD` with `MPD@type="list"`, `@profiles` carrying `urn:mpeg:dash:profile:list:2024`, one `<Period>` per ad with `@id` and `@duration`, each holding an `<ImportedMPD>`. | Validate against the list profile. | The document is valid; no XLink attribute is present; no alternative-MPD event is present. |
| T-44 | APS | A single-period alternative MPD answering a single-ad linear slot. | Validate. | The document is valid and the Player of T-57 plays the ad from it, so the two linear shapes are interchangeable for a single-ad slot. |
| T-45 | APS | An Overlay Resolution Document: `@profiles` carrying `urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026`, `@type="static"`, `@minBufferTime="PT0S"`, `@mediaPresentationDuration="PT0S"`, `@publishTime`, one `<Period id="resolution" duration="PT0S">` whose only child is `<svta:OverlayList>`. | Validate. | The document is valid with no Adaptation Set present, the base specification requiring one *"unless the value of the @duration attribute of the Period is set to zero"* (DASH §5.3.2.2, Table 4). |
| T-46 | APS | A sub-MPD referenced by an `<ImportedMPD>`: `@profiles` including `urn:mpeg:dash:profile:sps:2024`, `@type="static"`, one `<Period>` carrying `@duration`, no `@mediaPresentationDuration`, no `@availabilityStartTime`. | Validate. | The document is valid and every Representation carries an `@mimeType` from the RFC 4337 registry. |
| T-47 | APS | The document of T-45 with `<svta:OverlayList/>` carrying no `<svta:Candidate>` children, served `200` with a body. | Fetch and parse. | The status is `200`, a body is present, the body parses, and the parse yields zero candidates. The same shape serves the pause family. |
| T-48 | APS | A `ListMPD` whose merge yields no available media. | Fetch and merge. | The merge completes and produces no available media; this is the linear family's empty resolution and the Player treats it under T-64. |
| T-49 | APS | A candidate carrying the callback `<EventStream>` subtree. | Validate the whole document, then validate again with the foreign-namespace subtree skipped. | The first run reports the tracking carrier checked and its `<Event>` entries walked. A validator that reports the document valid while skipping that subtree has **not checked the tracking carrier at all**, and a conformance report citing only that run establishes nothing about the carrier. |
| T-50 | APS | A tracking `<EventStream>` whose `<Event>` entries each carry `@presentationTime` and `@id`, with the URL as element text content. | Validate. | Every entry carries `@id`, on which de-duplication is keyed, and every beacon URL is present as the element's text content. |
| T-51 | APS | A candidate carrying `<svta:Click clickThroughUrl="…">` with two `<svta:ClickTracking>` children holding their URLs as element text content. | Validate. | The document is valid and both click-tracking URLs are reachable from the single `<svta:Click>`. |
| T-52 | APS | A candidate carrying `<svta:AdSystem>`, `<svta:AdTitle>`, `<svta:Advertiser>` and `<svta:UniversalAdId>`, each with its value as element text content. | Validate, then present the candidate on a Player that reads none of them. | The document is valid; the candidate renders identically with the four elements present and absent, so emitting and reading are both optional. |
| T-53 | APS | Three candidates: `@form="video"` with an `<ImportedMPD>` child and no `@assetUrl`; `@form="image"` with `@assetUrl`; `@form="html"` with `@assetUrl`. | Validate. | All three are valid; the video option reaches its creative through `<ImportedMPD>`, whose `@earliestResolutionTimeOffset` is read in seconds. |
| T-54 | APS | One `<svta:RenderableAsset layout="squeezeback-double-box-with-background">` carrying `<svta:BackgroundElement assetUrl="…">`, and one carrying the same child under `layout="squeezeback-double-box"`. | Validate. | The first is admissible; the second is reported as an inadmissible placement of the background element, which is a composition attribute of the one layout that names it. |
| T-55 | APS | A resolution request carrying `sgaiVideoDecoders=2`, `sgaiImageOverlay=true` and `sgaiHtmlOverlay=true`. | Capture the request line. | The three parameters are present with those values; the triple identifies D1 per §3.4, and the triples `2,false,false`, `1,true,true`, `1,true,false` and `1,false,false` identify D2..D5. |
| T-56 | APS | Two further requests: one carrying no reserved parameter, and one carrying `sgaiImageOverlay=true` plus a vendor parameter named `x-acme-panelsize`. | Capture both request lines. | Neither request carries a reserved parameter with an empty value; the omitted parameters are read as undetermined rather than as unsupported; the vendor parameter carries the `x-<vendor>-` prefix. |

### O.4 Chapter 6 — interfaces

| ID | Under test | Setup | Action | Pass criterion |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T-57 | Player + APS | A linear insertion window resolving to a `ListMPD` of two ads. | Play from before the window to after it. | The request is issued at or after the Earliest Resolution Time; both ads play in document order; the primary content resumes at the insertion point. |
| T-58 | Player + APS | An overlay window resolving to a document of two candidates. | Play across the window. | The request is issued at or after the Earliest Resolution Time; the two candidates render in sequence within the cap; the primary content plays underneath throughout. |
| T-59 | Player + APS | A pause-trigger window; a viewer pause inside it. | Pause, hold, resume. | The request is issued speculatively at the Earliest Resolution Time or lazily at the pause; the pause ad is on screen for at most the window's `@maxDuration`; the presentation time is frozen for the pause, clipped to the time-shift buffer edge where the pause outlives it on live content. |
| T-60 | Player | An APS endpoint whose host does not resolve. | Reach the window. | The attempt is recorded as a failed execution, the next window of the family is attempted, and with the family exhausted the primary content plays uninterrupted, matching *"A failed execution results in smooth continued playback of the main media presentation"* (DASH §5.16.2.2.6). |
| T-61 | Player | An APS answering `503`, then a run answering `404`. | Reach the window in each run. | Both runs behave as T-60; the run counter for an `@executeOnce` window is unchanged, so the window stays executable. |
| T-62 | Player | An APS answering `200` with a body that is not well-formed XML, then a run whose body is well-formed but schema-invalid. | Reach the window in each run. | Both runs behave as T-60 and nothing is rendered from either body. |
| T-63 | Player | An overlay window whose APS answers `200` with a well-formed pause-family document. | Reach the window. | The attempt is recorded as a failure to resolve and the next window of the family is attempted — the outcome of T-64 is not produced, the two being opposite responses to the same status code. |
| T-64 | Player | An APS answering `200` with a well-formed document carrying no candidates. | Reach the window. | The attempt is recorded as a failed execution, the next window of the family is attempted, and an `@executeOnce="true"` window remains executable afterwards. |
| T-65 | Player | An APS that answers `200` with candidates after the window has elapsed. | Reach and pass the window. | The slot is not extended past what the cap bounds for its family; the primary content is uninterrupted. On a replacement slot the clip semantics of T-27 govern a late start. This specification fixes no deadline after which a pending response is abandoned, so a harness records what the implementation did rather than scoring it. |
| T-66 | Player | A candidate whose first beacon endpoint times out and whose second answers `500`. | Present the candidate. | The candidate renders whole; the primary content is unaffected; nothing reaches the viewer. Response handling matches the base specification, where for the callback event *"the client is expected to entirely ignore the response"* (DASH §5.10.4.5.1). |

### O.5 Chapter 7 — expected behaviour

One case per scenario, and one per device class wherever the class changes the
outcome. Each points at the annex that walks the scenario through; the annex is
the expected result the harness compares against.

| ID | Under test | Setup | Action | Pass criterion |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T-67 | Player | The pre-roll scenario of Annex A. | Start the session. | The rendered sequence, the request order and the beacon order match the walk-through of Annex A. |
| T-68 | Player | The mid-roll scenario of Annex B. | Play through the break. | The observed behaviour matches Annex B, including the resumption point. |
| T-69 | Player | The coexisting-overlay scenario of Annex C, on D1. | Play across the overlapping forms. | The observed sequence matches Annex C and at most one non-linear form is on screen at any sampled frame. |
| T-70 | Player | The hybrid scenario of Annex D: a linear ad with a concurrent overlay. | Play the break. | The observed behaviour matches Annex D. |
| T-71 | Player | The pause-triggered scenario of Annex E. | Pause and resume. | The observed behaviour matches Annex E, including the dismissal within one rendering frame. |
| T-72 | Player | The multi-ad break of Annex F. | Play the break. | The ads render in document order and the cap is enforced as Annex F walks it. |
| T-73 | Player | The legacy-Player scenario of Annex G. | Play the content. | The observed behaviour matches Annex G; the per-construct assertions are §O.7. |
| T-74 | Player | The overlay window crossing a pause-trigger window, Annex H. | Pause inside the crossing region. | The observed suspension, priority and restoration match Annex H. |
| T-75 | Player | The overlapping same-family windows with fallback, Annex L. | Reach the overlap with the first window failing. | The observed chain order and outcome match Annex L and T-40. |
| T-76 | Player + APS | One ad resolved by the APS from the Player-declared capabilities, Annex M. | Issue the request from each of two device classes. | The two responses differ as Annex M walks them, and both are answered without the Player having declared any conclusion about what can be served. |
| T-77 | Player | A non-linear ad over a replacement that is not advertising, Annex N. | Play across the replacement. | The observed behaviour matches Annex N. |
| T-78 | Player | The ClickThrough scenario of Annex K. | Activate the click during the ad. | The observed requests and the opened destination match Annex K and T-36. |

Per device class, where the class changes the outcome. The first block is one
candidate offering ordered options, Annex I; the second is the three-element
double box, Annex J.

| ID | Under test | Setup | Action | Pass criterion |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T-79 | Player | Annex I's candidate on D1. | Present it. | The first option in document order renders, D1 satisfying every form and layout in the set. |
| T-80 | Player | Annex I's candidate on D2. | Present it. | A `video` option renders where one is offered and no `image` or `html` option is chosen, D2 compositing no non-video surface on video. |
| T-81 | Player | Annex I's candidate on D3. | Present it. | An `image` or `html` option renders, D3 having one decoder with both surfaces. |
| T-82 | Player | Annex I's candidate on D4. | Present it. | An `image` option renders and an `html` option is passed over. |
| T-83 | Player | Annex I's candidate on D5. | Present it. | Only a `linear` full-screen option renders; where the candidate offers none, the candidate is skipped and the primary content continues. |
| T-84 | Player | Annex J's `squeezeback-double-box-with-background` with a `video` creative, on D1. | Present it. | The shrunk primary content, the video creative and the background image are all composited, which only D1 satisfies for a video creative. |
| T-85 | Player | The same layout with an `image` creative, on D3 and on D4. | Present it in each run. | Both runs composite the shrunk primary content, the image creative and the background image. |
| T-86 | Player | The same layout on D2 and on D5. | Present it in each run. | In both runs the option is passed over whatever the creative's form, the background image needing an image surface that neither class has; the next option renders, or the candidate is skipped and the primary content continues. |
| T-87 | Player | A `pause-fullscreen` pause ad whose form is `video`, on D5. | Pause inside the window. | The pause ad renders, the full-screen surface letting the Player release the primary content's resources; an `image` or `html` pause ad on D5 is passed over instead. |

### O.6 Error conditions

The fifteen conditions under which an ad opportunity can fail to be honoured on
the Player-visible interfaces, each mapped to the cases that exercise it. The
identifiers are this annex's own.

| ID | Condition | Player behaviour under test | Test cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| E-01 | The resolution request fails at transport level — name resolution, connection refusal, TLS failure, timeout before any final status — or returns a final status other than `200`. | The attempt is a failed execution; the next window of the family is attempted; with the family exhausted the primary content continues. Retry count, backoff and deadline are left to the implementation. | T-60, T-61, T-40 |
| E-02 | The response is `200` and the body is not a resolution document the Player can parse: malformed XML, an unknown root element, or schema-invalid content. | The attempt is a failed execution; nothing is rendered from the body; the primary content continues. | T-62, T-49 |
| E-03 | The response is `200` and carries a well-formed resolution document of a family other than the one the slot asked for. | The attempt is a failure to resolve and the chain continues — the opposite outcome from E-04, from the same status code. | T-63, T-12 |
| E-04 | The response is `200` and the document is well-formed and carries no candidates. | The attempt is a failed execution; the next window is attempted; the attempt does not count as an execution, so an `@executeOnce` window stays executable. | T-64, T-47, T-13 |
| E-05 | The resolution document arrives after the window has elapsed. | The slot is not extended past what the cap bounds for its family; the primary content is uninterrupted; on a replacement slot the clip semantics govern the late start. No deadline for abandoning a pending response is fixed here. | T-65, T-27 |
| E-06 | The slot declaration carries no maximum duration, or declares a maximum of zero. | With a zero maximum the opportunity does not fire. With no maximum declared, no ad is presented from that declaration and the primary content continues — the provisional narrowing of §4.5, open with the working group. | T-24, T-25 |
| E-07 | No presentation option on a candidate is satisfiable: the device renders none of the offered forms, or every offered layout needs more decoders or surfaces than the device has. | The candidate is skipped and the next in document order is attempted; the primary content continues once the candidates are exhausted; the next window is not attempted, a document carrying candidates being no failed execution. | T-28, T-79, T-80, T-81, T-82, T-83, T-86 |
| E-08 | A presentation option names a layout the window did not allow, or a token outside the enumeration of §3.2. | That option is passed over; the next option in document order is attempted; the candidate is skipped where none passes both the device check and the allowed-layout check; the check runs against the window that served the candidate. | T-20, T-21, T-02 |
| E-09 | A candidate's creative carrier falls outside video, image and HTML, or a non-audiovisual asset URL is expressed on an RFC 4337-bound `@mimeType` path. | Only a form the device renders reaches the screen; skipping the candidate as a non-conformant upstream signal is admissible, and this specification leaves the case of a renderable out-of-set carrier unconstrained. | T-15, T-53, T-46 |
| E-10 | The cap is reached: a candidate's declared duration would push the cumulative duration past it, or an accepted candidate's actual length exceeds its declared duration. | Rendering stops at the bound even mid-ad; the remaining beacons stop at the trim boundary; surviving candidates keep document order; the declared duration converts into the cap's timescale rounding up before comparison; dropping a candidate before playback on declared duration alone is admissible. | T-22, T-23, T-29, T-26 |
| E-11 | Rendering an accepted candidate fails at runtime: an ad segment returns 4xx or 5xx, a decode error occurs, or the network is lost mid-ad. | That ad is aborted and the primary content continues with no visible artefact and no beacon for the failed opportunity; moving to the next candidate or ending the break is the implementation's choice. | T-39 |
| E-12 | An event scheme, extension element or foreign namespace in the main MPD or in the resolution document is unknown to the Player. | The unknown construct and its whole subtree are ignored and the primary content continues; the application-level metadata elements may be ignored entirely. | T-38, T-52, T-88 through T-99 |
| E-13 | A tracking beacon or a click-tracking request fails, or a beacon is scheduled for a moment the ad never reaches — past a trim boundary, or after a pause ad was dismissed on resume. | The ad and the primary content are unaffected and nothing reaches the viewer; the remaining beacons stop at the trim boundary and from the resume onward; de-duplication is scoped to the candidate. | T-66, T-35, T-22, T-32 |
| E-14 | The resolution document implies two non-linear forms on screen at the same instant, or a pause begins while an overlay or a linear ad occupies the screen. | At most one non-linear form is active at any instant; sequenced forms play one after another; during a pause the overlay is suspended and a linear ad is suspended and later resumed from where it stopped; the suspended form is restored only where its window is still open; on live content the presentation time stays frozen for the pause. | T-30, T-31, T-32, T-74, T-59 |
| E-15 | The pause-ad candidates are exhausted while the viewer is still paused, or the pause window was already consumed. | The declared exhaustion behaviour applies, defaulting to `stop`; under `request-again` a document with no candidates is `stop` for the rest of that pause; the primary content returns immediately on resume; a once-per-session window yields one pause ad, counted from the frame rendering began. | T-33, T-34, T-16 |

Two relations hold across the table and a harness that conflates them scores the
wrong thing. **Window-level** fall-through (E-01 through E-04) advances to the
next window of the same family and reaches the primary content only once the
family is exhausted. **Candidate-level** fall-through (E-07 through E-09)
advances to the next candidate inside the document already obtained and reaches
the primary content only once the candidates are exhausted.

Everything a row describes as reported, logged or exposed is left to the
implementation: this specification defines no event name, no payload and no
delivery mechanism, and a Player that exposes nothing passes every row above.
One boundary is normative rather than a matter of interface shape: an error
overlay is a visible artefact, so a harness that observes one on any row records
a failure unless the application opted in.

### O.7 Backward compatibility

One case per construct §5 introduces, modelled on the legacy-Player scenario of
Annex G. Every construct of this specification is foreign-namespace open content
under DASH §5.2.1, which obliges the author to produce a document that survives
their removal: *"the MPD shall be authored such that, after XML attributes or
elements in the other namespaces than the DASH namespace are removed, the result
is a valid XML document formatted according to that schema and that conforms to
this document"* (DASH §5.2.1).

Setup, shared by every case: a representative document carrying the construct,
and a Player that predates this specification. Action: play the content.

Pass criteria, shared by every case: the construct is skipped silently; no error
is logged at a fatal level; no beacon fires for it; observable playback
continues.

What the viewer sees **around** the skipped construct is content-dependent and
is the Publisher's authoring choice rather than a property of the construct. On
live content the primary content continues, the opportunity being an expected
loss on such a Player. On on-demand content the Publisher may have authored a
standard linear break alongside it using only baseline constructs, which that
Player plays. The standard break is a baseline construct and is outside the
scope of the per-construct test; each case below asserts the silent skip alone.

| ID | Construct under test | What the document carries | Pass criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
