| T-88 | `<svta:OverlayPresentation>` and its event scheme | An `<EventStream>` of `urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-overlay:2026` with the element inside its `<Event>`. | The scheme is unrecognised and the stream is skipped; removing the foreign-namespace content leaves a valid document; playback continues. |
| T-89 | `<svta:PauseAdPresentation>` and its event scheme | An `<EventStream>` of `urn:svta:dash:event:sgai-pause-trigger:2026` with the element inside its `<Event>`. | As T-88; a viewer pause inside the window produces no request and no ad. |
| T-90 | `<svta:OverlayList>` | An Overlay Resolution Document. | The document is never fetched, the window that names it having been skipped; where it is fetched directly, its zero-duration `<Period>` holds no baseline content and the Player renders nothing. |
| T-91 | `<svta:Candidate>` | The same document with two candidates. | The candidates are skipped with their parent; no creative is fetched. |
| T-92 | `<svta:RenderableAsset>`, its `@layout` tokens and its `<ImportedMPD>` child | A candidate with three options, one of them `video` with an `<ImportedMPD>`. | All three are skipped with their parent; the sub-MPD is not fetched. The `<ImportedMPD>` is a baseline element nested inside an SGAI element and carries no legacy guarantee of its own — it is removed with the parent, which is the authoring lever this placement exists for. |
| T-93 | `<svta:BackgroundElement>` | A `squeezeback-double-box-with-background` option carrying it. | The background image is not fetched and no composition is attempted. |
| T-94 | `<svta:Click>` and `<svta:ClickTracking>` | A candidate carrying both. | No click surface is presented and no click-tracking URL is requested. |
| T-95 | `<svta:AdSystem>`, `<svta:AdTitle>`, `<svta:Advertiser>`, `<svta:UniversalAdId>` | A candidate carrying all four. | All four are skipped; their absence changes nothing in what the Player renders, so the same run on a Player that implements this specification and ignores them produces the same output, per T-52. |
| T-96 | The callback `<EventStream>` nested inside `<svta:Candidate>` | A candidate carrying its tracking schedule. | The whole subtree is removed with its SGAI parent and no beacon is requested. This is the same nesting lever as T-92: the carrier is authored for Players that implement this specification. |
| T-97 | The non-linear profile URI in `MPD@profiles` | An Overlay Resolution Document whose `@profiles` carries `urn:svta:dash:profile:sgai-overlay-list:2026`. | The unrecognised profile identifier leaves the document's processing unchanged for a Player reached by it, profiles restricting MPD and Segments rather than client behaviour (DASH §8.1, NOTE 1). |
| T-98 | The request-type URN in `@includeInRequests` | A `<RequestParam>` whose `@includeInRequests` names `urn:svta:dash:request:sgai-resolution:2026`. | The unknown URI is dropped before processing, the base specification requiring that *"The client shall drop unknown URIs from the @includeInRequests and @includeInHeaders strings prior to processing them"* (DASH Table I.4); the remaining request types process normally. |
| T-99 | The aggregate | A main MPD carrying every construct above at once. | Removing all attributes and elements outside the DASH namespace yields a document that validates against the DASH schema and plays; the run produces no fatal log entry and no beacon. |

### O.8 What is not tested here

- **The ADS's decisioning.** Which ads it picks, how many, in what order, and on
  what inputs. §O.2.2 observes only that nothing in this specification
  constrains it and that its output reaches the Player through the APS.
- **The APS-to-ADS exchange.** Its request, its response format and its latency
  budget are bilateral and outside this specification; a failure there reaches
  the Player as E-01, E-02 or E-04 and is tested only in that shape.
- **What this edition leaves open.** The retry policy for a resolution request
  (E-01); the deadline after which a pending response is abandoned (E-05); the
  status of the absent-cap rule, which is provisional and open with the working
  group (E-06, T-25); the verdict on a renderable creative carrier outside the
  admissible set (E-09); and the shape of any interface through which a Player
  surfaces these conditions to its application.
- **A Player that does not implement this specification**, beyond the
  silent-skip invariant of §O.7. Nothing here establishes how such a Player
  behaves in any other respect, and the base specification specifies no client
  behaviour normatively against which it could.


## Refinement gaps

The following items from the v9 analyses could not be resolved within a minor
refinement (they require requirement-level changes, a `context/` edit, or an
architectural decision). They are carried forward for the next major build to
address.

| # | Source | Issue ID | Summary | Why minor refinement is insufficient |
|---|--------|----------|---------|--------------------------------------|
| 1 | validation | `F1` (`A-1`) | §4.2, §4.3 and §4.4 carry no RFC 2119 modal; whether an ISO-style conformance chapeau binds in the indicative is never stated. | Deciding it changes the force of roughly forty statements at once and, for a SHOULD-sourced criterion such as R6.2, the modal itself. That is a drafting-convention decision, not a wording edit. |
| 2 | validation | `F2` (`DP-1.1#p2`) | `EventStream@value` and `MPD@type` are declared required while their only admissible value is fixed elsewhere or equals their own default. | Dropping either may produce a document a base validator rejects; keeping both needs a stated exemption from DP-1.1. Both are requirement-level. |
| 3 | validation | `F3` (`R15.3`) | `context/` grants the Player a MAY to skip a candidate whose carrier is inadmissible; §4.5.2 makes the skip unconditional. | Restoring the MAY weakens a normative obligation; keeping the MUST requires changing R15.3 in `context/`. |
| 4 | validation | `F4` (`R5.4`) | The criterion releases both the ADS and the APS from holding a device-capability view; §4.3 states it for the ADS only. | Extending the release to the APS is a new actor-level statement. |
| 5 | validation | `F5` (`DP-1#p1`, `DP-1.2#p1`) | The background fact is carried by both the layout token and the element's presence; a candidate's `@duration` duplicates the sub-MPD's `Period@duration`. | Naming a canonical side, or collapsing the two double-box tokens, changes the markup contract. |
| 6 | validation | `F6` (`R8.1`) | The two event scheme URIs and the resolution-document profile URI have no entry in §4.8's register, and `@form` has no justification anywhere. | Whether a URI counts as a "construct" for R8.1 is an open question the spec answers differently in §4.7 and §4.8. |
| 7 | validation | `F7` (`R11.3`) | *"It emits a decision document, typically VAST."* (§3 Terms) is the one in-place VAST mention outside an annex. | Whether the document-wide illustrative flag satisfies R11.3 is a `context/` reading. |
| 8 | validation | `F8` (`R3.2`) | §5.3.7.3 claims every layout-and-form pair has a row and has none for `linear` with an `image` or an `html` creative. | Adding the rows means stating decoder and surface budgets no analysis supplies; narrowing the claim changes what the table promises. Routed §5.b. |
| 9 | validation | `F10` (`R6.3`) | The spec mints no tracking scheme but never states the condition under which a new tracking carrier would be admissible. | Adding the gate is a new normative statement; whether it belongs in the spec or in governance is undecided. |
| 10 | validation | `F11` (`EC-1`) | §4.5.2 defines outcomes for a candidate that satisfies all three eligibility checks and for one that satisfies none, leaving the mixed case unstated. | The obvious rule (any failure skips) may be wrong for the cap check, which §4.5.4 governs with a MAY. Requirement-level. |
| 11 | validation | `F12` (`G-1`) | §4.6 obliges validation against a schema for `urn:svta:dash:sgai:2026` that this specification does not publish. | Publishing a schema is a commitment in versioning and hosting; dropping step 2 gives back the outcome §4.6 exists to prevent. |
| 12 | validation | `F13` (`G-3`) | Where a non-linear slot's presentation timeline starts is never fixed, so beacon times have no referent for the first candidate. | The window's `@presentationTime` and first render differ by the resolution latency; choosing between them fixes a requirement. |
| 13 | validation | `F14` (`G-2`) | §4.5.2 and §4.5.3 validate every candidate against the window's `@allowedLayouts`, which a linear window has no attribute to carry. | Stating the check is vacuous on the linear family, or adding the attribute, both change requirements. |
| 14 | validation | `D1`..`D9` (§5.c) | Nine findings the validation routed to `context/`: UC-05/UC-08 on D5 (`D1`), R1.3 against R4.8 (`D2`), `@maxDuration` on a pause window (`D3`, `G-5`), R32.4's scope declaration (`D4`, `G-4`), R1.1 / R6.4 / R6#p2 scoping (`D5`), R26.2 / R27.2 compositing (`D6`), the time-shift buffer in R25 (`D7`, `EC-3`), R15.2's declared forms (`D8`), and the stale prose of R20.4 and DR-10 (`D9`). | Each is an edit inside `context/`, which a minor refinement must not touch. |
| 15 | conformance audit | `M4` | `<svta:RenderableAsset>` has no declared content model, so §5.3.1's child table decides nothing about order. | The annex listings were aligned to the table's order, which removes the inconsistency; declaring whether order is significant is an architectural choice and is not made here. |
| 16 | conformance audit | `O1` | §5.1.3's justification for the whitespace-separated `@allowedLayouts` rests on `StringVectorType`, whose definition is not in the primary copy — Annex B is by reference. | Resolving it means fetching and citing `DASH-MPD.xsd` as a second normative source, which is an external grounding a minor refinement does not perform. |
| 17 | conformance audit | `O2` | Table I.4's `mpdlink` request type covers every `<ImportedMPD>` resolution this specification performs and is named nowhere. | Naming it adds a request-type token to §5.8.1's value space, which is a new construct in the Publisher's contract. |
